## 7. Risks and open decisions

| # | Decision or risk | Recommended default |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Directory or archive | Directory; document `tar` for transport. |
| 2 | Exit code for an unsigned intact bundle | 0, as the acceptance text says "verifies", with the caveat always printed. The stricter option is 2. |
| 3 | Signature present, no key given | Exit 2 `signature_unchecked`; reporting 0 would let a signed bundle pass unchecked. |
| 4 | Export while the task is live | Allow; `at_rest: false` and a warning. |
| 5 | Default artefacts | Export, receipts, both attempt logs, provenance, findings, diffs, matching flights. Opt-in: traces, verify receipts. Out: `audit.log`, CERI. |
| 6 | Redaction level | `standard` in PR 2; `strict` in PR 4. Docs must say `standard` still contains prompts, code and paths. |
| 7 | Re-verify embedded receipts on `audit verify` | No: integrity only. Re-running verifiers needs the repository and is `nexus verify`'s job. |
| 8 | Passphrase-protected keys | Not in v1.3. |
| 9 | Flight matching by time window can pick up another task's flight in the same workspace | Label the selection; `--flight` overrides; say so in the docs. |
| 10 | A config attempt is linked before state redaction (`cli/src/security.ts:704` versus `cli/src/core/state.ts:596`). If redaction ever changes an attempt's `path`, its chain reads as edited | I could not determine whether this happens. Add a characterisation test in PR 2 and report the result to the GV-3 owner. |
| 11 | `audit verify` is a "client" invocation (`cli/src/core/client_lease.ts:10-17`): it takes the lease, loads configuration, and a broken operator config stops it (`cli/src/index.ts:1488-1491`) | Accept for v1.3 and document it. Exempting it means editing `client_lease.ts`. |
| 12 | Task-id format, and whether one id can exist in both stores | Not determined; hence `--store` and an explicit ambiguity error. |
| 13 | AGENTS.md requires a XERJ reference query before non-trivial code | Not done here (read-only, no network). The PR 1 author records it. |

## Where code and plan disagree, and what I added
- **`hash_chain.ts` has changed since the plan**: `verifyChain` now reports a malformed chain link as `broken`/`unlinked`. I use it as it is; a file entry or log record with a malformed link becomes `manifest_chain_broken` / `log_chain_broken`.
- **Four finding kinds beyond the plan's list**: `manifest_missing`, `manifest_sha256_mismatch` (for `expectManifestSha256`), `limit_exceeded` (every bound) and `unreadable_entry` (I/O failure, never a pass).
- **`files_head` is `{seq, hash} | null`**, null for a bundle with no files; the plan did not cover the empty case.
- **`workspace_sha256` is not defined by the plan.** I typed it `string | null` and documented it as the SHA-256 of the workspace path. Slice 2 must pin the exact definition.
- **Path rules beyond the plan**: `manifest.json` and `manifest.sig.json` are reserved (case-insensitive), a path cannot be both file and directory, and a directory cannot be spelled in two cases.
- **Chain facts are computed by the writer**: a chained log's `records`, `status` and `head` come from its bytes via `verifyChain`; the caller supplies only `path`, `kind`, `closed`, `source`, `selection`.
- **Limits can be tightened, never raised**: `verifyAuditBundle` takes `limits`, and a value above the default is ignored. The tests use this to exercise bounds without 256 MiB files.
- **Unknown options are refused**: passing `publicKey` returns a `usage` error document instead of a silent intact result.
- **Missing bundle directory** is an error document with exit `error` (1). The plan does not say; slice 2 may prefer `usage` (2).

## Manifest members and hash definitions
Members: `schema_version` (1), `format` (`"nexus-audit-bundle/1"`), `bundle_id`, `created_at`, `nexus {version, commit, dirty}`, `subject {kind, task_id, session_id, store, workspace_path, workspace_sha256, status, updated_at, at_rest}`, `redaction`, `files[] {path, bytes, sha256, role, chain {seq, prev, hash}}`, `files_head`, `chained_logs[] {path, kind, records, status, head?, closed?, source?, selection?}`, `omitted[] {artefact, reason}`, `root_sha256`.

- **File `sha256`**: SHA-256 of the file's raw bytes, lowercase hex.
- **File entry link**: `linkRecord({path, bytes, sha256, role}, previous)`, so `hash` is SHA-256 of `` `${seq}\n${prev}\n${canonicalJson(entry without chain)}` ``, with `prev` = `CHAIN_GENESIS` for the first. Entries are sorted by path in code-unit order.
- **`files_head`**: `{seq, hash}` of the last entry's link.
- **`root_sha256`**: SHA-256 of `canonicalJson(manifest without root_sha256)` as UTF-8.
- **`manifest.json` on disk**: exactly `canonicalJson(manifest) + "\n"`.
- **`manifest_sha256`**: SHA-256 of those bytes.
- **Chained log `head`**: `{seq, hash}` of the last record's link as `verifyChain` reads the bundled bytes (one JSON object per line, blank lines skipped, an unparseable line counted as `null`).

## Finding kinds and exit codes
Produced now: `file_altered` (with expected and found hash and size), `file_missing`, `file_unexpected`, `unsafe_entry`, `unreadable_entry`, `manifest_missing`, `manifest_malformed`, `manifest_not_canonical`, `manifest_root_mismatch`, `manifest_chain_broken`, `manifest_sha256_mismatch`, `log_head_mismatch`, `log_chain_broken`, `limit_exceeded`, `unsupported_version`. Declared but not produced until slice 3: `signature_invalid`, `signature_key_mismatch`, `signature_missing`.

| Code | Names |
|---|---|
| 0 | `exported`, `intact_signed`, `intact_unsigned` |
| 1 | `tampered`, `error`, `option_parse` |
| 2 | `signature_unchecked`, `unsupported_version`, `usage`, `subject_not_found` |
| 130 | `interrupted` |

Result precedence: `unsupported_version`, then `tampered` (any finding), then `signature_unchecked` (`manifest.sig.json` exists), then `intact_unsigned`. `intact_signed` is never returned in this slice.

## Bounds (`AUDIT_LIMITS`)
| Constant | Value |
|---|---|
| `manifestBytes` | 4 MiB |
| `files` | 10,000 |
| `fileBytes` | 256 MiB |
| `totalBytes` | 2 GiB |
| `logRecords` | 1,000,000 |
| `logLineBytes` | 4 MiB |
| `jsonDepth` | 64 (counted on the text before `JSON.parse` and `canonicalJson`) |
| `pathDepth` | 4 |
| `pathSegmentChars` | 160 |
| `findings` | 100 |

The tree walk also stops after `files × pathDepth + 2` entries.

## Sample outputs
As written (exit 0, `intact_unsigned`):
```
INTACT BY HASH ONLY: all 3 files match the manifest. This bundle is NOT SIGNED: anyone able to write it could have replaced its files and the manifest together. To rule that out, compare the manifest hash 647fda43f5be786d5c4262c8af0d6b8ea708a75fafa53784498c4c53d30a6174 with one recorded at export.
chained log flights/f1.flightlog: intact, 2 records, as the manifest recorded
```
One byte of `receipts/last-turn.json` altered (exit 1, `tampered`):
```
NOT INTACT: 1 problem found. This bundle is not the one its manifest describes, or it could not be fully checked; do not rely on it.
  file_altered receipts/last-turn.json: the file is 23 bytes with SHA-256 aebdf846db191254fb5eef05c98277b5cae046e0944b6eaf36cd99f704996b67; the manifest records 23 bytes with SHA-256 24da8ce9e6486acfc2e93b977bb4605f2b647dd07bd58dd44e2a38411201e048
manifest sha256: 647fda43f5be786d5c4262c8af0d6b8ea708a75fafa53784498c4c53d30a6174
chained log flights/f1.flightlog: intact, 2 records, as the manifest recorded
```

| (c) stop at first finding | 8 | "every problem is reported", the cap test |
| (d) drop the unsigned caveat | 3 | round trip, honest boundary |
| (e) skip recorded-head comparison | 2 | the truncated-tail test |

For (a) I had to remove both layers, the path-rule gate and the tree enumeration. With only one removed, the other still blocks the open.

## Reference coding
I ran `xerj code opencode "manifest sha256 bundle verify tamper signature detached ed25519" -k 4 --full 400`. The index is flagged not verified complete and returned four weak matches (`dev-electron.ts:5`, `vite.pwa.ts:16`, `opencode-pty.ts:6`, a Figma `.d.ts`), none about bundle verification. Nothing taken, borrowed or copied. The design follows the in-repo patterns (`hash_chain.ts`, `verify_contract.ts`, `result_schema.mjs`). No field report was sent, because you allowed only the one local query.

## Not done or not tested
- **No fsync in the writer.** I removed per-file fsync because it took the test file from 3 s to 75 s on this host. After a power loss a renamed bundle could hold short files; verification would report them as `file_altered`. Say if you want it back, or a single directory fsync.
- **Intermediate-directory race.** `O_NOFOLLOW` protects only the last path component. Someone swapping a directory for a symlink between the listing and the open, while verification runs, is not prevented.
- **Rename over an empty directory.** If an empty directory appears at `out` between the existence check and the rename, POSIX rename replaces it.
- **`manifest.sig.json` content is not read or validated**; only its presence is noticed. A garbage envelope yields `signature_unchecked`.
- **The outermost catch in `verifyAuditBundle` has no test.** Every failure I could inject is caught earlier as a finding.
- **The default 256 MiB, 2 GiB and 1,000,000-record bounds were not exercised at full size**, only through tightened limits. The 4 MiB manifest bound was tested at its real size.
- **Process skips.** I did not write `tasks/todo.md` or run `graphify update`, because both would create files outside the allowed list.

- The four added finding kinds, and the reused `manifest_malformed` for path-rule violations.
- The `tampered` headline wording ("NOT INTACT: …"); the plan gives no text for it.

- The spy in `audit_fixture.ts`, which wraps the `node:fs` default export. It only sees the library because the library calls `fs.&lt;fn&gt;` on that object at call time.</result>

## What changed
- **`durable?: boolean`, default true, on `writeAuditBundle`.** When true it fsyncs each payload file, then `manifest.json`, then each directory of the partial tree and the partial directory itself, renames into place, then fsyncs the output's parent. When false it issues no fsync. The module comment documents it in one sentence.
- **Reuse from `durable_file.ts`.** Directory fsyncs use the imported `fsyncDirectoryDurably`; that file is not edited. `writeFileDurably` and `withDurableSync` did not fit: the first writes via temp-and-rename, takes a string and is not an exclusive create, and the second only affects that module's own writers. File fsync is therefore one `fsyncSync` on the descriptor already open.
- **No replace at rename.** Node has no no-replace rename, so the writer creates the final name exclusively just before the rename. Anything already there, including an empty directory that appeared after the first check, fails with `EEXIST` and is left as it was. If the rename itself then fails, the placeholder is removed only if still empty.
- **Race documented.** The module comment now says verifying a bundle that someone is modifying concurrently is out of scope, because `O_NOFOLLOW` guards only the last path component.
- **Spy extended** in `cli/test/helpers/audit_fixture.ts`: it returns ordered events, maps each `fsyncSync` to the path its descriptor was opened with, and re-syncs the named exports of `node:fs` so calls made inside `durable_file.ts` are seen.
- **Two new tests**; all other writes in the tests pass `durable: false`.
  - The default-durable test asserts an fsync for each of the 10 payload files, the manifest, the 6 subdirectories and the partial directory, all before the rename. It also checks the manifest's fsync comes after every payload's, the parent's fsync comes after the rename, the total is exactly 19, and `durable: false` gives 0.
  - The destination-appears test creates an empty directory at the output mid-write and asserts `EEXIST` with that directory untouched.

<summary>Agent "VR-1 slice 3 action hostile sandbox" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Slice 3 is built and uncommitted in `/home/mike/nexus-wt/vr1c`. It is not safe as a gate on untrusted changes without `--sandbox`: a hostile head can still get itself reported `VERIFIED`.

## Findings

1. **Not fixed, documented limit: without `--sandbox` a hostile head raises its own verdict.** A test the head adds rewrites the scratch `head.index` and `objects` beside its tree. The "pristine" copy is then written with a fixed product and the base's test file, and a change that fixes nothing is reported `VERIFIED`. The exploit is a test that asserts exactly that. With `--sandbox bwrap` the same head ends not `VERIFIED` (tested). No cheap fix exists inside `verify.ts`, because the process runs as the caller.
2. **Fixed: Git content filters from `.git/config` ran before any approval.** `add -A` (clean) and `checkout-index` (smudge) executed them when the tree's `.gitattributes` selected them; hooks and fsmonitor were already pinned off. `verify.ts` now overrides every `filter.*` key defined at local or worktree scope with the operator-level value, or nothing. Operator-level filters such as Git LFS still run (documented limit, tested).
3. **Fixed: a tracked `node_modules/.bin/node` shim ran on both sides.** The caller's checkout at the head had it, `verify` linked it into the base, and the fake pass came out `REGRESSION_FREE`. A dependency directory either tree holds is no longer linked, so the head is not run (`VERIFIER_DEFINITION_CHANGED`).
4. **Fixed: a `GNUmakefile` shadowing the base's `Makefile`.** Once the operator approved the edit, the fake target ran and was reported `REGRESSION_FREE`. `verify.ts` now treats a changed `GNUmakefile` or `makefile` as a definition change for a `make` verifier. The underlying gap is in `independent_check.ts`, which binds `Makefile` only; that file is claimed, so it is untouched.
5. **Hardened: a path that is not valid UTF-8.** It was lowered only by accident (`INTERNAL_ERROR` from an `ENOENT`), while the guard would have classed a weakened test with such a name as a new file. It is now `BLOCKED` (`UNSUPPORTED_TREE`).
6. **Documented limits, pinned by tests:**
   - An approved `conftest.py` that fakes every test gives `REGRESSION_FREE`, never `VERIFIED`.
   - Product code that reads the expected value out of the test file is `VERIFIED`.
   - A tree nested deeper than five levels is at most `REGRESSION_FREE`.
7. **Your brief's claim "nothing from the repository is interpolated into a shell command" is not literally true.** `tester.ts` puts a project directory into the type-check line (`cd '&lt;dir&gt;' &amp;&amp; tsc …`), single-quoted. A directory named `$(touch …) 'q' "d"` executed nothing (tested).
8. **Existing `action/action.yml` has no injection issue.** No `${{ }}` in a run body; inputs arrive through `env`. `examples/github-actions/nexus.yml` pins `actions/checkout@v4` by tag while the repository's workflows pin by SHA; I did not edit it.
9. **For other owners:**
   - `baseEnv()` in `worktrunk/git.ts` caches the whole environment keyed only by `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, so a later in-process call does not see a changed `HOME` or `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`.
   - The `runScript` helper in `examples.test.ts` kills its child after 120 s (see the test table).

## How the sandbox is applied

No claimed file is edited. `sandboxedVerifierCommand` in `verify.ts` calls `wrapSandboxedCommand` and passes the resulting quoted line to `runVerifierShell`; the type-check executor goes through the same function, so `typecheck_evidence.ts` is untouched.

- **Bubblewrap:** `customBwrapArgs` add a tmpfs over the scratch directory, re-bind only the run's own tree, bind each linked dependency directory read-only, then clear the environment and set the verifier's allowlist.
- **Docker:** the wrapper's argv is rewritten the way `wrapDockerWorker` does it: its `--env` entries are replaced, `:ro` mounts and a container name are added.
- **Unusable engine:** `BLOCKED` with the new reason `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` (exit 3), nothing run.
- **CLI:** `--sandbox [engine]` takes `auto`, `bwrap`, `docker` or `none`, plus `--sandbox-network`.

Two deliberate differences from `run`: `NEXUS_SANDBOX_NETWORK` is not consulted (only the flag turns the network on), and `--sandbox-network` without `--sandbox` is a usage error rather than a warning.

| | Without `--sandbox` | With `--sandbox` |
|---|---|---|
| Other scratch trees, pristine copy, scratch index and objects | writable | not visible |
| Caller's checkout | writable | read-only, or not visible under HOME or `/tmp` |
| Linked dependency directories | writable | read-only |
| HOME | readable and writable | masked |
| Network | the caller's | none unless `--sandbox-network` |
| Processes outliving the run | possible | no |

## Attack table

Test files are under `cli/test/`; the full table with test names is in `docs/VERIFY_IN_CI.md`.

| Attack | Outcome | Test file |
|---|---|---|
| Rewritten `scripts.test`; verifier only in head | lowered | `verify_command` (existing) |
| `pretest`/`posttest` hooks; rewritten `Makefile` target; `GNUmakefile`; `.npmrc` `script-shell` | lowered, never run | `verify_hostile` |
| `conftest.py` | `BLOCKED`; approved: limit (finding 6) | `verify_hostile` |
| Head's policy file; `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE` | prevented | `verify_typecheck_guard` (existing) |
| Head deletes the base's stricter policy | lowered | `verify_hostile` |
| `node_modules/.bin/node` shim | lowered, never run | `verify_hostile` |
| Fake `node` in the tree, `bin` entry, `preinstall` | prevented | `verify_hostile` |
| Product reads expected value from the test file | limit | `verify_hostile` |
| Test rewrites tests, product or itself during the run | prevented | `verify_command`, `verify_typecheck_guard`, `verify_hostile` |
| Test rewrites scratch state | limit without sandbox; prevented with | `verify_hostile` |
| Test writes caller's checkout or dependency directory | limit without sandbox; denied with | `verify_sandbox` |
| Five environment variables | recorded by name, none raises the known-bad change | `verify_hostile`, `verify_command` |
| Head an ancestor of base; base chosen after the weakening | lowered | `verify_hostile` |
| Option-like refs and refs with shell text | prevented (usage error) | `verify_hostile` |
| Worktree path or tree directory with spaces, quotes, newline, `$(…)` | prevented | `verify_hostile`, `verify_sandbox` |
| `--verifier` with metacharacters | by design; recorded verbatim, source `configured` | `verify_hostile` |
| Test file replaced by an outside symlink; links to sensitive paths | `GUARD_INCOMPLETE` | `verify_hostile` |
| Submodule; planted repository (`.git` directory or file) | `BLOCKED` | `verify_command`, `verify_hostile` |
| `.gitattributes` filters and diff drivers, fsmonitor, hooks | prevented (finding 2) | `verify_hostile` |
| Committed tree over the file limit | `TREE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` | `verify_hostile` |
| Path longer than the file system allows | `INTERNAL_ERROR`, exit 1 | `verify_hostile` |
| 60-level directory chain | at most `REGRESSION_FREE` | `verify_hostile` |
| Control characters in names; invalid UTF-8 names | escaped; `UNSUPPORTED_TREE` | `verify_hostile` |
| Forged verdict, JSON and ANSI in output | prevented | `verify_hostile` |
| Hostile action inputs and event fields | prevented | `verify_action` |

### `.nexus-security.json`

- **Loader:** `SecurityManager.loadPolicy`, `cli/src/security.ts:354-388`. It uses `existsSync` + `readFileSync` + `JSON.parse`, follows symlinks, and has no size bound.
- **Fail-open, confirmed:** the bare `catch {}` at `security.ts:376-378` falls through to the defaults at `381-387` with no message. A malformed file silently drops all of the project's own tightenings (`blockedCommands`, `protectedPaths`, `allowedDomains`, `verificationPaths.extra`, mode `block`). It does not go below the built-in defaults.
- **Soft failures:** an invalid `mode` silently becomes `approve` (`246-250`); non-arrays are ignored (`366`, `370`, `372`).
- **Keys** (`SecurityPolicy`, `security.ts:135-143`):
  - `blockedCommands`: substring match, appended to defaults (`364-367`, used at `400-410`).
  - `protectedPaths`: appended (`368-371`, used at `581`).
  - `allowedDomains`: an allowlist enforced at `cli/src/tools.ts:1540-1556`.
  - `verificationPaths.{mode, extra, allow}` (`14-18`; modes at `180`).
  - `egressInspector`: never taken from the file (`360-362`, `374`); it comes from `NEXUS_EGRESS_POLICY` (`cli/src/core/egress_policy.ts:33-41`).
- **"Untrusted can only tighten":** `parseVerificationPolicy(raw, trusted)`, `security.ts:238-254`. An untrusted workspace may set only `approve` or `block`, `allow` is dropped, and `extra` is always accepted. A trusted one may set `off`, `record` and `allow`.
- **The policy file protects itself:** class `policy` (`175`) stays at least `approve` (`853`), and full access does not auto-allow it (`863`).
- **Decision and record:** `verificationEditDecision` (`831-865`), `authorizeVerificationEdit` (`868`), and `recordVerificationAttempt` (hash-chained, `697-706`).
- **Constructors (all inherit whatever `loadPolicy` does):** `agent.ts:474`, `tools.ts:754`, `team.ts:82`, `core/specialist_inputs.ts:375,534`, `core/verify.ts:169`.
- **`nexus verify`** strips `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE` and reads the base tree's file as untrusted (`core/verify.ts:159-172`).
- `reload()` (`security.ts:350`) has no caller in `cli/src`.

### Workspace trust

- The store is a list of real paths in `~/.config/nexus/trusted_workspaces.json` (`cli/src/workspace_trust.ts:28-30, 45-51`). **There is no content hash anywhere.**
- `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE=1` grants trust (`workspace_trust.ts:38`); read-only forces untrusted (`:37`).
- `nexus trust` is at `cli/src/index.ts:1084-1103`; the startup prompt is `promptWorkspaceTrust` (`index.ts:252, 307, 316`; `cli/src/startup_trust.ts`).

### CS-9 command rules

- **Module:** `cli/src/core/command_rules.ts`. Order is `allow:0, prompt:1, forbidden:2` (`:33`); the strictest rule wins (`130-144`); `allow` needs every segment allowed and no shell-active character (`145-148`).
- **Parsing is strict** (unknown field rejected at `155, 161`; examples self-checked at `175-187`).
- **But loading fails open:** `agent.ts:1546-1555` falls back to `NO_COMMAND_RULES` plus a one-time notice (`dispatch_tool_call.ts:391-398`).
- **Decision point:** `cli/src/core/dispatch_tool_call.ts:399-455`. `forbidden` refuses in every mode (`415-441`, `denialReason: "COMMAND_RULE_FORBIDDEN"`).
- **`prompt` is skipped under full access** (`:448`). This matters for GV-1: a system "require a human" rule must not be skippable by `--yes`.
- **Deciding rule recorded:** `CommandRuleDecision` (`command_rules.ts:63-76`) is pushed at `dispatch_tool_call.ts:400-414` (capped at 500), persisted (`core/state.ts:209, 838`; `session.ts:182`), and exported as `taskState.commandRuleDecisions` (`session_export.ts:95`, markdown at `173-175`).
- Doc: `docs/COMMAND_RULES.md`.

### Approval modes

- **Launch precedence:** `buildConfig`, `cli/src/config.ts:440-468`. Order: read-only &gt; `--yes` (auto plus `fullAccess`) &gt; `--approve` &gt; `--mode` &gt; `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE` &gt; review. `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE=auto` also sets `fullAccess` (`461-463`).
- **Flags:** `index.ts:371-374, 442-444, 476-479`.
- **Runtime changes all go through `NexusAgent.setApprovalMode`** (`agent.ts:745-760`). Callers:
  - `repl.ts:150-158, 201-203, 1037-1048`;
  - the "always" answer at `repl.ts:626-629`;
  - `sidecar.ts:145-146, 887-891, 1017-1020`;
  - `slash/session_commands.ts:175-182`.
- **The REPL auto-approves when its own mode is auto** (`repl.ts:534`), so a clamp only inside the approval policy is not enough.
- **Decision function:** `ApprovalPolicy.requiresDecision` (`cli/src/core/approval_policy.ts:29-88`); full access returns false first (`33-41`).
- **`PolicyContext` is built at three sites:** `dispatch_tool_call.ts:314-322`, `core/spine_handlers.ts:194-200`, `core/completion_oracle.ts:22-28`.
- **Headless denial:** `DECISION_REQUIRED_NO_OPERATOR` (`dispatch_tool_call.ts:502-543`). Its text tells the user to "Run with --yes or --mode auto" (`505-507`), which is wrong under strict mode.
- **Shown and recorded:** console lines at `dispatch_tool_call.ts:546-552`; the `ApprovalReview` payload (`cli/src/approval_review.ts:12-17`) in `approval.request` (`cli/src/protocol.ts:199`); `receipt.denialReason` (`types.ts:154`); `flightRecorder.recordToolResult`. None of these carries a policy source today.
- **Other `--yes` meanings:** `verify --yes` approves a discovered verifier (`index.ts:682, 720`), which is an approval bypass. `doctor --fix --yes` (`:861`) and `upgrade --yes` (`:1446`) are not.

### MCP

- **Config files:** `mcpConfigFiles`, `cli/src/mcp.ts:559-569`. Workspace `.mcp.json` / `mcp.json` load only when trusted and not read-only; then `~/.config/nexus/mcp.json`, `~/.nexus/mcp.json`, and `~/.config/opencode/mcp.json`.
- **Choke point exists:** every connection enters `McpClientManager.connections` at `mcp.ts:606` (`loadAndConnect`), `:621` (`addConnection`) or `:654` (`addServer`, called from `repl.ts:1437` and `slash/extension_commands.ts:117`).
- **The server is spawned at `connect()`** (`mcp.ts:603-604, 642-643`), so the gate must sit before construction.
- Invalid config is silently ignored (`mcp.ts:612-614`).
- Managers are built at `agent.ts:477, 789` and `index.ts:1027`.

### Plugins

- **Directories:** `pluginDirs`, `cli/src/plugins.ts:44-51`.
- **Choke point:** `loadPluginFile`, `plugins.ts:96-117`.
- The allowlist must key on the file (path or SHA-256) before `import()`, not on the plugin name.
- Load errors are swallowed (`:90, :114`).

### Sandbox and network

- Flags: `index.ts:390-391`; `config.ts:470-475`.
- Network: `sandboxNetworkEnabled`, `cli/src/sandbox.ts:189-192` (flag or operator `NEXUS_SANDBOX_NETWORK`).
- The bridge has `requireSandbox` (`tools.ts:721, 1013, 1669, 1747`), passed at `agent.ts:468-472`.

### Doctor

- `config.valid` (`cli/src/doctor.ts:379-394`) already reports a deferred operator-config error (`config.ts:104-113, 413`; `index.ts:128, 806`). This is the pattern to copy.
- Extension findings: `doctor.ts:1176-1240`. Posture line: `1357-1365, 1606-1607`.
- There is no policy finding.

### Already called "policy"

- `VerifyGuard.policySource: "base_tree" | "default"` (`cli/src/verify_contract.ts:221`; set at `core/verify.ts:225, 237, 756`).
- `operationPolicy` in the operator's `config.json` (`config.ts:572-611, 683-712`).
- `NEXUS_EGRESS_POLICY`.
- `docs/security/` holds one remediation note.
- No `.nexus/` policy file exists.

### Reusable hardened-reader precedents

- `readOperatorConfigDocument` (`config.ts:633-681`): `lstat`, no symlink, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat` re-check, 64 KiB bound, version check, unknown key rejected.
- Root-owner check: `sandbox.ts:469` (`stat.uid !== 0 || (stat.mode &amp; 0o022)`).
- Non-blocking open: `core/checkout_trust.ts:32-39`.
- `writePrivateFile`: `core/durable_file.ts:120`.

## 2. Design

### Format (section 3 of your brief)

No TOML parser exists anywhere:
- `cli/package.json` runtime dependencies are `commander`, `dotenv`, `ora`, `picocolors`.
- `core/Cargo.toml` has `serde` and `serde_json` only.
- Node 22/24 has no TOML module.
- `security.ts:213-223` is a section-header regex, not a parser.

**Recommended default: a strict hand-written TOML subset, keeping D16's `.toml` paths.**

| | TOML subset | JSON |
|---|---|---|
| Matches D16 text | yes | needs a plan amendment (PLAN.md is claimed) |
| Comments for admins | yes | no |
| Duplicate keys | rejected by our parser | `JSON.parse` silently takes the last one, so `{"strict":true,"strict":false}` yields false |
| Parser risk | about 200 lines to own and test | none |
| User expectation | people will write full TOML and be rejected | none |

The duplicate-key problem means JSON would need a custom scanner too, so the parser cost is not avoided by choosing JSON. The user-expectation cost is mitigated by line-numbered errors and `nexus policy check &lt;file&gt;`.

**Grammar accepted:**
- UTF-8, LF or CRLF, no BOM, no control characters except tab.
- Blank lines; `#` comments (full-line or trailing).
- Root `key = value` lines before any table.
- `[table]` headers, single level.
- Keys and table names match `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*`.
- Values: a basic string `"…"` on one line with only the `\"` and `\\` escapes; `true` / `false`; a non-negative decimal integer; an array of basic strings (may span lines, trailing comma and comments allowed).

**Rejected, each with `line N`:**
- Literal or multi-line strings, any other escape.
- Floats, signs, hex, underscores, dates.
- Inline tables, dotted or quoted keys, `[[…]]`, nested or mixed arrays.
- Duplicate key, duplicate table.
- Anything after a value except a comment.
- Strings over 1024 characters, arrays over 500 items, files over 64 KiB.

**Shape and size:** output is `{root, tables}` with a line number per key; the parser is schema-agnostic and the validator rejects unknown tables and keys. About 180-220 lines.

**Parser tests:**
- About 25 accepted documents.
- About 60 rejected documents, asserting line and message.
- Render-then-parse round trip for generated documents.
- A mutation loop asserting only `PolicyParseError` is ever thrown.

### Schema

One normalized `PolicyDocument` type covers all tiers. The two managed tiers are TOML. The project tier stays `.nexus-security.json`, mapped by an adapter.

**Recommendation on restrict-only: encode it in both the types and the merge.** `PolicyDocument` has no field that can express a grant, and the merge is a lattice meet proven by a generated table.

| Key | Type | Merge | Enforces |
|---|---|---|---|
| `schema_version` | int, required in managed tiers | n/a | version rule in section 5 |
| `approval.strict` | bool | OR | shorthand for the next three |
| `approval.disable_auto` | bool | OR | mode `auto` from any source |
| `approval.disable_yes` | bool | OR | `-y/--yes`, including `verify --yes` |
| `approval.disable_full_access` | bool | OR | any `fullAccess` source |
| `approval.require_human` | array of `exec`, `write_workspace`, `egress` | union | always ask, even under full access |
| `verification.mode` | `approve` or `block` only | max | a floor; `off` and `record` are type errors |
| `verification.protected` | globs | union | extra protected paths |
| `commands.forbid`, `commands.prompt` | prefix strings such as `"git push"` | union | CS-9 rules; no `allow` key exists |
| `commands.disable_allow_rules` | bool | OR | drops the user's `allow` rules |
| `paths.protected` | patterns | union | |
| `egress.inspector` | `record` or `block` | max | floor over `NEXUS_EGRESS_POLICY` |
| `egress.allowed_domains` | allowlist | intersection | |
| `egress.secret_destinations` | allowlist | intersection | caps `allowDestinations` |
| `sandbox.required` | bool | OR | |
| `sandbox.forbid_network` | bool | OR | |
| `mcp.allow` | allowlist: `name`, `command:/abs/path`, `url:https://origin` | intersection | |
| `mcp.disable_workspace` | bool | OR | |
| `plugins.allow` | allowlist: `sha256:&lt;hex&gt;`, `file:/abs/path` | intersection | |
| `plugins.disable_workspace` | bool | OR | |
| `workspace.disable_env_trust` | bool | OR | ignores `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE` |

- **Allowlists:** absent means unrestricted; present (even empty) means only the listed items; absent is the identity for intersection.
- **Legacy project relaxations** (`mode` `off`/`record`, `verificationPaths.allow`) live in a separate `ProjectRelaxations` type. They apply only when the workspace is trusted, integrity is MATCH, and no managed tier sets `verification.mode`.
- So a trusted project may relax a built-in default only where no managed tier has spoken, exactly as today.

### Merge and attribution

- `mergePolicies(system?, user?, project?) → EffectivePolicy`, frozen.
- Because the merge is a meet, precedence cannot change the value; it decides attribution only.
- For a scalar, the deciding tier is the highest tier whose own value equals the effective value. Each list element carries the first tier that contributed it. Otherwise the source is `default`.
- `PolicySource = { tier: "system"|"user"|"project"|"operator-rules"|"default", file?, key, line? }`.
- One renderer, `describeSource`, produces for example `[policy: system /etc/nexus/policy.toml approval.strict]`.
- Every prompt and denial string appends it, including `[policy: default]`. That is how "the effective tier is printed beside every decision" is met.

### Modules

- `cli/src/core/managed_policy.ts`: pure types, validation, merge, clamp helpers, `describeSource`.
- `cli/src/core/managed_policy_toml.ts`: the parser.
- `cli/src/core/managed_policy_load.ts`: file-system reads with every input explicit — `loadManagedPolicy({ systemPath, systemOwnerUid, userPath, userUid, workspace, trust, fs })`.
- One composition function binds the constants and memoizes once per process.
- Until `types.ts` and `agent.ts` are free, consumers take an optional `policy` parameter that defaults to the memo (the same style as module state at `config.ts:33-34`). Afterwards it is threaded through `HarnessConfig.managedPolicy`.

### Fail-closed rules

Both managed tiers throw `ManagedPolicyError { tier, file, code, line? }`.

| Condition | System tier | User tier |
|---|---|---|
| File absent | tier absent | tier absent |
| `/etc/nexus` exists but is not root-owned, or is group/world-writable, or is a symlink | refuse | n/a |
| Symlink (`lstat` or `ELOOP`) | refuse | refuse (same as `config.ts:641`) |
| Not a regular file (opened with `O_NONBLOCK`) | refuse | refuse |
| `EACCES`, `EIO`, any other error | refuse | refuse |
| Over 64 KiB | refuse | refuse |
| Owner | must be uid 0 | must be euid or 0 |
| Mode has `0o022` bits | refuse | refuse |
| `fstat` dev/ino differs from `lstat` | refuse | refuse |
| Invalid UTF-8, BOM, NUL | refuse | refuse |
| Syntax error, duplicate, unknown table or key, wrong type, bad enum | refuse | refuse |
| Empty file or missing `schema_version` | refuse | refuse |
| `schema_version` above supported | refuse | refuse |

**Exit code 2**, matching existing configuration failures (`index.ts:54-60, 122-127`). On `nexus run` this shares code 2 with `unverified` and `usage` (`run_contract.ts:72-73`). Stderr is two lines:

```
nexus: managed policy error (system tier): /etc/nexus/policy.toml: line 7: unknown key 'approval.disable_yess'.
Nexus will not start without this policy. Fix the file (root-owned; ask your administrator) and run `nexus doctor`. No setting or environment variable bypasses it.
```

The user-tier variant ends: `Fix it, or move it aside: mv ~/.config/nexus/policy.toml ~/.config/nexus/policy.toml.bad`.

**Recovery path:**
- `--version`, `--help`, `nexus doctor` and `nexus policy check` always run. Doctor follows the deferred-error pattern at `config.ts:109-113`.
- The system tier has no override of any kind.
- `doctor --fix` never touches policy files.

### Project integrity

- **Store:** `~/.config/nexus/policy_trust.json`, written with `writePrivateFile`, keyed by workspace real path, value `{sha256, approvedAt}`.
- **Order:** bounded no-follow read → SHA-256 → status → strict parse → split into the restrictive part and relaxations.
- **Statuses:** ABSENT, NEW, MATCH, MISMATCH, REMOVED.
- **The restrictive part always applies**, as it does today for untrusted workspaces.
- **Relaxations apply only on trusted and MATCH.**
- **Interactive NEW or MISMATCH with relaxations:** ask, show the relaxations, record on yes.
- **Headless:** never ask. Drop the relaxations, apply the restrictive part, emit a notice, and record it. The run continues tighter, and an edit that needed the relaxation ends BLOCKED through the existing `needs_input` path (`dispatch_tool_call.ts:342-361`).
- **`NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE=1`** counts as status `ENV_TRUSTED` for CI, unless `workspace.disable_env_trust` is set.
- **A project parse error stays non-fatal** but becomes loud: built-in defaults, no relaxations, a notice, and a doctor warning.

### GV-5 refusals

All exit 2 at launch.

| Trigger | Message |
|---|---|
| `--yes` under `disable_yes` or `strict` | `nexus: --yes is disabled by managed policy (system tier, /etc/nexus/policy.toml, approval.strict). Run without it; approvals will be asked.` |
| `--mode auto`, or `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE=auto` | `nexus: mode 'auto' (from --mode) is disabled by managed policy (&lt;source&gt;). Use --mode review or --mode read-only.` |
| `/mode auto`, the "always" answer, sidecar always-approve | not an exit: mode unchanged, the one call is approved, notice printed with the source |
| `--sandbox none` or no sandbox under `sandbox.required` | forced to `auto` plus `requireSandbox`; an explicit `none` is refused |
| `--sandbox-network` or `NEXUS_SANDBOX_NETWORK` under `forbid_network` | refused at launch |
| MCP server not in `mcp.allow` | not connected; `MCP server 'x' not started: not in mcp.allow (&lt;source&gt;)` |
| Plugin not in `plugins.allow` | not imported; same form |

**CS-9 agreement:**
- Managed `forbid` and `prompt` rules are evaluated by the same `decideCommand`, in a combined set.
- A managed `prompt` holds under full access. This changes `dispatch_tool_call.ts:448` for managed-sourced rules only.
- `CommandRuleDecision` gains an optional `source`.

## 4. Choke points to change

| Point | Today | After | Editable now |
|---|---|---|---|
| `config.ts:412-468` `buildConfig` | flags and env decide | throws the policy error, refuses per GV-5, forces the sandbox | yes (re-check claim) |
| `security.ts:354-388` `loadPolicy` | project file, silent fallback | merges managed tiers, integrity, loud project errors | yes |
| `security.ts:158-165` | default protected paths | add `policy.toml`, `policy_trust.json` | yes |
| `approval_policy.ts:29-41` | full access returns false | `require_human` and mode clamp checked first | yes |
| `dispatch_tool_call.ts:314-322, 399-455, 505-510` | operator rules only, no source | combined rules, managed `prompt` holds, source appended, guidance fixed | yes |
| `spine_handlers.ts:194-200`, `completion_oracle.ts:22-28` | build `PolicyContext` | same clamp helper | yes |
| `mcp.ts:601-606, 620-621, 642-654` | connect anything configured | one `admit()` before construction | yes |
| `plugins.ts:96-99` | import every `.js` | hash allowlist before `import()` | yes |
| `repl.ts:150-158, 201, 534, 626-629, 1037-1041`; `sidecar.ts:145, 887-891, 1017-1020`; `slash/session_commands.ts:175` | switch to auto freely | clamp helper before `setApprovalMode` | yes |
| `workspace_trust.ts:36-38` | env grants trust | honour `disable_env_trust`; add the hash store | yes |
| `doctor.ts:379-394, 1357-1365, 1606` | no policy finding | four `policy.*` findings and a posture line | yes |
| `agent.ts:745-760, 468-478, 789-792, 1546-1555` | setter and manager wiring | authoritative clamp, threading, `requireSandbox`, persistence | **no** |
| `index.ts:114-128, 682-720, 1084-1103` | startup, `verify --yes`, `trust` | startup check for every client command, refuse `verify --yes`, record the hash, register `nexus policy` | **no** |
| `types.ts` (`HarnessConfig`, receipt at `:154`) | | `managedPolicy`, `policySource` | **no** |
| `sandbox.ts:189-192` | env enables network | clamp; mask policy files | **no** |
| `core/verify.ts:225, 237, 756`; `verify_contract.ts:221` | `base_tree` or `default` | add `system`, `user` | **no** (`verify.ts`) |
| `protocol.ts:199` and `protocol.rs` | no policy field | optional `policy` on `approval.request` | **no** (`.rs`) |

## 5. Security analysis for G-3

| Attack | Rule that stops it |
|---|---|
| A lower tier loosens a higher one | No grant is expressible; the merge is a meet; relaxations are void where a managed tier speaks. |
| Deleting the user or project file | An absent tier is the identity, so the result is never below the system tier. Deleting the project file can only remove relaxations. |
| Deleting the system file | Needs root; an unsafe `/etc/nexus` directory fails closed. |
| A repository plants `policy.toml` | Only two fixed absolute paths are read as managed; nothing under the workspace or cwd. |
| Project file honoured before trust | Bytes are hashed before parsing; relaxations require trusted and MATCH. See the open decision in section 8. |
| Environment variables | No variable names a policy path. `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE`, `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE`, `NEXUS_SANDBOX_NETWORK` and `NEXUS_EGRESS_POLICY` are all clamped after the merge. |
| `HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` redirected | Affects only the user tier, which the user owns anyway; the system path is absolute. |
| Symlink or swap race | `lstat`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat` dev/ino comparison, loaded once, frozen. |
| Malformed, truncated or duplicate key | Strict parser; an empty file is an error. |
| Newer file read by an older Nexus | Every key addition bumps `schema_version`; a reader accepts only its supported set and fails closed on a higher one. Rollout order: Nexus first, then policy. |
| Corrupt `command_rules.json` | Under any managed tier, a load failure drops `allow` rules only. |
| Model edits the policy files | Protected paths plus the `policy` verification class. |

**Residual risks to document:**
- An unsandboxed shell running as the user can edit the user tier and `policy_trust.json` for the next run. Only the system tier is tamper-resistant.
- An admitted in-process plugin can defeat any in-process control (PLAN.md:88), so `plugins.allow` with hashes is the real control.
- An `mcp.allow` entry by bare name is spoofable by whoever writes `mcp.json`; pin with `command:` or `url:`.

## 6. Acceptance tests

- **Precedence table (`managed_policy_merge.test.ts`).** Generated.
  - Per key, the Cartesian product of small domains across the three tiers. Booleans have 27 cases. List domains are {absent, [], [a], [b], [a,b]}, giving 125 cases. Verification mode also varies the project's legacy modes and trust state.
  - Expected value and deciding tier come from an independent oracle per key type, not from the merge.
  - Also assert idempotence, commutativity, associativity and monotonicity.
- **"Project cannot loosen system."** For every key and system value, and every project document (trusted, env-trusted, legacy relaxations, malformed, absent), the effective value is at least as restrictive as the system value.
- **Corruption classes (`managed_policy_load.test.ts`).** Every row of the fail-closed table, through the injected loader, asserting code and exact message.
- **Real CLI, system-path injection.**
  - Recommended: no production seam. A helper `cli/test/helpers/system_policy_cli.ts` reuses the interposition pattern at `cli/test/operator_config_startup.test.ts:33-49`: spawn `node --import tsx --input-type=module -e &lt;script&gt;`, patch the `fs` calls for exactly `/etc/nexus` and `/etc/nexus/policy.toml` to redirect to a temp file reporting uid 0 and mode 0644, then import `src/index.ts`.
  - Production code keeps a literal constant and no env, argv or setter.
  - Guard tests: the literal `/etc/nexus` appears in exactly one source file that references neither `process.env` nor `process.argv`; and a run with `NEXUS_SYSTEM_POLICY`, `NEXUS_POLICY`, `NEXUS_POLICY_FILE`, `NEXUS_HOME`, `NEXUS_ROOT`, `XDG_CONFIG_DIRS` pointing at a permissive file still enforces the interposed system tier.
  - Rejected alternative: a latch-guarded `setSystemPolicyPathForTests` export. It is simpler but ships in `dist` and is callable by an in-process plugin.
- **Strict mode.** `run --yes` and `run --mode auto` exit 2 with stderr containing the tier, file and key, and no request is made. `/mode auto` is refused at runtime.
- **Integrity.** MATCH, MISMATCH, NEW and REMOVED, each interactive and headless; env trust with and without `disable_env_trust`.
- **Lockout recovery.** With a corrupt tier, `--version`, `--help`, `doctor` and `policy check` work. `doctor` reports `policy.system` as fail with the same message, and passes after the fix.
- **Doctor.** `doctor --json` carries `policy.system|user|project|effective` findings with file, status, SHA-256 and deciding keys.

## 7. Slice plan

1. **Pure library, no claimed file.** The three `managed_policy*.ts` modules; the `toml`, `merge`, `load` and `integrity` tests; a new `docs/MANAGED_POLICY.md` with schema, grammar and recovery.
2. **GV-1 enforcement.** `config.ts`, `security.ts`, `approval_policy.ts`, `dispatch_tool_call.ts`, `command_rules.ts`, `workspace_trust.ts`, `spine_handlers.ts`, `completion_oracle.ts`, plus the real-CLI helper and the corrupted-policy tests.
3. **GV-5.** `mcp.ts`, `plugins.ts`, `repl.ts`, `sidecar.ts`, `slash/session_commands.ts`, and the strict-mode tests.
4. **Visibility.** `doctor.ts`, a new `policy_cli.ts` (`show|check|approve`), and the published schema.
5. **Claimed files, as they free up.** `index.ts`, `agent.ts`, `types.ts`, `sandbox.ts`, `core/verify.ts` with `verify_contract.ts`, `protocol.ts` with `protocol.rs`, `session_export.ts`, `run_contract.ts`, the manual and changelog, and the G-3 policy suite.

- **Dependencies:** slices 2 and 3 share the approval subsystem, so merge them one at a time (AGENTS.md).
- **Not closed until slice 5:** commands that never call `buildConfig` get the startup check only then.
- **Process:** each PR needs the XERJ "Reference coding" section. I did not query XERJ.

**Published schema: yes.** Generate `docs/protocol/policy-v1.schema.json` and `POLICY_VERSIONS.json` from `PolicyDocument` with `generateResultSchema` (`cli/scripts/result_schema.mjs:17`), with a parity test. It describes the parsed document, so `nexus policy check` remains the operator's primary validator.

## 8. Open decisions

| Decision | Recommended default |
|---|---|
| Format | TOML subset at D16's paths |
| System path | The constant `/etc/nexus/policy.toml`, not configurable at build or run time |
| User tier on error | Fail closed, as D16 says, with the move-aside recovery |
| Headless MISMATCH | Continue tighter, with relaxations dropped; do not exit |
| "Never read before trust" | Never honour relaxations before trust and MATCH, but keep applying restrictions from an untrusted file. The literal reading would regress today's behaviour and the `nexus verify` base-tree guard (`verify_contract.ts:221`). Your call. |
| CS-9 rules | Keep `command_rules.json` separate; managed tiers add `forbid` and `prompt` only |
| Exit code | 2 with a `managed policy error` prefix; no new `RUN_EXIT_CODES` row while the manual is claimed |
| Refuse or clamp explicit flags | Refuse at launch; clamp with a notice at runtime |
| Migration | None needed: `.nexus-security.json` keeps its shape. The only visible change is a notice on a malformed file, and a one-time approval of existing relaxations in trusted workspaces. |
| macOS and Windows | macOS uses the same POSIX path. Windows has no system tier (no ownership check) and doctor says so. Reserve `%ProgramData%\nexus\policy.toml` in the docs. |
| Root-run CI job writing a real `/etc/nexus/policy.toml` | Optional, not required |

**Not determined:**
- Whether new tests need registering in `cli/scripts/test_tiers.mjs` or `test_shards.mjs`.
- Whether the Rust core or TUI enforces or renders any of this.
- Whether the `fs.default.*` interposition works against compiled `dist`; it is proven only under `tsx`.

<result># LSP maturity and enhancement: scoped proposal and plan (read-only)

Commit read: `b1a4b4f6` ("Merge pull request #713 … pa4-provider-neutral-workflows") in `/home/mike/nexus-wt/gv1`. Nothing was run, built or modified. Paths are relative to that worktree; `lsp.ts` means `cli/src/lsp.ts`. "Verified" means read in code; "inferred" is marked.

The headline: the security posture is already strong (trust, read-only and sandbox gates, scrubbed environment and PATH, bounded framing). The weak areas are correctness of what the model is told (stale buffers, position units, timeouts too short for Rust), lifecycle hygiene, zero configuration, zero doctor coverage, and docs that contradict themselves.

## 1. Inventory

### Client (`cli/src/lsp.ts`, 663 lines, no dependency)

| Area | What exists |
|---|---|
| Server choice | By file extension only, `getServerForFile` 530-549: `.rs` rust-analyzer (`awaitProgress`), `.ts/.tsx` and `.js/.jsx` `typescript-language-server --stdio`, `.py` `pyright-langserver --stdio`, `.go` `gopls`. `.mts/.cts/.mjs/.cjs/.pyi` are unmapped. An override table exists (`opts.servers`, 506-509) but the product never passes one (`cli/src/tools.ts:1009-1018`). |
| Spawn | `spawn(cmd, args, {cwd: rootPath, env})` at 125-129, no shell. stderr is discarded (130), so a start failure has no reason text. |
| Environment | `buildLspEnvironment` 33-55: the MCP allowlist (`cli/src/mcp.ts:56-78`) plus toolchain variables (line 10); proxy URLs with credentials dropped (36-38). PATH keeps only absolute entries that do not resolve into the workspace (46-53), except when the workspace contains home or a system bin directory (40-45). |
| Environment parity | Stricter than commands, which use a deny list (`cli/src/command_env.ts:12`). `HOME` passes through and the filesystem is unrestricted. |
| Sandbox | Never sandboxed. The gate refuses to start a server when read-only, untrusted, or any sandbox is set or required (`cli/src/tools.ts:1010-1016`). A refusal also stops servers already running (521-528). |
| Framing | `Content-Length`, byte-accurate, 16 MiB cap per message and buffer (8, 202-227, 277); an oversized stream is dropped and the server SIGKILLed (193-200). JSON parse errors are swallowed (275). |
| `initialize` | 151-165, 5 s timeout: `processId`, `rootUri`, hover formats, `definition.dynamicRegistration: true`, `publishDiagnostics` with `versionSupport`, `synchronization.didSave`, `window.workDoneProgress`. Not sent: `general.positionEncodings`, `workspaceFolders`, `initializationOptions`, `clientInfo`, `didChangeWatchedFiles`. |
| Server capabilities | Ignored except `textDocumentSync.save` (168-171). |
| Document sync | `syncFile` 354-394: `didOpen` once, then a full-text `didChange` plus `didSave` when the disk content differs. Only the queried file is synced. There is no `didClose` anywhere. `notifyFileOpen` (337-347) is dead code. |
| Diagnostics wait | `firstDiagnostics` 419-436 returns any cached list at once, otherwise waits for the first publish. With `awaitProgress` it then polls every 100 ms until progress is idle, quiet for 1.5 s (or a 4 s grace), and 8 s have passed since an edit (397-417; line 9). |
| Timeouts | Manager default 5000 ms (587), which the tool uses unchanged (`cli/src/tools.ts:1641`). Requests default to 8000 ms (280); `initialize` 5000 ms. |
| Rust empty first publish | Handled by the `awaitProgress` loop above, plus stale-version rejection when the server sends a version (263-266). |
| Request ids, cancellation | Incrementing integer (285). A timeout deletes the pending entry (296-301); no `$/cancelRequest` is sent and no AbortSignal is accepted. |
| Server-initiated requests | Answered (230-235, 318-324): `workspace/configuration` gets one null per item, everything else gets `result: null`. |
| Shutdown | 478-488: sends `shutdown`, then `exit` and SIGTERM in the same tick without awaiting the reply. No SIGKILL escalation, no process group. |
| Crash or hang | `exit` rejects pending requests and wakes waiters (136-139, 184-190). The next call starts a new server because `initialized` is false (558-559). No backoff. A hang is bounded by the timeouts. |
| Lifetime, concurrency | One manager per `RustCoreBridge` (`cli/src/tools.ts:1003-1021`), one client per command and arguments (556), reused across calls. Concurrent starts are de-duplicated (560-578). |
| Cleanup | `bridge.shutdown()` calls `shutdownAll` (`cli/src/tools.ts:1023-1025`); a `process.on("exit")` backstop exists (511-517). SIGINT paths call `app.shutdown` (`cli/src/index.ts:588-600, 713-714`); I did not trace every path. |
| Roots | Always the workspace root (120, 148, 564). No nearest-config detection, no multi-root. |
| Paths and URIs | Containment is checked on the real path (359-362), but the URI is built from the unresolved path (367). Maps are keyed by exact URI string (263, 267). Text is read as UTF-8 with no size cap (363). |
| Position encoding | Not negotiated. `line-1` and `character-1` are passed through (446, 463), so the unit is UTF-16 by protocol default. |
| Severity | 1 is error, 2 is warning, everything else is "information" (258); "hint" is declared (88) but never produced. |

### Tools exposed to the model

- **Names and schemas:** `lsp_hover`, `lsp_definition` (path, line, character; "1-indexed", unit unstated) and `lsp_diagnostics` (path), hand-written in `cli/src/core/capability_definitions/lsp.ts:3-108`.
- **Classification:** `mutating: false`, `effect: "exec"`. Review mode therefore asks before each call and auto mode does not (`cli/src/core/approval_policy.ts:51-73`).
- **Parallelism:** all three are in `READ_ONLY_TOOLS` (`cli/src/core/agent_support.ts:624-626`), so they run in the parallel read batch (`cli/src/core/run_turn.ts:904`).
- **Tool group:** `lsp`, hidden until the operator text matches `/\b(lsp|language server|go to definition|hover|diagnostics)\b/i` or the model calls `enable_tools` (`cli/src/core/tool_groups.ts:25-29`). I found no role-specific LSP filtering.
- **Path protection:** applied (`cli/src/tools.ts:1307-1325`).
- **Diagnostics output:** sanitised, redacted, 1-based, capped at 50 records and 32 000 characters, fenced as untrusted (`cli/src/diagnostics.ts:219-237, 256-269`).
- **Hover output:** raw server text, not fenced, capped or sanitised at this layer (`cli/src/tools.ts:1610-1622`).
- **Definition output:** raw JSON of server locations: `file://` URIs with 0-based ranges (`cli/src/tools.ts:1624-1636`). I did not trace the generic cap in `toolResultContent` (`cli/src/agent.ts:3325`).

### Surroundings

- **TUI:** only a verb mapping to "Inspect" (`core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_view.rs:2097-2098`). No server state, no diagnostics view, no LSP-specific sidecar event.
- **`nexus doctor`:** nothing (no match in `cli/src/doctor.ts`).
- **Configuration:** none. No server override, disable switch or timeout setting.
- **Telemetry:** no spans in `lsp.ts`; only the generic `onToolStart` observer (`cli/src/core/dispatch_tool_call.ts:262`).
- **Docs:** three one-line entries in `docs/USER_MANUAL.md:620-622`, with nothing on prerequisites or refusals, plus `docs/LSP_REAL_SERVERS.md`.
- **Other references:** `cli/src/core/execution_tracking.ts:379` already lists a non-existent `lsp_references`; `cli/src/material_deps.ts:56` reuses `buildLspEnvironment`.

|---|---|---|

| `cli/test/lsp_progress.test.ts` (4) | fake staged | `awaitProgress` returns the later error; never-ending progress is bounded by the timeout. |
| `cli/test/lsp_trust.test.ts` (13) | fake | Refusals through the executor (untrusted, read-only, sandbox); environment without secrets; PATH filtering; split multi-byte framing; the 16 MiB boundary; trust revocation; stderr flood. |

**Weekly workflow** (`.github/workflows/lsp-real-servers.yml`): Monday 04:17 UTC and on demand, not a required check, ubuntu-22.04. Pins: Node 22.22.1, Go 1.27.4, TypeScript 5.6.3, typescript-language-server 4.3.4, pyright 1.1.390, Rust 1.95.0 with its rust-analyzer component, gopls v0.24.0. All four are required (`NEXUS_LSP_REQUIRE`).

**Passing status:** the docs record manual hosted runs that passed (44606102675 on 2026-11-19 with 10 tests; 44670790609 on `f87ff07f`, 2026-11-20). Whether any scheduled run has passed is not recorded, and I did not check.

**Gaps with no test:**
- Non-ASCII positions for hover and definition.
- The shape of definition output.
- A stale buffer in a second, already opened file.
- A list cached before the file's first open.
- Workspaces or files reached through a symlink (URI mismatch).
- A missing binary: how long the failure takes and what the message says.
- Initialize timeout; shutdown against a server that ignores SIGTERM; grandchild processes.
- Server requests other than progress-create (`workspace/configuration`, `client/registerCapability`, `workspace/applyEdit`).
- Automatic restart inside one manager: the real-server assertion at `lsp_real_servers.test.ts:252` accepts "unavailable".
- Large or non-UTF-8 files; nested project roots; JavaScript with a real server.
- The product's 5 s timeout: every real-server test passes 60-120 s.
- The real-server tests retry hover until the case timeout, which the product does not do.

## 3. Defects and weaknesses

### Correctness

1. **Stale buffers in other open files (verified).** `syncFile` updates only the queried file (354-394) and nothing is ever closed. Once file B has been opened by any LSP call, the server keeps B's old in-memory text after the agent edits B on disk. Diagnostics, hover and definition for A that depend on B are then computed against old B and reported as `ok`.
2. **A list cached before first open is returned as current.** A publish for a never-opened URI is stored (263-267). The first-open branch does not clear it (369-371), and `firstDiagnostics` returns it immediately (420-421). Whether servers publish package-wide before open (gopls, rust-analyzer) is my inference; the code path is verified.
3. **The tool timeout defeats the Rust settling design (verified constants).** The tool uses 5000 ms (`cli/src/tools.ts:1641`; 587). The post-edit window is 8000 ms (line 9; 411), so the loop always ends on the timeout and returns whatever is cached. `docs/LSP_REAL_SERVERS.md` records about 13-18 s for a first Rust call, so the first `lsp_diagnostics` on Rust is expected to return "published no diagnostics within 5000ms" (inferred from the documented timing).
4. **Position units are undefined.** There is no `positionEncodings` negotiation (154-164) and no conversion (446, 463). The schema says "character offset" (`capability_definitions/lsp.ts:29, 68`). A column counted in code points or bytes is wrong on any line with astral or non-ASCII text before the symbol. Diagnostic columns are UTF-16 units plus one (`cli/src/diagnostics.ts:233`).
5. **Definition output is inconsistent with its input.** It returns 0-based ranges and `file://` URIs (`cli/src/tools.ts:1631`), while inputs and diagnostics are 1-based workspace paths. The model must add one to chain calls, and nothing tells it so.
6. **URI identity.** The URI comes from the unresolved path (367, 398) and `rootPath` is not a real path (120). A server that canonicalises paths publishes under a different URI, the waiter never fires, and the result is "unavailable" (inferred). Exact-string keys are also sensitive to percent-encoding differences.
7. **Severity.** Hints are reported as "information" (258).
8. **Server requests.** `workspace/applyEdit` gets `null` rather than `{applied: false}`, and unknown methods get a success instead of MethodNotFound (321). The three methods you named are answered acceptably.
9. **Hover and definition have no readiness wait.** `syncFile` is followed immediately by the request (622-625, 645-648). The real-server tests need retries (docs, "Hover and definition" paragraph), so a first call in the product commonly returns "No hover info" or an empty list with `success: false`, which is indistinguishable from "no symbol here".

### Robustness

10. **Missing binary.** The `error` handler only clears a flag (132-134) and does not reject the pending `initialize`. A missing server therefore appears to cost the full 5 s on every call (inferred from Node's spawn behaviour; needs a characterising test), and failures are not cached (570-575). The message conflates "not installed" with "failed to start" (594), and stderr is discarded (130).
11. **Shutdown.** No wait and no SIGKILL escalation (478-488), and the reference is dropped at once (485). A server that ignores SIGTERM, or its children (tsserver, cargo, proc-macro servers), can survive (inferred for children). The `shutdown` request's 8 s timer is not unref'd.
12. **Unbounded state.** `opened` holds the full text of every file ever touched (102, 370) and `diagnosticsMap` grows with every published URI. There is no file-size cap (363).
13. **No cancellation.** An operator interrupt cannot abort a wait, and no `$/cancelRequest` is sent on timeout (296-301).
14. **Shared progress state.** `progressSeen` is reset by any edit (380-381), although parallel LSP calls are allowed (`run_turn.ts:904`).
15. **Monorepos.** One root (148) and no `workspaceFolders`, so nested Cargo, Go or tsconfig projects depend on each server's own discovery.

### Security

16. **Code execution is inside the existing trust decision (verified).** A hostile repository cannot get a server started merely by the agent asking: untrusted, read-only and sandboxed sessions are refused before spawn (`cli/src/tools.ts:1010-1016`), tested in `lsp_trust.test.ts:50-105`. In a trusted workspace a server does run project code (build scripts, proc macros, tsserver plugins, the workspace's own `typescript`) with no sandbox, the user's `HOME`, and no prompt in auto mode.
17. **Env override widens trust.** `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE=1` trusts any workspace (`cli/src/workspace_trust.ts:38`).
18. **Hover text is an unfenced injection surface.** It comes from doc comments in the workspace (`cli/src/tools.ts:1613-1617`), unlike diagnostics.
19. **Sandbox means no LSP at all.** A claim directory named `SUITE_VR1C_DEFAULT_SANDBOX_20261122` exists. If a default sandbox lands, LSP becomes unavailable by default (inferred from the name only).

### Usability

20. No configuration, no doctor finding, no manual section; the tool descriptions do not name the supported languages.
21. `docs/LSP_REAL_SERVERS.md` contradicts itself. The "Skip versus fail" section says the workflow requires `typescript,python`. "What it does not cover" says there is no incremental-edit or crash scenario and that gopls is `@latest`. Later paragraphs, the test and the workflow say the opposite.

|---|---|---|

| Fresh answers (never stale as `ok`) | Not met | Defects 1, 2, 3 |

## 5. What "enhancement" could mean (ranked)

All of these can be tested without a model by extending the fake server and, where a real server matters, adding a case to the weekly job.

| # | Capability | Agent gain | Risk | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Warm-up and readiness (start on group enable; retry hover and definition until ready) | Removes first-call failures | Low | S |
| 2 | `lsp_references` | Impact analysis before an edit; the name is already anticipated | Read-only; needs a cap | S |
| 3 | Document and workspace symbols | Position-free entry point, which removes most column guessing | Read-only | S |
| 4 | Symbol-anchored input (line plus symbol text, client computes the UTF-16 column) | Removes the encoding hazard for models | None | S |
| 5 | Implementation and type definition | Navigation in interface-heavy code | Read-only | S |
| 6 | Post-edit diagnostics as an advisory pre-check | Faster feedback loop | Stale or false signal; advisory only | M |
| 7 | Pull diagnostics (`textDocument/diagnostic`) where advertised | Deterministic wait instead of heuristics | Per-server variance | M |
| 8 | Configuration (command, extensions, disable, timeouts, initialization options) | Unblocks other servers and versions | Project-level config is a code-execution vector | M |
| 9 | Call hierarchy, signature help | Modest | Read-only | S each |
| 10 | Rename as a proposed edit | Safe multi-file rename | A `WorkspaceEdit` with file operations must be reduced to text edits through the normal edit gate | M-L |
| 11 | Code actions and quick fixes | Auto-fix imports | Actions can carry server-executed commands (`workspace/executeCommand`); only edit-only actions are safe | L |
| 12 | Formatting | Low; formatters already run as commands | Edit path | S |
| 13 | TUI diagnostics or server-state view | Operator visibility | Protocol change; files claimed | M |
| 14 | Incremental sync | Performance only | Offset bugs | M; reject for now |

**More languages.** Each costs about one table row, a fixture, a pinned install and a weekly case.
- clangd (C and C++): cheap, a single binary, but needs `compile_commands.json`. Highest value after the current four.
- Java (jdtls): heavy, with a JVM, a data directory and minute-scale start. Size M.
- C#, Ruby, PHP: S-M each.
- Kotlin and Swift: toolchain-heavy on Linux CI; defer.
- Zero-cost alternative: enhancement 8 lets users bring any server, unsupported.

**LSP and the type-check gate.** They are fully separate today. The gate shells out to `tsc`, `cargo check`, `go vet`, `pyright` or `mypy` per discovered project, including nested ones (`cli/src/tester.ts:163-181, 206-251`). It uses approval binding, config and tool hashes, and a baseline (`cli/src/core/completion_oracle.ts:73-141`). The only shared code is the record type and sanitiser in `cli/src/diagnostics.ts`.

They can disagree in these ways:
- LSP is per file, single root, and reads in-memory buffers (defect 1).
- The server's bundled or workspace TypeScript may differ from the project's `tsc`.
- pyright against mypy.
- `go vet` covers analyzers that gopls runs differently.
- rust-analyzer's native diagnostics against rustc's.
- LSP has timing heuristics; the gate does not.

Recommendation: LSP never satisfies or blocks the gate. It may be a clearly labelled, advisory pre-check (enhancement 6), and receipts may cite LSP records as tool facts but never as the verdict.

## 6. Reference coding (XERJ)

Four read-only queries ran against the `opencode` corpus (MIT, index 21 days old, flagged not verified complete). The client implementation itself was not retrieved, so I can report nothing on opencode's server-request handling, root detection in code, or diagnostics waiting; a miss is not proof of absence. Only one peer is indexed.

What the docs, schema and tests show:
- **Off by default, with a warning.** `opencode/packages/web/src/content/docs/lsp.mdx:59-100` says servers "can get out of sync, use significant memory … slow down agent workflows", and that running typecheck CLIs directly is often better.
- **Lifecycle.** Servers start lazily when a file with a matching extension is opened, and are reused (same file, "How It Works").
- **Configuration.** Per server: `disabled`, `command[]`, `extensions[]`, `env`, `initialization` (`opencode/packages/schema/src/config/lsp.ts:10`; `lsp.mdx:163`).
- **Model-facing tool.** One `lsp` tool, experimental behind a flag and its own permission key, with operations `goToDefinition`, `findReferences`, `hover`, `documentSymbol`, `workspaceSymbol`, `goToImplementation`, `prepareCallHierarchy`, `incomingCalls`, `outgoingCalls` (`opencode/packages/web/src/content/docs/tools.mdx`, the "lsp (experimental)" section near line 159). There is no rename, code action or formatting.
- **Auto-download.** Built-in servers are downloaded unless `OPENCODE_DISABLE_LSP_DOWNLOAD` is set.

Take as ideas: the configuration shape; the read-only operation set as the enhancement boundary; an experimental flag for new operations; the "LSP is advisory, the CLI type-check is the authority" position.

Reject: auto-download (network, supply chain, conflicts with "no new runtime dependency"); project-level config that can name a command without a trust decision; a single multiplexed tool, since Nexus's per-tool capabilities fit its approval model better.

Nothing was copied.

## 7. Proposed units (PLAN.md style)

### LS: language-server maturity and enhancement

| Unit | What | Accept | Size | Pri |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LS-1 | Hardening: re-sync or close every open document before each query; clear the cache on first open; key by canonical path; per-server timeouts with readiness retry; fast, distinct "not installed" versus "failed to start" with a stderr tail; awaited shutdown with SIGKILL escalation; bounded open-document set and file-size cap; fenced, capped hover; workspace-relative 1-based definition output; hint severity; correct `applyEdit` and unknown-method replies | Each defect in §3 (1-3, 5-12, 18) has a fake-server test that fails before and passes after; the leak guard shows no surviving process; the weekly job stays green with product-default timeouts | M | P1 |
| LS-2 | Position encoding and roots: negotiate `positionEncodings`; convert between a documented model-facing unit and the negotiated one; send `workspaceFolders`; detect the nearest project root, reusing the roots `discoverTypechecks` finds | Non-ASCII fixtures (BMP and astral) resolve the right symbol on fake and real servers; a nested-project fixture gets diagnostics per sub-project | M | P1 |
| LS-3 | First enhancements behind an experimental setting: warm-up, `lsp_references`, document and workspace symbols, symbol-anchored positions; `nexus doctor` findings per server (found, version, why refused); user-level config; spans; manual section; docs corrected | Schemas generated from types; outputs fenced and capped; doctor test; one real-server case per new operation | M | P1 |
| LS-4 | More languages (clangd first), advisory post-edit diagnostics, pull diagnostics | Pinned weekly case per language; advisory output never changes a verdict (tested) | M | P2 |

**Release-gate implications.**
- G-1: unchanged.
- G-3 (gate-bypass suite): add cases showing that an untrusted, read-only or sandboxed session starts no server, that project config cannot name a server command, and that no LSP result raises a verdict.
- G-6: manual section and doctor documentation.
- New gate, G-LS: the weekly real-server run is green on the release head, triggered manually before tagging, because it is not a required check.

<pasted_content id="2d81">

1. Core Navigation & Symbol Lookup

• Exact definitions and references: Jump straight to symbol definitions and list all usages across files instead of guessing with regex or text search.
• Document and workspace symbols: Outline file structures or search symbols across a project without reading entire files into context.
• Type and call hierarchy inspection: Read exact types, signatures, documentation, and caller/callee relationships to gauge blast radius before editing.

2. Real-Time Feedback & Self-Correction

• Incremental diagnostics: Receive type and syntax errors immediately after edits so the agent can self-correct without waiting for full builds or test runs.
• Document synchronization: Keep the language server's in-memory state aligned with the agent's edits via proper initialization, change tracking, and clean shutdown.

3. Semantic Refactoring & Editing

• Safe symbol renames: Rename functions, classes, variables, files, or folders consistently across the codebase.
• Code transformations: Support structured edits such as extracting code blocks into functions or moving functions between files.

4. Production Lifecycle & Agent Ergonomics

• Process & capability management: Handle server startup, capability discovery, workspace initialization, error recovery, and restarts reliably across languages (such as Pyright, rust-analyzer, gopls, clangd, and jdtls).
• Resource & prompt discipline: Manage cold-start indexing times and memory overhead, while guiding the agent to prefer LSP for semantic queries and grep/read for text, comments, or literals.
</pasted_content id="2d81">

## Files
- **Added, source:** `cli/src/core/managed_policy_toml.ts`, `managed_policy.ts`, `managed_policy_load.ts` (holds the `/etc/nexus/policy.toml` constant) and `managed_policy_inputs.ts` (`resolveProductionPolicyInputs()`, called from nowhere).
- **Added, schema and docs:** `cli/scripts/gen_policy_schema.mjs`, `docs/protocol/policy-v1.schema.json`, `docs/protocol/POLICY_VERSIONS.json`, `docs/MANAGED_POLICY.md`.
- **Added, tests:** `cli/test/managed_policy_{toml,merge,load,integrity,schema}.test.ts` and `cli/test/helpers/policy_fixture.ts`.
- **Edited:** `docs/protocol/README.md` only (one appended section, +14 lines).
- `git status --short` lists only these; `cli/package.json`, the npm lockfile and both Cargo files have no diff.

## Where I departed from the plan
- **Relaxation void rule widened.** The plan voids project relaxations only when a managed tier sets `verification.mode`. But `verificationPaths.allow` exempts paths in `classifyVerificationPath` (`security.ts:715`), so a trusted project could exempt a path a managed `verification.protected` names. Relaxations are now void when a managed tier sets any key under `[verification]`, including `protected = []`.
- **`blockedCommands` is not mapped to `commands.forbid`.** Today it is a substring tripwire (`trimmed.includes(pattern)`, `security.ts:404`), not a word prefix, so `"dd if="` as a prefix rule would stop matching `dd if=/dev/zero`. The adapter returns the patterns unchanged as `blockedCommandPatterns`; slice 2 must keep the legacy matcher for them.
- **Seventh integrity status, `UNREADABLE`.** A project file that is a symlink, not regular or over 64 KiB has no hash, and calling it `REMOVED` would be wrong.
- **Unsafe system directory refuses even with no policy file inside.** This is the table read literally; it widens the lockout surface, so please confirm.
- **Messages I had to write.** The plan gives the two-line managed error form (used verbatim) but no GV-5 refusal texts, so those are mine (below). The user-tier second line keeps "Nexus will not start without this policy." and prints the real path, shell-quoted when needed, instead of `~`.
- **Code versus plan references:** `allowedDomains` is loaded in `security.ts` and I found no consumer there (my wider search failed on a shell glob, so other files are unchecked). Everything else I read matched the plan.

## Keys that could loosen
No key loosens when set in a managed file. Two cautions for later slices:
- The four allowlists (`egress.allowed_domains`, `egress.secret_destinations`, `mcp.allow`, `plugins.allow`) would become grants if a consumer treated an entry as "add this". The helpers are filters only; `secret_destinations` in particular must be applied over the operator's own destination set.
- With no managed `[verification]` key, a trusted and approved project file can still relax the built-in default to `off` or `record`, as it can today. This is documented as a residual risk; an admin closes it with `verification.mode`.

## Final key list
| Rule | Keys |
|---|---|
| required, no merge | `schema_version` (supported: 1) |
| OR | `approval.strict`, `approval.disable_auto`, `approval.disable_yes`, `approval.disable_full_access`, `commands.disable_allow_rules`, `sandbox.required`, `sandbox.forbid_network`, `mcp.disable_workspace`, `plugins.disable_workspace`, `workspace.disable_env_trust` |
| union | `approval.require_human`, `verification.protected`, `commands.forbid`, `commands.prompt`, `paths.protected` |
| intersection (absent = unrestricted, present even if empty = only these) | `egress.allowed_domains`, `egress.secret_destinations`, `mcp.allow`, `plugins.allow` |
| max | `verification.mode` (approve &lt; block), `egress.inspector` (record &lt; block) |

`approval.strict` is folded into the three `disable_*` values; the source key shows which one decided. Allowlists intersect as identical strings, so `*.example.com` in one tier and `api.example.com` in another admit nothing.

## Exact messages
Managed error, first line: `nexus: managed policy error (&lt;tier&gt; tier): &lt;file&gt;: [line N: ]&lt;detail&gt;.`
- System second line: ``Nexus will not start without this policy. Fix the file (root-owned; ask your administrator) and run `nexus doctor`. No setting or environment variable bypasses it.``
- User second line: `Nexus will not start without this policy. Fix it, or move it aside: mv &lt;file&gt; &lt;file&gt;.bad`

The system line tells the admin to run `nexus doctor`, which does not report on policy yet; the docs say so.

Error codes: `directory_unsafe`, `symlink`, `not_regular`, `unreadable`, `changed`, `owner`, `mode`, `too_large`, `invalid_utf8`, `bom`, `nul`, the parser codes and the validator codes. Every detail text is listed in `docs/MANAGED_POLICY.md` and asserted in the tests.

GV-5 texts, each followed by `[policy: &lt;tier&gt; &lt;file&gt; &lt;key&gt;]` or `[policy: default]`:
- `--yes is refused: managed policy disables it.`
- `--yes is refused: it selects approval mode 'auto', which managed policy disables.`
- `--yes is refused: it grants full access, which managed policy disables.`
- `&lt;origin&gt; is refused: managed policy disables approval mode 'auto'.`
- `&lt;origin&gt; is refused: managed policy disables full access.`
- `&lt;origin&gt; is ignored: managed policy disables approval mode 'auto'; the mode stays '&lt;current&gt;'.`
- `'always' is not remembered: managed policy disables approval mode 'auto'; this one call is approved.`
- `approval required: managed policy requires a human decision for &lt;effect&gt;, also under full access.`
- `&lt;origin&gt; is refused: managed policy requires the sandbox.`
- `the sandbox is on (auto): managed policy requires it.`
- `&lt;origin&gt; is refused: managed policy forbids network access in the sandbox.`
- `MCP server '&lt;n&gt;' is not started: it is not in the managed allowlist (mcp.allow).`
- `MCP server '&lt;n&gt;' is not started: managed policy disables MCP servers configured by the workspace.`
- `plugin '&lt;path&gt;' is not imported: it is not in the managed allowlist (plugins.allow).`
- `plugin '&lt;path&gt;' is not imported: managed policy disables plugins from the workspace.`
- `command refused: managed policy forbids '&lt;rule&gt;'.`
- `approval required: managed policy asks before '&lt;rule&gt;', also under full access.`
- `the egress inspector runs in '&lt;floor&gt;' mode, not '&lt;asked&gt;': managed policy sets the floor.`
- `'&lt;host&gt;' is not reachable: it is not in the managed allowlist (egress.allowed_domains).`
- `no credential is sent to '&lt;origin&gt;': it is not in the managed allowlist (egress.secret_destinations).`
- `NEXUS_TRUST_WORKSPACE is ignored: managed policy disables it.`

## New findings
- **22 (pinned):** `LocationLink[]` passes through raw with `targetUri` fields. A single `LocationLink` object becomes `{success: false, output: "[]"}`.
- **23 (pinned):** a missing `line`/`character` reaches the server as `{line: null, character: null}`; `"2.5"` is sent as `1.5`. There is no validation at the handler.
- **By reading only, not tested:** `cli/src/core/run_turn.ts` (~943-945) turns a `success: false` result with no error into `Error executing lsp_hover: Unknown error`. That is what the model would see for "no hover here" and for an empty definition.

## Generic tool-result cap
There is none per result. `executeTool` returns hover text as is (pinned at 300,145 characters). I traced the agent layer by reading; only the scanner verdict is tested:
- `agent.ts` `toolResultContent` → `screenForInjection` fences a result only when `core/injection_scanner.ts` suspects injection, and scans the first 200,000 characters.
- The only size bound is context-admission compaction in `agent.ts` `compactionCandidate`, which cuts unseen results when the request would not fit the context window.
- `NEXUS_TOOL_OUTPUT_MASK_TOKENS` is opt-in and masks only results the model has already acted on.

So hover is covered by no cap and by no unconditional fence.

About 41 s per run. The cases that wait on product timers dominate: missing binary 10.0 s, staged false clean 6.6 s, symlink 5.1 s, post-edit 5.1 s, initialize 5.0 s, shared progress 4.1 s, ignoring server 1.1 s.
