## Public functions
**`jsonrpc.ts`**
- `BoundedLineReader` (`push`, `end`, `bufferedBytes`, `oversizedLines`): splits bytes into lines and never holds more than the cap.
- `exceedsDepth`: nesting pre-scan that skips strings.
- `parseMessage`: classifies a line as request, notification, response or invalid (with code, id and a `respond` flag).
- `isRequestId`, `rpcError`, `oversizedLineError`, `resultResponse`, `errorResponse`, `notification`, `request`: small builders and the id rule.
- `encodeMessage`: one line per message, lone surrogates replaced.
- `RequestCorrelator` (`open`, `settle`, `abandon`, `pending`): integer ids from a random 31-bit start; `settle` returns matched, stale or unknown.

**`validate.ts`**
- `validateInitialize`, `negotiatedProtocolVersion`: version and `clientInfo` checks; the answer is always 1.
- `validateCwdShape` (pure) and `canonicalCwd` (injected `AcpFs`): the two halves of the `cwd` rule.
- `validateSessionId`: exactly `session_\d{8}_\d{6}_[a-z0-9]{8}`.
- `validateNewSession`, `validateLoadSession`: required `mcpServers` array; non-empty `additionalDirectories` or any `systemPrompt` refused.
- `validatePrompt`, `resourceLinkLine`: blocks to prompt text; a link becomes one reference line.
- `validateCancel`, `validateCancelRequest`, `validateReceiptGet`, `validatePermissionResult` (shape only), `singleLine`.

**`mapping.ts`**
- `SIDECAR_EVENT_DISPOSITION`, `TOOL_KINDS`, `toolKind`: the two classification tables.
- `initializeResult`, `postureFromState`, `newSessionResult`, `loadSessionResult`, `sessionUpdate`: session-level builders.
- `assistantDeltaToUpdate`, `toolCallId`, `toolStartToUpdate`, `toolEndToUpdate`: streaming updates.
- `unifiedDiffToBlocks`, `fileDiffToUpdate`: one diff block per hunk, `oldText` null for a new file.
- `approvalDigest`, `bindApprovalToToolCall`, `approvalToPermissionRequest`: the permission request.
- `permissionReplyToCommand`: the one decision point; its return type has `always: false` as a literal.
- `turnToStop`, `validationReceiptFromSections`, `verificationFromTurn`, `verificationUpdate`, `verdictChunk`, `promptResult`, `nexusReceipt`: turn result and verification.
- `historyToReplay`, `sidecarErrorToRpc`, `cancelledRequestError`.

## NV block as written
```ts
// NOT verified against the published specification as of this slice: see AC1_PLAN.md NV list.
// Every value below rests on a summary of the specification's schema page or on Nexus's own choice. Correct them here.
export const ACP_NV_MAX_PROTOCOL_VERSION = 65_535;           // uint16 per a summary of the schema
export const ACP_NV_REQUEST_ID_KINDS = ["string", "integer"] as const; // null and fractions refused; agent requests use integers
export const ACP_NV_NOT_INITIALIZED_CODE = ACP_ERROR.INVALID_REQUEST;  // our choice of rule and code
export const ACP_NV_BATCH_CODE = ACP_ERROR.INVALID_REQUEST;            // the specification has no batch rule
// Also unverified and therefore not used by any code here: the fs/* and terminal/* method shapes, every numeric
// limit above, and whether real clients send a non-empty mcpServers by default.
```
Verified items moved out with "published specification, &lt;page&gt;" comments:
- `ACP_RECEIPT_GET_METHOD`, with the two prefix constants and the reverse-DNS recommendation marked as an open owner decision.
- `ACP_VERIFICATION_CARRIER` and `ACP_CANCEL_REQUEST_METHOD`.
- `-32800` in `ACP_ERROR`.
- `max_turn_requests`.
- All three prompt capabilities sent as false.
- The two declared deviations, `ACP_DEVIATION_NO_CLIENT_MCP_SERVERS` and `ACP_DEVIATION_PARTIAL_REPLAY`.

`messageId` is not sent and has no field.

## Where the plan differed from the code
- **Credential failure**: the sidecar has no credential error code (`protocol.ts:74`). The `-32000` mapping is in `turnToStop`, for a failed turn whose `inferenceFailure` has condition `AUTH_FAILURE` or HTTP 401/403. All eight sidecar codes map to `-32603`.
- **Posture**: `read_only` is a field of `session.state` (`protocol.ts:134`), not of `posture`. Nothing in `session.state` carries the "count of extensions not loaded", so it is not reported.
- **History extent**: `history.total` counts conversation entries only (`emitHistory` in `sidecar.ts`), while `messages` includes tools and the outcome. `sent` is counted the same way as `total`.
- **Empty evidence id**: `tool.end` and `file.diff` can carry `evidence: ""` (`open.get(dispatch) ?? ""` in `observerFor`). The tool call id then falls back to `call:&lt;call_id&gt;`.
- **Session id length**: real ids are 32 characters, so the 128-character limit is unreachable; the regex is the effective bound. A 1-to-8 character suffix would have let a prefix through to `resolveResumeId`, so the suffix is exactly 8.
- **Schema builder limits** (`gen_protocol_schema.mjs:117`): it throws on an empty tuple, so `authMethods` and `additionalDirectories` are typed `unknown[]` with a comment. It never writes `additionalProperties: false`, so "no custom root fields" is enforced by a test allowlist, not by the schema. For the same reason any object is a valid `result` at the root, because the load result is `{}`.

## Tool-kind table
37 rows: all 36 names of `HARNESS_TOOLS` plus `verifier`. `delete_file` is `delete`; no tool is `move` or `switch_mode`; an unknown name maps to `other` through an own-property lookup. A test imports `HARNESS_TOOLS` and fails for any name without a row, and for any row that is not a catalogue tool or `verifier`.

## Reference coding
I read the opencode corpus files `packages/cli/src/acp/tool.ts`, `permission.ts`, `content.ts`, `replay.ts`, and grepped `translate.ts`, `compaction.ts` and `sessions.ts`. I did not open the hermes-agent checkout.
- **Took as ideas**: the kind-by-tool-name switch with `other` as default; a diff block in the permission request; the command as the title of an execute call; replaying a tool as `tool_call`; `session_info_update` carrying only `_meta`.
- **Rejected**: the `allow_always` option; reading file content to build diffs (theirs reads the file and applies the patch, ours uses only hunk text the sidecar already sent); turning embedded resources and images into prompt parts; `messageId`; client MCP servers.
- No code was copied, and there was no new XERJ query beyond the plan's.

## Existing files needing an edit
None. No existing test enumerates `docs/protocol` or the generators in a way the new files break.

## For your review
- **Schema coupling**: `AcpVerification` uses `ExecutionState`, `EngineeringOutcome`, `TurnReason` and `VerificationStatus` from `core/turn.ts`, and `ReceiptSection` from `evidence.ts`. A new `TurnReason` will fail `acp_schema.test.ts` until someone adds a ledger entry, as already happens for the sidecar schema. Say if you want plain `string` there instead.
- **`receipt_id` can repeat**: it is the validation receipt id whenever the receipt section shows one, including an `INVALIDATED` one or one left from an earlier turn. Two turns can then share a `receipt_id`, which matters for the slice-3 store keyed by it.
- **Deviation from the plan in replay**: `historyToReplay` sends the verdict text chunk after the carrier, not the carrier alone, on the D-AC-7 reasoning. Easy to remove.
- **Hidden existing file**: `review.before === null` on a file that exists gives `oldText: null`, which the specification reads as "new file". I add a text block saying the content is not shown.
- **Steering in replay** is sent as `user_message_chunk`.

**Where a client-controlled value reaches**
- A string request id of up to 256 characters is echoed in responses.
- `mcpServers[].name` (first 32, single-line, at most 100 characters) reaches `_meta` in the session result.
- `cwd` is passed to `realpath`, and the "does not exist" and "not a directory" errors differ, so it is an existence oracle for any path the user can stat.
- A `resource_link` URI is passed to `realpath`. The output differs only for paths inside the workspace; outside it gives the same `Reference:` line whether or not the path exists.
- The URI itself (at most 2,048 characters, one line) enters the prompt, so a client can point the model at any path or URL. Only the gates stand between that and a read.
- A diff `path` is resolved against the workspace from the model's argument and can name a path outside it. It is display only.
- `clientCapabilities` and every `_meta` are dropped by validation; the validated values carry none of them.
- Prompt text with a NUL is refused; that is my choice, not the plan's.

**Open questions**
- The `_nexus/` and `nexus/` prefixes versus reverse-DNS (owner decision).
- Whether the result types should become distinguishable in the schema.
- Whether the `eval_*` failure set of four on this host matches what you expect.</result>

## Findings from the reading that the plan or brief does not state
These apply to whoever writes slices 3 and 4.

1. **Verification-file protection asks by default.** With an operator present (the sidecar always passes `onConfirm`), `verificationEditDecision` returns `ask`, so allow-once would permit the edit. It only denies without asking when the workspace `.nexus-security.json` has `verificationPaths.mode: "block"` (`cli/src/security.ts:831-861`). The gate test needs that setup.
2. **Forbidden deletion emits no tool events.** It is refused before `onToolStart` (`dispatch_tool_call.ts:235-258`), so there is no `tool.start` or `tool.end`. The denial is only observable in the tool result of the model's next request (`gateway.requests`) and on the file system.
3. **A forbidden command rule does not set `denied` on the wire.** The sidecar sets it only when `operatorDeclined` is true, so the event is a plain failed `tool.end` with the rule text.
4. **Resume start-up order.** Events arrive as `hello`, `history`, `session.state`, `notice`. A failed resume has no `history` and a fresh session id; the reason is only in the notice text.
5. **`SidecarServer.start()` emits synchronously.** `hello` and `session.state` fire inside `start()`, and `tool.detail` and `receipt` are answered inside `handleLine`. An in-process link must queue and deliver asynchronously or the adapter is re-entered.
6. **Workspace path is redacted.** `session.state.workspace` passes `redactPathForOutput`, so the plan's realpath comparison on `session/load` can fail for a path with secret-looking components. It should be treated as a mismatch (-32602), not a crash.
7. **Posture has no extension count.** Plan 4.10 wants "count of extensions not loaded" in the posture; neither `session.state` nor `AcpPosture` carries it.
8. **Checkpoint cancel produces two messages.** Answering `checkpoint.choice` with `cancel` yields `error {id: turnId, INTERNAL, "This turn was cancelled before it started."}`; the cause is in the earlier `checkpoint.choice.request.message`, which also lists choices a client cannot make.
9. **Errors with the turn id are terminal.** Every sidecar `error` carrying the turn id ends the turn with no `turn.result` (BUSY, NO_PROVIDER, INTERNAL, BAD_REQUEST).
10. **The 32 in-flight limit looks unreachable.** With 4 sessions and one prompt each, and starting sessions counted against the 4, a 33rd in-flight request cannot occur. Testing it needs a constructor-injected lower limit.
11. **Turn cost.** A recorded turn through the real `SidecarServer` took about 1.5 to 2.6 s here (`protocol_wire.test.ts`, 8.9 s total). Twelve real turns under 20 s is tight; hostile cases that are not about the sidecar should use a scripted link.
12. **`sidecar_client.ts` imports.** `safe_text.ts` and any value import from `protocol.ts` pull in `ui.ts` (`ora`, `picocolors`). There is no commander, but "nothing that does I/O at import time" needs checking before PF-4 exports the module.
13. **Argument order for the child.** Commander lets the last repeated option win, so forwarded operator flags must come before the adapter's `-w`/`-r`. The link should itself refuse forwarded `-w`/`-r` and a resume id that starts with `-`.

## Decision for you
- **Continue here:** message me and I will write slices 3 and 4 in `ac1b` as briefed; everything I read is still in context.
- **Yield to Codex:** nothing to clean up in `ac1b`. The findings above are the only output.

Either way the takeover is unacknowledged in `nexus-claims.log`.</result>

<result>## 1. What I read

OpenCode `anomalyco/opencode@dev` at commit `bab0d0413ff31edd07b55fc499294e2e031c57d2` (committer date 2026-11-23T00:57:21Z), fetched live with `gh api`. I read all 19 workflow files, both composite actions under `.github/actions` (`setup-bun`, `prefetch-ghostty`), the two under `.github/release` (`upload`, `download`), `CODEOWNERS`, `TEAM_MEMBERS`, the PR template, both `nix-hashes` scripts, six `release/` files, `turbo.json`, root and `packages/cli` `package.json` scripts, and `packages/app/e2e/QUARANTINE.md`. The local 2026-10-31 clone was used only for `script/lint-changed.ts` and the workflow-name diff: since then `bot-docs-locale`, `deploy-changelog`, `deploy-posts`, `deploy-www` and `release-dry-run` were removed, and `check-docs` and `deploy-site` added. nexus-code was read from `origin/release/v1.3.0` at `96e96377`.

- **OpenCode:** tiered and speed-first. PRs run only affected unit tests on Linux plus typecheck; the full tier (Windows, compiled builds, Playwright) runs after merge, nightly and as a stable-release gate. Its engineering effort goes into a privilege-separated release and deploy pipeline.
- **nexus-code:** one monolithic gate. Every PR and every release-branch push runs the complete suite three times (Node 22, Node 24, and again inside the packaging job) with supply-chain gates. It is strict and reproducible but serial and slow.

Fetched copies are in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/43bcbb67-2cff-4fa8-a05e-f8aa598df73b/scratchpad/oc/.github`.

## 2. OpenCode workflows (all live API)

| File | Trigger | What it does | Notable technique |
|---|---|---|---|
| `check-test.yml` | push dev/beta, PR, dispatch, `workflow_call` | Unit tests, compiled-build smoke, Playwright suites | PR = Linux only, affected only; matrix built with `fromJSON`; Playwright `--shard=1/2` legs; aggregator job per OS; quarantine legs with `continue-on-error` |
| `check.yml` | push dev, PR | oxlint, `tsc`, lint of changed files | `fetch-depth: 2`, diff against `HEAD^1`; no `permissions` block |
| `check-process-daemon.yml` | PR/push with `paths:` | Rust: Miri, fmt, clippy, tests on Linux, macOS, Windows x64/arm64 | `Swatinem/rust-cache`; job timeouts 15/30/45; diagnostic rerun steps on failure |

| `check-bot-scope.yml` | PR, dispatch | Required check: bot branch may change only `nix/hashes.json` | Passes trivially on every other branch |
| `bot-nix-hashes.yml` | push dev with `paths:` | Recomputes Nix hashes, opens an auto-merging PR | Unprivileged compute, privileged publish that runs only `.github/nix-hashes/` |

| `deploy.yml`, `deploy-site.yml`, `deploy-files.yml`, `deploy-update.yml` | push dev/production | Cloudflare Worker deploys | Same build/deploy split; 15-minute minted tokens; `checkout@v3.6.0` |

Branch rules on `dev` (live API):
- Required checks are `unit (linux)`, `typecheck` and `bot-scope`.
- `strict_required_status_checks_policy` is false.
- Merges are squash-only.
- No workflow has a `merge_group` trigger, so there is no merge queue.

## 3. Recommendations, ranked by expected benefit

### 1. Fan the CLI suite out across jobs behind an aggregator (adapt)

- **OpenCode:** `check-test.yml:239-243` defines shard legs (`- id: app-1` / `shard: 1/2`). `:337-355` is an aggregator `e2e (${{ matrix.os }})` with `if: ${{ !cancelled() &amp;&amp; ...}}` that runs `test "$AFFECTED" = success &amp;&amp; test "$SUITES" = "$EXPECTED"`.
- **nexus today:**
  - `cli/scripts/test_shards.mjs:49` hard-caps shards (`count &gt; 2` throws).
  - `run_tests_driver.mjs:15` runs `concurrency: 1`, so two test processes share a 4-vCPU runner.
  - `ci.yml:103` says "Recent runs take about 32-34 minutes".
  - `cli/scripts/test_durations.json` holds 11 of 427 files, all unit-tier, so balancing falls back to source bytes for the rest.
- **Change:**
  - Lift the cap in `partitionTestFiles` and accept a shard index (`NEXUS_TEST_SHARD_OF=2/4`).
  - In `ci.yml`, add `test-shard` with `matrix: {node: [22, 24], shard: [1, 2, 3, 4]}`.
  - Turn `test-matrix` into an aggregator with the same `node` matrix, so the required names `test-matrix (22)` and `test-matrix (24)` survive.
  - Set `NEXUS_TEST_DURATIONS_OUT` per shard in CI and upload it. `run_tests.mjs:109-114` writes one path per process, so the two in-job shards currently overwrite each other.
- **Effect:** the suite step drops from about 33 minutes towards 9-10 with four legs. This is a linear estimate, not measured. It shortens both the PR run and the mandatory post-merge run, which is the "five PRs take hours" cost.
- **Cost and risk:**
  - Setup repeats per leg.
  - As in OpenCode, the aggregator sees one combined result, so a Node 22 failure also reddens `test-matrix (24)`.
  - Without `!cancelled()` the aggregator is skipped on failure, and a skipped required check counts as passing.
  - `release_workflow_parity.test.ts`, `check_requirements.test.ts:555`, `eval_integrity.test.ts:510` and `verify_action.test.ts:152` assert `ci.yml` text and must change with it.
- **Process conflict:** none; required checks stay meaningful.

### 2. Stop running the suite a third time in `linux-x86_64` (adapt)

- **OpenCode:** build jobs never re-run tests. `release.yml:171-204` builds the CLI once and later jobs download it (`:264-269`). In `check-test.yml`, `build` is separate from `unit`.
- **nexus today:** `ci.yml:179-190` repeats npm ci, build, lint, `cargo test`, the release build and the full suite on Node 22. That is exactly `test-matrix (22)` (`:71-105`), before packaging.
- **Change:** drop the suite and `cargo test` from `linux-x86_64`, keeping the packaging, reproducibility, SBOM and smoke steps.
- **Effect:** about a third fewer runner-minutes per run. It is also a prerequisite for item 1, because otherwise this job stays the 33-minute long pole.
- **Cost and risk:** `release_workflow_parity.test.ts` deliberately asserts that this job runs the suite, and `ci.yml:188` says "Keep release packaging parity". The parity that mattered was `release.yml` against `test-matrix`, which is unaffected. This is an owner decision.

### 3. Add a concurrency group to `ci.yml` (adopt)

- **OpenCode:** `check-test.yml:33` groups by PR number for pull requests and by `run_id` otherwise, with `cancel-in-progress: true`. The comment reads "every push and manual run keeps its own group, so each dev and beta commit gets a verdict".
- **nexus today:** `ci.yml` has no `concurrency` key; `codeql.yml:15-17` does.
- **Change:** copy the idiom so PR runs cancel and release-branch pushes never do.
- **Effect:** each superseded PR push stops burning three jobs of about 35-45 minutes.
- **Risk:** negligible. `case()` is a newer expression function; the `&amp;&amp;`/`||` form works too.

### 4. Point scheduled suites at the release branch (adopt)

- **OpenCode:** `nightly-beta.yml:3` says "GitHub runs schedules only from the default branch", and `:22` runs `gh workflow run check-test.yml --repo "$GITHUB_REPOSITORY" --ref beta`.
- **nexus today:** `real-project-smoke.yml` (nightly) and `lsp-real-servers.yml` (weekly) check out with no `ref`, so on schedule they test `main`. Development is on `release/v1.3.0`, so the alarms watch frozen code for the whole cycle.
- **Change:** a small dispatcher on `main` with `permissions: actions: write` that resolves the newest `release/v*` branch and dispatches both workflows with `--ref`.
- **Effect:** smoke and LSP regressions surface during the cycle instead of after the merge to `main`.
- **Process conflict:** it needs a workflow-only change on `main` mid-cycle and an `actions: write` token. Both are owner calls.
- **Related, not from OpenCode:** it has no Dependabot or Renovate config. Your Dependabot pain is the same default-branch rule, and the fix is `target-branch:` in the `main` copy of `dependabot.yml`. That copy also still lists `/demo` and `experiments/*`.

### 5. Cache Rust builds, restore on PRs and save only on pushes (adapt)

- **OpenCode:** `check-process-daemon.yml:68` uses `Swatinem/rust-cache@6323deb...`, with the note at `:180` "No cache-on-failure: a timed-out run once saved a partial cache that broke re-runs". `setup-bun/action.yml:74` saves only when the event is not a pull request.
- **nexus today:** each job runs clippy, `cargo test` and `cargo build --release` cold (`ci.yml:76-78`, `:184-186`, `:227`). Only `~/.cargo/bin` is cached.
- **Change:** add a pinned rust-cache step for `core -&gt; target` in the test jobs, saving only on push.
- **Effect:** removes most Rust compile time from every job; I could not size it from the files.
- **Risk:** the packaging build runs in the pinned container with its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (`package-linux.sh:45-55`), so reproducibility evidence is untouched. It is one more third-party action to pin.

### 6. Diff against the merge commit's first parent (adopt)

- **OpenCode:** `check.yml:41-43` runs `bun run lint:changed HEAD^1`, "so base-branch commits the pull request has not merged are not counted as its changes (the event's base SHA can predate them)". `check-test.yml:46` does the same.
- **nexus today:** `ci.yml:75`, `:183` and `:232` use `github.event.pull_request.base.sha`.
- **Change:** on pull requests, resolve `HEAD^1` for `NEXUS_FMT_BASE` and diff coverage.
- **Effect:** removes the wrong-base case for the rustfmt gate and diff coverage. It does not fix a stale merge commit, and OpenCode has nothing for that.

### 7. Split `release.yml` into an unprivileged build and a privileged publish (adapt)

- **OpenCode:** `release.yml:5-7` says build jobs "run repository code ... with no environment, secrets or id-token", and sign and publish jobs "check out only .github/release/ ... They never execute repository code". The `upload` and `download` actions tie artifacts together by a SHA256SUMS digest.
- **nexus today:** `release.yml:7-18` gives one job `contents: write`, `id-token: write` and `attestations: write` while it runs `npm ci`, the suite and cargo builds.
- **Change:** a `package` job with `contents: read` uploads `dist` and outputs a digest. A `publish` job verifies the digest, attests and creates the draft or prerelease.
- **Effect:** dependency install scripts and test code no longer run beside a write token and OIDC.
- **Process conflict:** none. The owner still pushes the tag, and stable still stays a draft.

### 8. Smaller hygiene items (adopt)

- Extract the evaluator setup into a composite action. It is copied at `ci.yml:20-59`, `:112-151` and `release.yml:22-83`, which is the drift the rc.12 parity test exists to catch. The parity test greps workflow text and would need to read the action file.
- Add job-level `timeout-minutes`. `ci.yml` only bounds the suite step, so a hung `cargo test` or packaging step can run for six hours.
- Add `persist-credentials: false` to the three checkouts in `ci.yml`.
- Fix `release.yml:105`, which says "12-20 minutes" where `ci.yml:103` says 32-34.

### 9. Quarantine lane (adapt, owner decision)

- **OpenCode:** `QUARANTINE.md` requires "fix a quarantined test or delete it within 14 days". The legs use `continue-on-error: ${{ matrix.quarantine }}` (`check-test.yml:227`) and run nightly.
- **nexus today:** `flake-report.yml` detects flakes but there is nowhere to park one.
- **Assessment:** a documented list run in a non-required leg would stop one known Node 22 ordering race from blocking every merge. It weakens the required checks by definition, so adopt it only with the expiry rule.

### 10. Skip heavy jobs for docs-only changes (adapt, low confidence)

- **OpenCode:** the `affected` job plus an aggregator that expects `skipped` (`check-test.yml:352`).
- **nexus today:** 65 test files under `cli/test` reference `docs/`, `.github/`, `README.md` or `AGENTS.md`, and the doc gates at `ci.yml:60-63` read docs.
- **Assessment:** a skip is only safe for an inventoried subtree that no test reads. Do that inventory first.

### Not applicable, or worse than what nexus has

- **Affected-only tests** (`turbo test --affected`, `check-test.yml:126`): package-graph selection in a monorepo. nexus's CLI is one package, and your recorded failures are cross-cutting ones this would hide.
- **Deferring matrix legs to post-merge:** OpenCode accepts a red `dev` (`check-test.yml:231-232`: Windows legs "still fail on dev pushes often enough"). The jobs run in parallel, so it would not save wall time either.
- **Non-strict checks with no post-merge gate:** this contradicts your "green PR is not a green main" rule.
- **Blacksmith runners:** the PR tier uses 4-vCPU machines, so there is no core gain, and it would change the runner images behind your reproducibility evidence.
- **Merge queue:** OpenCode does not use one. I believe GitHub does not offer it on user-owned repositories; I did not check this.
- **Triage and stale bots, scheduled auto-publish, bot auto-merge:** irrelevant to a single-owner repo, or contrary to release authority.

**Where nexus is already stronger:**
- Every action is SHA-pinned on current majors, and every workflow has a top-level `permissions` block.
- It has CodeQL, `cargo deny`, SBOM validation, byte-reproducibility checks and the cross-runner `repro.yml`.
- It gates on deprecated lockfile entries, tag ancestry and version-only stable diffs.
- It has a flake budget, a per-file watchdog with restart, and a Node matrix on every PR.

OpenCode has none of these in `.github`.

## 4. Risky to copy

- **`pull_request_target` with a write token:** `triage-pr-standards.yml:4` and `triage-contributor.yml:4`. Safe there only because nothing is checked out.
- **Unpinned tooling under a write token:** `bun-version: latest` at `triage-close-issues.yml:19` and `triage-close-prs.yml:31`; `ubuntu-latest` in all five triage workflows and `nightly-beta.yml:16`.
- **Dispatch inputs interpolated into shell:** `triage-close-prs.yml:37`, `:44`, `:46`.
- **No `permissions` block:** `check.yml`, and no top-level block in four of the five triage workflows.
- **Workflow-wide `checks: write` on PR runs:** `check-test.yml:36-38`, with credentials persisted in jobs that run repository code (`:59-62`).
- **Deprecated action:** `actions/checkout@f43a0e5... # v3.6.0` in all four deploy workflows (for example `deploy.yml:37`). `check-test.yml:41` carries the `FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24` workaround. Both conflict with your no-deprecated rule.
- **Automation that pushes and merges:** `nix-hashes/publish.sh:50` force-pushes and `:64` runs `gh pr merge --auto --squash`, under `contents`, `pull-requests` and `actions` write (`bot-nix-hashes.yml:168-171`).

## Findings, most severe first

**1. F1 is still bypassable when something other than the test swaps the file — CONFIRMED**
- **Where:** `cli/src/core/spine_handlers.ts:234-242`.
- **Defect:** the fix compares a digest taken before the verifier with one taken after it, so any swap that is put back before the second digest goes unseen.
- **Scenario:** the model's `run_command` starts a detached process (`setsid nohup bash … &amp;`) with its files outside the workspace. The process waits for `node --test`, then copies a weakened test over `test/math.test.js`. That weakened test restores the base bytes when it loads. The deliverable was still `a - b`.
- **Result at head:** `outcome=VERIFIED`, `independent=true`, `definitionBound` unset, detail "tests that failed before the change pass after it". The merge-base gives the same result, so this is a residual hole and not a regression.
- **Smallest fix:** treat the pass as unbound when `this.turnBackgroundCommands &gt; 0`. That counter is already kept (`dispatch_tool_call.ts:639`) and is not consulted here. It is a mitigation only: it resets each turn and relies on a regex.
- **Real fix:** judge the pass on a private copy, as `nexus verify` already does with `headPristine`.
- **If neither lands:** reword the PR and `docs/evidence/REVIEW_FIXES_02.md` from "closes F1" to "closes the in-test self-restore variant".

**2. The hashed set is not "all workspace test files" — CONFIRMED by script**
- **Where:** `snapshotTests` / `workspaceTestFiles` (`cli/src/tester.ts:955-988`), used by the new `testSnapshotDigest` (`spine_handlers.ts:43-55`).
- **Defect:** discovery silently truncates and skips, and the digest inherits that.
- **Not hashed:**
  - a test below directory depth 6;
  - anything past the first 2000 matching files;
  - a symlinked `tests` directory;
  - root `conftest.py`, `jest.config.js`, `src/__snapshots__/*.snap`, and fixtures outside `test/`, `tests/`, `__tests__/`, `spec/`.
- **Symlinks:** a symlinked test file is read through to its target, including outside the workspace; only the digest keeps it, nothing is printed.
- **Smallest fix:** land #732 first. Its `discoverWorkspaceTestFiles().complete` makes `snapshotTests` return null on incomplete discovery, and this PR then fails closed for depth, cap and symlinks.
- **Not covered by either PR:** non-test-path files. Those rely on the existing verifier-definition snapshot (`package.json`, `Makefile`, pytest configs, referenced scripts).

**3. A snapshot that cannot be taken reports the wrong reason — CONFIRMED**
- **Where:** `spine_handlers.ts:242-245`.
- **Defect:** a null snapshot is folded into "test files changed while the verifier ran (including a test that restored itself before exit)".
- **Scenario:** an honest fix in a workspace holding a 300 KiB file under `test/` gets `REGRESSION_FREE` with that detail, though nothing changed. The merge-base gives the same outcome with a different detail, so the outcome level does not regress.
- **This repository hits it:** at the repository root, `docs/reviews/evidence/nexus_maturity_2026-10-11_verification/final_make_test.log` matches the test-path pattern and exceeds 256 KiB. Every turn run there will carry this message. `cli/` alone is fine (485 files, 6.9 MB, about 50 ms per snapshot).
- **Smallest fix:** give the null case its own detail, such as "the test files could not be snapshotted (too large or unreadable)".

**4. The `REVIEW_REJECTED` branch is untested and looks unreachable — CONFIRMED untested, PLAUSIBLE unreachable**
- **Where:** `cli/src/core/verify.ts:495-498`.
- **Untested:** I deleted the branch in my worktree; `verify_review` 7/7, `verify_schema` 4/4 and `verify_command` 18/18 still pass. The only test naming the reason (`verify_review.test.ts:103-107`) asserts the constants table.
- **Unreachable:** the reviewer re-reads fields the same function just derived. A weakening always sets `requiresApproval`, which already yields `REGRESSION_FREE` before this branch.
- **One way in:** a declared deliverable whose path the guard classes as a test file (for example `src/spec/x.js`) would now be `BLOCKED` where it may have been `VERIFIED`. I did not run that case.
- **Smallest fix:** add one `verifyChange`-level test that produces `REVIEW_REJECTED`, or drop the branch and the reason until a reviewer with independent input exists.

**5. Review `ACCEPT` is printed on results where nothing ran — PLAUSIBLE from reading**
- **Where:** `verify.ts:691`, `:710`, and the text row at `:982-986`.
- **Defect:** `conclude()` runs the reviewer on the no-verifier and not-approved exits, so the output shows `review ACCEPT: 0 suspected defect(s)…` beside `UNVERIFIED` or `BLOCKED`.
- **Also:** the comment at `verify.ts:454-455` says it runs "after the guard and the verifier have written their receipts", which is not true on those paths.
- **Smallest fix:** leave `NOT_RUN` when there is no head run, or rename the row to say what it is, an evidence-consistency check.

**6. Schema v3 publishes members `nexus verify` never writes — CONFIRMED**
- **Where:** `cli/src/verify_contract.ts:215-217`; `docs/protocol/verify-result-v3.schema.json:781,785`.
- **Defect:** reusing `IndependentReviewVerdict` pulls `boundaryReport`, `changeAssessment` and `suspectedDefects[].line` into the public contract. Any later change to those internal types forces a verify-result ledger entry.
- **Smallest fix:** `Pick` the five members the reviewer emits.

**7. Downstream messages name the wrong cause — PLAUSIBLE from reading**
- **Where:** `cli/src/core/operation_runner.ts:553-554`, `operation_runner_execution.ts:772`.
- **Defect:** `definitionBound: false` now also means "tests mutated", but these strings still say the verifier's definition could not be bound.
- **Smallest fix:** print `independenceDetail` there.

**8. Slop**
- `testSnapshotDigest` duplicates `testsChangedSince(workspace, before)`, which already compares two snapshots.
- Its `file\0content\0` framing is ambiguous, and content is decoded as UTF-8, so some binary changes collide.
- `ReviewEvidence` picks `verifier` and never reads it (`verify_review.ts:14-17`).
- Two tests in `verify_review.test.ts` (lines 98-107) assert constants only.
- `convergence_scheduling.test.ts` now has two tests numbered 27.
- The new `hash_chain` CLI test passes `process.env` through instead of the throwaway-HOME helper that `runAudit` uses.

- **F4:** see findings 4 to 6.
- **F5:** defect is that a cancelled unit went back to RUNNABLE and was resubmitted without limit. Fixed for `apply("cancelled")`; with the hunk reverted test 26 fails after about 20 s of resubmission. `scheduler.cancel()` at line 693 still sets RUNNING units to RUNNABLE, and `operation resume` restarts a CANCELLED operation (`operation_cli.ts:368`). <summary>Agent "Review PR #732 verify fixtures" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**REQUEST CHANGES** — PR #732 at head `9580dab0a6ccb75bfe2ae00445b825694129023b` (base `release/v1.3.0` at `ab7af749`).

The PR catches a direct read of a baseline test file inside the scratch tree, but hole (b) stays open by three routes I ran to `VERIFIED`. It also takes `VERIFIED` away from four ordinary honest fix shapes, and `verify_hostile` now runs past the 300 s per-file watchdog on this host.

## How it works

- **Detection:** each verifier run is wrapped in `/usr/bin/strace -f -yy -e trace=%file,getdents,getdents64` outside Bubblewrap (`cli/src/core/verify.ts:118-133`, `906-930`). The log is reduced to per-path observation counts, keeping only paths under the run's own scratch tree (`:245`).
- **What is withheld:** `independent` becomes false, so the verdict is `REGRESSION_FREE` (exit 2), when any of three rules fires:
  - the head run observes an "oracle" file more often than the base run (`:316-322`);
  - the base run opened any non-test, non-product workspace file and test-side source matches a broad regex (`:1031`, `:1061-1066`);
  - a "candidate-only" path was observed, but only if a changed JS/TS product file matches a static regex (`:1038-1047`, `:1155`).
- **No trace:** Docker, missing strace, or an unreadable trace caps the verdict at `REGRESSION_FREE`. `--sandbox none` skips the check (`:1134`).

## Findings, most severe first

1. **`verify.ts:245` with `sandbox.ts:772-787` — reads outside the scratch tree are dropped, and the caller's checkout is visible in the box when it is outside `$HOME` and `/tmp`.** CONFIRMED.
   - Scenario: with the repository under `/var/tmp`, product code that read `&lt;checkout&gt;/test/math.test.mjs` by absolute path got `VERIFIED`. So did one that ran `git --git-dir &lt;checkout&gt;/.git show HEAD:test/math.test.mjs`.
   - This is the shape of CI runners that check out to `/builds` or `/workspace`.
   - Smallest fix: in `sandboxedVerifierCommand`, cover the caller's worktree and git common dir with `--tmpfs` before binding back the linked dependency directories.

2. **`verify.ts:221-250` — the parser ignores `link`/`rename`, so a hardlink reads the baseline test file itself.** CONFIRMED.
   - Scenario: the head hardlinks `test/math.test.mjs` to `src/.cache` and reads the link; result `VERIFIED`, no `testFixtureReads`.
   - The trace reports the new name, which is not in the oracle set; the candidate-only rule is skipped via finding 3.
   - Smallest fix: treat `link`, `linkat`, `rename*` and `symlink*` whose source is an oracle file as an oracle observation.

3. **`verify.ts:1038-1047` — the candidate-only rule is gated on a static regex over changed JS/TS files.** CONFIRMED.
   - `process.getBuiltinModule("f"+"s")` listing `test/` and deriving the answer from a filename got `VERIFIED`. This is the attack the PR's own "derives the expected value from test filenames" test claims to catch.
   - `import { readFileSync as r } from "node:\u0066s"` reading `fixtures/expected.json` got `VERIFIED`.
   - Changed `.py`, `.go`, `.rs` and `.jsx` product files are never scanned, so the rule never applies to them (from reading, not run).
   - Smallest fix: drop the gate and attribute by pid, or keep the rule unconditional and whitelist runner noise.

4. **`verify.ts:1031`, `1050-1067`, `1142-1146` — ordinary honest fixes lose `VERIFIED`.** CONFIRMED; all four cases below are `VERIFIED` at base and `REGRESSION_FREE` at head.
   - A test file that imports `node:fs` without reading anything, or reads its own fixture. The detail blames `.npmrc, node_modules/.bin/node, package.json, src/package.json`.
   - A test that only calls `/re/.exec(...)` trips the same rule at head (not run at base).
   - A fix that adds `import … from "./util.js"` for an existing module. One extra `src/package.json` lookup is reported as an oracle read.
   - A fix that reads its own data file `src/table.json`.
   - The regex matches `exec(`, `open(`, `import(` and `spawn` in any test source, and npm/Node housekeeping files count as "oracle" because the product graph comes only from `package.json` entry points.
   - Smallest fix: exclude runner-owned paths and failed lookups from the oracle set, and narrow the test-side regex.

5. **`cli/test/verify_hostile.test.ts` — wall time 543 s at head against 153 s at base.** CONFIRMED on this host, which was loaded (load average about 18).
   - The 2,000-file test alone took 232 s here; the PR description says 223.7 s. Its own timeout is 300 s.
   - `scripts/run_tests_driver.mjs` kills a file at 300 s. Hosted checks pass; I did not read the CI logs, so I cannot say whether the traced path runs there.
   - Smallest fix: cover the bound with the existing unit test in `independent_verification`, or move the end-to-end case to its own file with a small injected limit.

6. **`tester.ts:1006-1029` — any directory symlink in the workspace marks discovery incomplete.** CONFIRMED for `nexus verify`: a `documentation -&gt; docs` link is `VERIFIED` at base, `REGRESSION_FREE` at head.
   - The same functions feed ordinary agent turns (`completion_oracle.ts:207`, `agent.ts:633`), so those turns lose `VERIFIED` too; that is from reading, and no turn-level test covers it.
   - `agent.ts:634` and `independent_check.ts` (in `judgeWithoutCopy`) still say "too large" for what is now a symlink or depth cause.
   - Running discovery on this repository's own root returns `complete=false`, because nine tracked test-path files sit seven or more directories deep.
   - Deep tests were already unbindable at base under a separate depth-5 rule, so depth is not a new loss for verify.

7. **`verify.ts:227` — unrecognised procfs aliases.** CONFIRMED: `statSync("/proc/self/task/&lt;pid&gt;/cwd/test/oracle-N.test.mjs")` as a filename-existence probe got `VERIFIED`. Content reads through any alias are still caught by the returned-descriptor annotation.

8. **`verify.ts:251-266` — dead code.** CONFIRMED: the strace filter never emits `clone`, `fork` or `fchdir`, so those branches never run and child working directories are not tracked.

9. **`verify.ts:1064` — test files are excluded from the masking guard.** PLAUSIBLE: a runner that re-reads the test source on failure (jest code frames, pytest tracebacks) would give the base one extra open that hides one candidate read. Node's own runner did not do this (7 observations either way), and my simulation was stopped by rule 4.

10. **`verify.ts:906-923` — no probe that strace can actually trace Bubblewrap.** PLAUSIBLE: with ptrace denied or a setuid `bwrap`, every run would fail and be reported as a test failure instead of "trace unavailable". The trace log is also unbounded and read whole into memory.

11. **Slop.**
    - `workspaceTestFiles` (`tester.ts:1052`) has no callers left.
    - The first new ledger entry (`04cd4b…`) hashes a schema that never existed on the release branch.
    - The tests detect strace through `PATH` while the code uses fixed paths.
    - When no sandbox is usable the new tests return after one `notEqual`.

## Attacks that did not work

- `/proc/self/cwd` and `/proc/self/root` paths, a symlink to the test file, and a child process reading it: all caught.
- A child Node with `UV_USE_IO_URING=1` reading the test file: caught (one variant tried).
- Scratch siblings (`base`, `head-pristine`, scratch objects, the trace log): hidden by the tmpfs over the scratch directory (from reading; reading a previous step's verifier output from inside the box was not attempted).
- A head that adds a directory symlink to push discovery over the bound: `REGRESSION_FREE`, not favourable.
- Hard-coded expected values are undetectable by design; the old "limit" row in the docs was removed and should come back.

## Verdict honesty

- Every new path lowers the verdict and gives a reason in `independenceDetail`. No path returns `VERIFIED` with less evidence than before.
- Exit codes are unchanged. The schema gains optional `runs[].testFixtureReads` at version 2, which the stated policy allows, and the ledger hash matches the file (`cc9dd93b…`).
- There is no new reason code, so a consumer has to parse the detail text.
- Docker-only and non-Linux hosts can now reach `VERIFIED` only with `--sandbox none`, the less safe mode. This is documented, but it is your call whether to ship that incentive.
- Each verify run took about 10–14 s at head against about 3 s at base in my experiments.

## Conflicts

- **Current `release/v1.3.0` (`ab7af749`):** clean merge; `tsc --noEmit` passes and `verify_schema` is 4/4 on the trial merge.
- **#737 (`65e7a1a9`):** textual conflict in `docs/protocol/VERIFY_RESULT_VERSIONS.json`, in either merge order. There is also a semantic one: #737 moves to `VERIFY_RESULT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 3` with a generated `verify-result-v3.schema.json`, while this PR adds its field to the v2 schema and ledger. Whichever lands second must regenerate v3 and its hash.

<summary>Agent "CI speedup shard fan-out" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The fan-out is built and uncommitted in `/home/mike/nexus-wt/ci1`. Local checks pass; nothing has run in CI, so the job graph, check names and timings are unconfirmed.

## How the suite runs
- **Before:** `ci.yml:105` ran `npm --prefix cli test` (`test:cli &amp;&amp; test:scope`) inside each `test-matrix` job. `test_shards.mjs:110-131` split the files into two shards and ran them concurrently (`Promise.all`), each a `run_tests.mjs` process running files one at a time (`run_tests_driver.mjs:15`, `concurrency: 1`) under the 300 s per-file watchdog.
- **After:** each `test-shard` leg runs `npm --prefix cli run test:cli -- --shard=K/4`. The leg selects its quarter of the files, then runs it in the same two concurrent processes with the same watchdog. With no arguments, `npm test` and `make test` behave as before.
- **Fixtures:** the three shared-fixture evaluator files stay pinned to shard 1.

## Job graph

| Job | needs / if | Timeout | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test-gates` (node 22, 24) | none | 40 min | full evaluator setup, version/doc/requirements/invariants/deprecated-lock gates, `npm ci`, build, `make lint`, `cargo test`, `test:scope` |
| `test-shard` (node 22, 24 × shard 1–4, `fail-fast: false`) | none | job 50, suite step 30 | full evaluator setup, `npm ci`, build, release core build, cargo tools, one shard; uploads artifact `test-shard-node&lt;N&gt;-&lt;K&gt;of4` (durations + `suite.log`) with `if: always()` |
| `test-matrix` (node 22, 24) | `needs: [test-gates, test-shard]`, `if: always()` | 5 min | fails unless both results are `success` |
| `linux-x86_64` | none | 120 min | as before |
| `diff-coverage` | none | 30 min | as before |

- **Non-suite gates:** they run once per Node version in `test-gates`, because I could not show that lint, `cargo test` or `test:scope` are independent of the Node version.
- **Release build:** `test-gates` does not build the release core; every leg does.
- **`linux-x86_64` and `release.yml`:** `release.yml` is untouched. `linux-x86_64` got only the hygiene items; the base step adds one new env var, `NEXUS_CHANGED_BASE`, which its suite step now sees.

## Decisions for you
- **`always()` instead of `!cancelled()` on the aggregator.** A job skipped by a false `if` reports as passing, so a manual cancel at the PR head could satisfy the required check. With `always()` it runs and fails. Say if you want `!cancelled()`; the parity test pins the current choice.
- **Each `test-matrix` check also fails when a leg of the other Node version fails.** `needs.&lt;matrix job&gt;.result` is one aggregate across all legs. This is stricter, never weaker, and both checks are required anyway. Per-node precision would need the API or marker artifacts.

## Expected wall time
- **Per required check:** leg setup plus about 8–9 minutes of suite, instead of setup plus gates plus 32–34 minutes.
- **Basis:** the 32–34 minute comment in `ci.yml`, divided by four at unchanged in-runner concurrency, with equal size-weighted shards.
- **Not measured:** leg setup time (apt, toolchains, `npm ci`, cold release build) and current job durations. All job timeouts are therefore guesses with headroom.
- **Why four shards:** it puts 12 jobs in flight per run; eight shards would put 20.
- **Balance:** the durations manifest is unchanged (11 measured files of 427), so the first CI runs are balanced by file size.

## Aggregator cases (walked by hand)
1. **All legs pass:** both results `success`, check passes.
2. **One leg fails:** `test-shard` result is `failure`; both `test-matrix` checks fail.
3. **Leg hits a timeout:** the 30-minute step timeout fails the leg; the 50-minute job timeout also yields a non-success result. Check fails.
4. **Leg skipped:** result `skipped`, check fails. Legs and gates have no `if`, and the parity test enforces that.
5. **Run cancelled by the concurrency group:** results are `cancelled`; the aggregator still runs and fails, on the superseded SHA only.
6. **Leg setup fails before tests:** same as case 2; the artifact upload finds nothing and is ignored.

## Workflow-text tests changed
- **`release_workflow_parity.test.ts`:**
  - The evaluator-setup-in-order-before-the-suite assertion now targets `test-shard` instead of `test-matrix`; `linux-x86_64` and release `package` are unchanged at exactly 45 minutes.
  - The shard step must have a step timeout of at most 45.
  - New: `npm test` is exactly `test:cli &amp;&amp; test:scope`; the shard matrix is exactly 1..N of `--shard=K/N`; both jobs cover Node 22 and 24; `pipefail` guards the `tee`; `test-gates` carries every former gate plus `test:scope`.
  - New: the aggregator's `needs`, `always()` and result checks, and the absence of a `name:`.
  - New: the concurrency block, a timeout on every job, and `persist-credentials: false` on every checkout.
  - Seven deliberate breakages of `ci.yml` each failed a test.
- **`check_requirements.test.ts`:** the `--enforce` gate is now looked up in `test-gates`. The test additionally requires it to precede `npm ci`, the build and cargo, and requires `test-matrix` to need `test-gates`.
- **`eval_integrity.test.ts`:** the Go pin and toolchain installer counts go from 2 to 3, because three jobs now set up the evaluator environment; all must still be the exact version.

## Shard scripts
- **Selection:** `--shard=K/N` supports N up to 16. A malformed spec, an out-of-range index, an unknown flag or an empty selection fails with exit 2 or 1.
- **Durations bug fixed:** the two concurrent processes used to overwrite the same `NEXUS_TEST_DURATIONS_OUT` file. Each now writes a part and the parent merges them; tests no longer inherit the variable.
- **Merge script:** `cli/scripts/merge_test_durations.mjs` and `make test-durations-merge DIR=&lt;downloaded artifacts&gt;` rebuild `test_durations.json`. It takes the largest measurement per file, drops entries for files that no longer exist, and fails on malformed input.

## Hygiene
All four items are done; none left out.
- **Concurrency:** pull-request runs share a group per PR number and cancel in progress; every other run is alone in a group named after its run id.
- **Timeouts:** on every job, as in the table.
- **`persist-credentials: false`:** on all five checkouts; I found no later step that needs the token.
- **Changed-file base:** on pull requests it is now `HEAD^1` when the checkout is a merge commit, in `test-gates`, `linux-x86_64` and `diff-coverage`. Both `check_fmt.sh` and `diff_coverage.mjs` use `base...HEAD` with full history, so the first parent gives exactly the PR's changes.

- **Type check and lint:** `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `actionlint` exit 0; YAML parses.
- **Three runs each on Node 24, all passing:** `test_shards` 9/9, `merge_test_durations` 2/2, `release_workflow_parity` 4/4, `check_requirements` 28/28, `verify_action` 9/9, `node_floor` 6/6, `flake_report` 8/8.
- **`eval_integrity`:** 26 pass, 7 fail both before and after, with identical failing sets (host fixtures). The changed assertions sit before the failure point in their test.
- **Partition on the real list (427 files):** N=4 gives 108/105/107/107 files at 25.0% weight each; N=8 gives 52–55 files at 12.5% each. Union is complete and shards are disjoint for both.
- **Dry run of shard 8/8:** 53 files in 463 s, merged durations written. It exited 1 with 16 failures, all from the unbuilt `cli/dist`; after `npm run build` the three affected files passed through the same runner.
- **Gates:** `check_test_leaks` exit 0; `check_invariants` exit 0; `check_requirements --enforce` exit 1 with only the expected clean-worktree complaint.
- **Not done:** no Node 22 run (host has only 24 and 20), which AGENTS.md asks for on test-runner changes.

<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS** at `67f5a5379346286f08154e24656c1ec071f571df`. This is the current head and it did not move. I reviewed a throwaway worktree at `~/nexus-wt/rev745`, which is not clean: the test mutation described below was reverted with `git checkout`, and nothing was committed or pushed.

The authority boundary holds: no path from a denial to VERIFIED, and the verify-side denials are real. The dispatch-side tool classification is weak, and the live receipt projection is not wired. Neither weakens an outcome, so I would not block on them. I would fix the classifier before SB-1 builds on it.

**Blocking:** none.

**Non-blocking, ranked**

1. **Tool-side denial detection parses error text that the model and workspace influence** (`cli/src/sandbox_events.ts:57-74`, called from `cli/src/core/dispatch_tool_call.ts:875`).
   - `sandboxDenialFromError` regex-matches `receipt.error` for `sandbox|docker|path|network|not found…`. That text carries model-supplied arguments and filenames.
   - I ran it. `ENOENT … '/home/u/ws/Dockerfile'` produces `denied/policy`, and `old_string not found in docker-compose.yml` produces `unavailable/engine`, both on `edit_file`. `Unknown tool 'sandbox_helper'` is also recorded as a denial.
   - So a model can mint chained `sandbox_*` flight records, and a real denial can be mislabelled. The regexes are inconsistent: `path` makes a filesystem violation, `escaped` makes a violation.
   - This is a forgery and mislabelling risk for audit data, not a verdict risk. Events carry no verdict and the error text is not output the command controls.
   - Fix: have the sandbox layer set a structured `denialReason: "sandbox"` with a category on the tool result. `spine.ts` already has a `denialReason` field on the result.
   - Confirmed by running.

2. **The tool-receipt event is effectively non-bounded and non-deduplicated.** Each dispatch writes one event, with `reason` capped at 512 characters (`sandbox_events.ts:34`). The count is bounded by the number of calls, so there is no per-run cap. Reasons embed raw error text, including absolute paths such as the `/home/u/ws/…` one in the run above. Receipts therefore leak host paths. The verify-side reasons are fixed strings, so they are fine. Fix: cap the events per receipt, and replace the error text with a fixed reason per category.

3. **`receiptSections` is dead wiring.** The new `sandboxEvents` parameter is never passed. The only caller, `cli/src/sidecar.ts:1105`, passes three arguments. The "Sandbox denials" section in the live receipt projection can't render, and PR text that implies otherwise overstates it. The tests only call the function directly, so they would not notice. Fix: pass `lastTurn` events through, or drop the claim.

4. **Tests do not cover the dispatch path.** I removed the `receipt.sandboxEvents` assignment and the `recordSandboxDenial` call in `dispatch_tool_call.ts`. All 32 tests in `sandbox_denial_events`, `audit_export` and `verify_sandbox` still passed. Only the classifier and recorder are tested in isolation, not the receipt and flight wiring. Fix: one dispatch-level test with a forced confinement error.

5. **The v2 schema file was edited.** `docs/protocol/verify-result-v2.schema.json` gained 68 lines, the same as v3, and `VERIFY_RESULT_VERSIONS.json` only records the v3 entry. Adding an optional member is additive, but v2 is described as frozen. Please confirm that is intended.

**Your questions**
- **Q1:** `verify` events are built from the real engine-selection and `sandboxedVerifierCommand` unavailable branches (`cli/src/core/verify.ts:920-933, 1075-1085`). These are code-controlled and trustworthy. The tool side is finding 1. Suppression is possible only by omission. Non-confinement errors never produce events, but `Stop` still gives BLOCKED.
- **Q2:** no weakening found. `SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` still throws `Stop`, and the events are only added before the throw.
- **Q3:** the verify events have bounded text. See finding 2 for the tool side. No environment dumps found.
- **Q4:** see finding 4.
- **Q5:** the new `sandboxEvents` member is optional, `schema_version` stays 3 and the ledger entry was added. The flight types are new strings. I did not check whether older flight readers tolerate unknown event types.
- **Q6:** no conflict with #737 or #738 seen. I did not diff `policy_runtime.ts` closely.

I did not run `verify_hostile`, `verify_command` or the base-versus-head failing-set comparison. `verify_sandbox` timed out at 120s on an earlier run, and a later run completed.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review PR #741 ACP adapter" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**REQUEST CHANGES** at head `0c2e33cec87d123f66ff09c1c6b3ff5685595d4d`.

The client-side authority claim held in every experiment I ran: no client value produced an approval, a mode change or trust. The blockers are operator-side and lifecycle defects. My processes are stopped by pid and the worktree is removed.

## Blocking

1. **Auto mode is not refused** (`cli/src/index.ts:1503-1509`). CONFIRMED: `nexus --yes acp`, `--mode auto` and `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE=auto` each wrote a file with zero `session/request_permission`; a shell command also ran under the last two. The plan (D-AC-3) refuses these at start-up. Fix: refuse them in the `acp` action.
2. **SIGTERM and SIGINT no longer end the process** (`cli/src/acp_stdio.ts:374-375`). CONFIRMED: idle and mid-turn, the adapter was still alive 12-20 s after the signal and exited only when stdin closed; the children were killed. Fix: `process.exit` after `close()`.
3. **`checkpoint.choice.request` is never answered** (`acp_stdio.ts:289-312`). CONFIRMED: with a private key file in the workspace, the prompt got no update and no reply for 60 s, until `session/cancel`. It never picks a permissive option, but the plan says answer `cancel` with -32603.
4. **Stale approval after `$/cancel_request`** (`acp_stdio.ts:168-175`, `331-332`). CONFIRMED: cancel a prompt with a permission pending, and the next prompt's write is denied with no request sent to the client. Fix: clear `pendingApproval` in that path.

## Non-blocking

5. **No backpressure** (`acp_stdio.ts:59`, `371`). CONFIRMED: with stdout unread, RSS went from 130 MB to 582 MB in 20 s.
6. **After three oversized lines the process stays alive and silent** (`acp_stdio.ts:69`, `75-85`). CONFIRMED.
7. **Sessions are never freed.** CONFIRMED: the 5th session is refused for the life of the connection; `session/close` is -32601.
8. **`always: false` is unguarded by the adapter test.** CONFIRMED: mutating it to `true` at `acp_stdio.ts:109` left the three fake-sidecar tests green. Forcing every matched reply to approve, and removing the session cap, each failed a test.
9. **`acp_stdio.test.ts:13` uses a 10 s deadline for real turns.** CONFIRMED: both real-turn tests failed in 2 of 3 local runs (14-40 s wall) and passed once. CI is green, and an existing sidecar test is equally slow here, so this is host latency exposing a tight deadline.
10. **Docs.** Reading only: the help text and `docs/ACP.md` do not say "experimental" (only `initialize` does); `docs/PROTOCOL.md:15` still says "No Agent Client Protocol"; the stdio-MCP deviation is not stated as a spec deviation; `loadSession: true` with partial replay is documented accurately, but not advertising it would be more honest.

## Held up when run

- **(a) Read-only:** unsolicited allow results had no effect; no request, no file.
- **(b) Permission replies:** `allow_always`, an unoffered id, a JSON-RPC error, wrong types and reject-then-allow all denied; a string id was ignored. `allow_once` with extra `always: true` fields approved once and the next write asked again.
- **Gates:** forbidden command rule, verification `block` mode and forbidden deletion sent no request and took no effect. Default verification mode asks, and allow-once then permits the edit.
- **(c) `cwd`:** relative, file, missing and newline are refused; a symlink or any outside directory is accepted, as the plan intends. Client MCP servers were not started and were reported.
- **Load:** `latest`, a prefix and another workspace's session are refused; same-workspace load replays.
- **(e) Framing:** a 256 MiB line peaked RSS at 204 MB; depth bomb, batch, malformed JSON, pre- and repeated `initialize` are answered correctly.
- **(f) EOF mid-turn:** exit 0 in 7.4 s, `sleep` and `harness-core` gone.
- **Late allow after `session/cancel`:** no write.
- **Stdout:** every line parsed in all runs.
- **Managed policy:** start-up load and clamps apply (reading only).

## Tests

- The other four `acp_*` files passed 3 of 3 (53 tests, under 3.2 s each).
- `acp_stdio.test.ts` does not cover real approvals, cancel races, timeout, EOF, signals, framing, `cwd`, checkpoint choice or auto mode.

## Not checked

- Head-versus-base failing sets, and the `index.ts` help and command suites (stopped first).
- The load id-match mutation (the run hung and I killed it).
- The 10-minute timeout, Node 22, real clients, and the unhandled-rejection path from `finishPrompt`.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review PR #752 ACP fixes" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS** at `bf218d039eb00ebcd1b7ca0db2045ec3f7b0dc28`. The head did not move. The stuck `stopping` flag below is not an auto-approval or security hole, but it is a defect in the new code.

**Findings**

1. **`stopping` can stick true forever** (`cli/src/acp_stdio.ts:171-178` against `:393-396`). Confirmed by running.
   - `finishPrompt` clears `stopping` and then awaits the receipt for up to 2 s, with `pendingPrompt` still set.
   - A `$/cancel_request` arriving in that window sets `pendingPrompt = undefined` and `stopping = true`, then sends `cancel`.
   - The sidecar has no active turn, so `cancel()` (`sidecar.ts:508-519`) emits no `turn.result`. `stopping` never clears.
   - I drove this with a fake sidecar. Every later prompt gets "still stopping" and the session is unusable until it is reopened.
   - The same run shows prompt id 3 receiving two responses (-32800, then a result). The double answer is pre-existing in #741 but is made worse here.
   - Fix: in the cancel branch, only set `stopping` when the sidecar still has an active turn. Alternatively, have `finishPrompt` snapshot and clear `pendingPrompt` before its first `await`. Also clear `stopping` when a sidecar `error` or a session close arrives.
   - Non-blocking for auto approval, but I would fix it before release. It is a small change.
2. **Cancel then deny ordering.** By reading. `dropPendingApproval(deny=true)` sends `deny`, and `cancel` follows. The sidecar's turn can proceed to the model between the two commands. In the real-sidecar `cancelstale` run no stray response or error reached the client. Non-blocking.
3. **`stopping` after a checkpoint choice is untested.** Confirmed by mutation (see E). Non-blocking.

**A. Auto approval.** I found no route past the start-up check. Confirmed by running:
- `--yes`, `--mode auto` and `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE=auto` each gave exit 2, the stderr message and empty stdout.
- A `NEXUS_APPROVAL_MODE=auto` line in the throwaway HOME's `config.env` also gave exit 2.
- `--read-only --yes` and `--read-only` with the env set to auto start in read-only (exit 0), which is correct.
- `--full-access` is not a CLI flag. The `fullAccess` branch of the check is reachable only through config or env.
- A project `.nexus.env` in the workspace did not select auto and started normally (exit 0).

By reading, nothing after start-up can switch the mode:
- The sidecar mode changes only on `approve` with `always` (the adapter hardcodes `always:false`) or on the `slash` command (never sent by the adapter).
- `session/new` copies the already-checked config.
- `_meta` is dropped by `validate.ts`.

I did not read `policy_runtime.ts`. As I understand it, managed policy can only clamp the mode.

**B. Signals.** Confirmed by running:
- Idle: SIGTERM gave 143 and SIGINT gave 130, both in about 50 ms.
- Mid-turn with a permission pending: 143 in about 605 ms and 130 in about 656 ms.
- No leftover `harness-core` from rev752. The only ones remaining belong to other worktrees.
- By reading, `close()` awaits each sidecar's `shutdown`, which aborts the turn, awaits `idle()` and then calls `app.shutdown()`. `process.exit` runs after that, so in-flight saves are not cut off. A hung `idle()` would block exit with no timeout, so I recommend a hard-exit timer as a follow-up.

**C/D.**
- Real-adapter `cancelstale` (`$/cancel_request` for the prompt with a permission pending):
  - The prompt got -32800.
  - The next prompt asked again and wrote `b.txt` only.
  - `a.txt` was not written.
- A late reply to an abandoned permission id approves nothing. This is by reading, and the new test also covers it.
- `session/cancel` while a permission is pending: by reading, `finishPrompt` drops the permission.
- Not run: two rapid cancels, and cancel of an id that is not a prompt. Reading suggests both are harmless.
- `tools` and `pending` are cleaned up in `finishPrompt`.

<output-file>/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/d25d0d24-8aed-4b03-a951-966dc54f0666/tasks/a1a4b2d9ebf834e68.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Plan VR-2 verify for clients" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I wrote the VR-2 plan to `/home/mike/nexus-wt/_plans/VR2_PLAN.md`. The design needs no change to `cli/src/core/verify.ts` or the verify-result schema (stays v3). Two claim conflicts, covered under Main risks, will delay slices 1 and 2.

**Decisions taken**
1. **The caller controls nothing.** The MCP tool `nexus_verify` takes no arguments, and the ACP method `_nexus/verify` takes only `sessionId`. Any extra field (a verifier, base, path, claimed outcome) is rejected with an error and no verification runs.
2. **The base commit is pinned when the server or session starts.** Otherwise the caller could commit weakened tests first and be judged against them. The change checked is always the working tree on disk.
3. **Only the operator's verifier runs.** That is the existing `--authoritative-verifier` flag, and `--deliverable-file` supplies the deliverables. A verifier discovered in the repo is never approved on these surfaces, so the caller gets the existing BLOCKED / `VERIFIER_NOT_APPROVED` with nothing run and is never prompted. Without deliverables the best result is REGRESSION_FREE.
4. **Sandbox is always on, network off, no opt-out.** Running the verifier is acceptable: serve-mcp already lets the same caller run arbitrary shell commands through `run_command` without approval.
5. **The call is refused** in read-only mode, when managed policy requires a human for command execution, while another verification runs (one per process, not queued), and in ACP while a prompt runs on that session. Prompts are also refused during a verification.
6. **Verification runs in a forked worker process.** `verifyChange` runs its child processes synchronously (`spawnSync`), so in-process it would freeze the server for minutes. The fork also makes timeouts and cancellation enforceable.
7. **Receipts:**
   - ids are `vr-` plus 32 random hex digits, kept in memory per process (32 at most), with a SHA-256 of the served document.
   - MCP reads them back through `resources/read nexus://verify-receipt/&lt;id&gt;`; ACP through `_nexus/receipt/get` by explicit id, bound to the session.
   - Without an id, `receipt/get` still returns turn receipts only, so existing clients see no change.
   - There is no file store: anything the server's user can write, the caller can overwrite with `run_command`.
8. **Every verdict, BLOCKED included, is a successful result with a receipt.** An error comes back only when no verdict exists.

**Slices**
1. **Shared service and MCP tool (M):** new `cli/src/core/verify_service.ts` and `verify_worker.ts`; edits to `cli/src/mcp.ts`, `cli/src/index.ts` (serve-mcp action), one exported helper in `cli/src/config.ts`, `docs/MCP_GUIDE.md`, `docs/USER_MANUAL.md`. New tests `verify_service.test.ts` and `mcp_verify.test.ts`.
2. **ACP extension (S/M):** `cli/src/acp_stdio.ts`, `acp/types.ts`, `acp/validate.ts`, `acp/mapping.ts`, the regenerated ACP schema and its ledger entry, `docs/ACP.md`, `cli/test/helpers/acp_examples.ts`. New test `acp_verify.test.ts`; updates to `acp_schema.test.ts` and `acp_mapping.test.ts`.
3. **Docs and evidence (S):** new invariant row `I-AUTH-6` in `docs/INVARIANTS.md`, `docs/protocol/README.md`, `docs/evidence/VR2_VERIFY_FOR_CLIENTS.md`, a status note in `docs/v1.3.0/PLAN.md`.

The requirements ledger and the tool-description budget tests are unaffected: the new tool is not in Nexus's own model-facing tool list.

**Main risks**
- **Same-user tampering:** the guarantee covers the request only. A caller with shell as the server's user can still tamper with `node_modules`, `~/.gitconfig` content filters (which verify trusts as operator config) or the Nexus install. Only OS isolation stops that; the plan documents it as a limit.
- **Claim conflicts:**
  - `index.ts` is held by an AC-1 claim whose branch has merged, but it is under 24 hours old (until 2026-11-24T01:10Z), so only the owner can release it before then. This blocks slice 1.
  - `acp_stdio.ts` is in open PR #754, so slice 2 starts after it merges.
  - The ACP types, mapping and docs claims belong to a Codex process (pid 11247) that is still alive.
- **Existing serve-mcp gaps, left out of scope:** no input line-length cap, `--read-only` ignored for workspace tools, hard-coded server version `1.2.1`.

**Could not determine**
- Whether forking a `.ts` worker inherits `--import tsx` under the test runner on Node 22 and 24. Check this first in slice 1.
- Whether the Codex agent holding the ACP claims is still working on them.
- XERJ was available but its corpora have no peer exposing verification as an MCP tool or ACP extension. The plan's reference-coding section records the queries and the hits from opencode.</result>

<result>Slice 1 of VR-2 is implemented in `/home/mike/nexus-wt/vr2` and every test and gate I ran passes; nothing is committed. Two deviations from the plan need your attention: the kill mechanism, and `harness.test.ts`, which I left untouched.

## Files
- **New:** `cli/src/core/verify_service.ts` (370 lines), `cli/src/core/verify_worker.ts` (59), `cli/test/verify_service.test.ts` (289), `cli/test/mcp_verify.test.ts` (350).
- **Edited:** `cli/src/mcp.ts` (+103), `cli/src/index.ts` (+16, `serve-mcp` action only), `cli/src/config.ts` (one `export`), `docs/MCP_GUIDE.md` (+32), `docs/USER_MANUAL.md` (+4).
- `verify.ts` and `verify_contract.ts` are unchanged.

## Worker launch
- The service uses `child_process.fork`, detached, with stdout ignored and an IPC channel. The worker file extension follows the service module's own (`.ts` or `.js`).
- **Source mode:** `execArgv` is set explicitly to `["--import", import.meta.resolve("tsx")]`, so it does not rely on inheriting flags. Proven by the tests (Node 24 only; Node 22 not run).
- **Dist mode:** `execArgv` is `[]`. I checked this once by hand: built `dist`, got VERIFIED over stdio, then removed the build.
- The worker sends `ready` before the job is sent, and messages are tagged, because tsx also talks on the IPC channel.

## Security properties and their tests
All in `mcp_verify.test.ts` over real stdio unless noted.
- **No arguments:** 23 hostile `arguments` values and extra `params` members each get `-32602`, with no receipt and no worker started.
- **Operator verifier only:** with no `--authoritative-verifier`, the result is BLOCKED / `VERIFIER_NOT_APPROVED`, `runs: []`, and a marker file the repo's test script would create is absent. The server sends no requests to the client.
- **Pinned base:** a weakened test committed after the pin gives BLOCKED / `VERIFICATION_EDIT_BLOCKED` (`verify_service.test.ts`). A second test documents the boundary: one committed before the pin becomes the reported base.
- **Verdicts:** good diff VERIFIED, weakened test BLOCKED, broken product UNVERIFIED / `VERIFIER_FAILED`.
- **Sandbox:** the VERIFIED receipt records a confined engine with network off.
- **Refusals:** `--read-only`, `--mode read-only`, managed `require_human = ["exec"]`, and a workspace with no commit each return `isError` with no receipt.
- **Single flight:** two calls in one chunk; the second is refused as busy and the first completes.
- **Timeout and cancel:** the watchdog (service test) and `notifications/cancelled` (MCP test) both leave the worker and the `sleep` verifier gone, no receipt and scratch directories removed. A cancelled call gets no response.
- **Size cap, receipts:** an oversized result is an error. Ids, eviction at 32, frozen receipts, the SHA-256, forged ids and byte-identical re-reads are covered in both files.

## Results
- `verify_service`: 10 pass, 0 fail. `mcp_verify`: 6 pass, 0 fail.
- **Stress:** each file 5 of 5 sequential and 4 of 4 concurrent, all passing.
- **Regression:** `mcp` 4/0, `mcp_http_delivery` 6/0, `mcp_tool_through_spine` 3/0, `verify_command` 18/0, `verify_hostile` 46/0, `verify_sandbox` 10/0, `verify_schema` 4/0, `check_requirements` 28/0, `context_convergence` 57/0, the two `McpServer` tests in `harness.test.ts` 2/0.
- **Gates:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `check_invariants.mjs` and `check_requirements.mjs` exit 0. No gate required a ledger entry.

## Deviations
1. **Kill by process tree, not process group alone.** The verifier runs under `timeout`, which leaves the worker's group, so killing the group does not reach it. The service lists the worker's descendants with `ps` and signals each by pid, plus the group: SIGTERM first, SIGKILL after 10 s.
2. **`harness.test.ts` not edited.** It has a live claim (codex, PA-4). The equivalent assertion is in `mcp_verify.test.ts`.
3. **MCP test starts the server through `cliEntry()`** (the build when current, else source) to stay under the 300 s per-file CI limit. Locally the file takes about 175 s from source.
4. **Small additions beyond the plan:** `watchdogMs` and `killGraceMs` test seams; `--mode read-only` treated as read-only; an `operator_hint` for REGRESSION_FREE with no deliverables; `_meta` allowed in `params`.
5. **`config.ts` is imported dynamically inside the `serve-mcp` action**, to keep the `index.ts` edit within that command.

## Not done
- Node 22 was not run.
- If the server is SIGKILLed, the worker stops only after its current verifier run ends.
- The `INVARIANTS.md` row and the evidence doc are left for slice 3.
- `test_durations.json` has no entry for the new files (a missing entry is not a failure).</result>

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS** at `4196a306c97152ff735498d7c6bdb5a0bcdd0c75` (PR #756, base `release/v1.3.0`). The head did not move. I found no way to get VERIFIED for a weakened or broken change. One real defect: SIGTERM of the server leaks the verifier and scratch dirs.

**Findings, ranked**

1. **Non-blocking, confirmed by running.** `cli/src/index.ts:~1195-1217` and `cli/src/core/verify_service.ts:forkRunner`: a SIGTERM to the real server leaves the worker's verifier running and the scratch trees on disk. serve-mcp has no SIGTERM handler, and `process.once("exit")` does not fire on signal death. I ran the server directly under node with a SIGTERM-ignoring verifier mid-run. After SIGTERM, `sleep` and `bwrap` survived and `nexus-verify-*` and `nexus-verifier-*` dirs remained. SIGKILL of the server behaves the same.
   - Fix: add SIGTERM and SIGINT handlers that call `server.shutdown()` and wait for the runner to settle.
   - Stdin EOF and `notifications/cancelled` are clean: processes gone in about 1s, no scratch left, and no response after cancel.
2. **Non-blocking, by reading.** `mcp.ts` `startStdio` (the added `void this.handleMessage(req).then(...)`) has no `.catch`. A throw in that path would be an unhandled rejection and could crash the server. Add a catch that writes a JSON-RPC error.
3. **Non-blocking, by reading.** `forkRunner` kill logic. The descendant list comes from one `ps` snapshot, so there is a theoretical pid-reuse window.
   - Targets are only the worker's subtree plus its pgid, so it cannot reach pid 1, the server or the caller.
   - If `ps` is missing, the verifier (which `timeout` moves out of the worker's process group) is orphaned until its deadline.
4. **Non-blocking, documentation.** The MCP_GUIDE limit paragraph is honest but incomplete. It names `node_modules`, `.venv`, git config and the install. It omits:
   - the server user's HOME: `~/.config/nexus/config.env` operator settings (for example `NEXUS_VERIFIER_ENV_PASSTHROUGH`) and any user-tier managed policy, which a same-user `run_command` can edit or delete;
   - an operator verifier script that lives outside the workspace;
   - PATH binaries;
   - the SIGTERM and SIGKILL orphan behaviour.
5. **Non-blocking, by reading.** The `readOnly` option is set only from `--read-only` or `--mode read-only`. `REDACTED` from the environment or config does not refuse `nexus_verify`. The doc says as much, but this is inconsistent with `loadConfig`.
6. **Non-blocking, confirmed.** A failing verifier's `outputTail` in the receipt includes the server's passthrough environment (PATH, home paths, usernames, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` path). Secret-named variables were not passed, and the `receipt_sha256` matched the served bytes. The receipt also carries the real workspace path. This matches CLI output, and the caller already has `run_command env`.
7. **Documented limit.** `node_modules` and `.venv` are linked live, so planting a shim there is outside the guard. I did not run this one.

**Checked and held, all confirmed by running**
- **Arguments, property 1.** Every shape was refused with -32602: object, array, string, null, number, false, empty string, nested, `__proto__`, `constructor`, `_meta` inside `arguments`. An extra `params` member was refused; `_meta` beside `arguments` and extra JSON-RPC envelope members were ignored and had no effect. Hostile `initialize` params and capabilities had no effect.
- **Receipt URIs.** Traversal, a trailing slash, a query string, upper case, a 100 KB id, a newline, `file://`, percent-escapes and other receipt ids all returned "not available".
- **Pinned base, property 3:**
  - Weakened test committed plus `update-ref`: BLOCKED / `VERIFICATION_EDIT_BLOCKED`.
  - `git replace` of the base to the weakened commit: BLOCKED (`--no-replace-objects` is pinned).
  - Amend plus gc pruning the base: usage error, no verdict.
  - `core.worktree` repointed: usage error.
  - `.git` replaced by a gitdir pointer: BLOCKED / `UNTRUSTED_CHECKOUT`.
  - A `filter.clean` command plus `.gitattributes`: the filter never ran.
  - `.git/info/exclude`: no raised verdict.
- **Verification-path tampering, property 2.** Editing the operator's workspace `verify.sh` gave `VERIFIER_DEFINITION_CHANGED`. A `.npmrc` `script-shell` gave the same. Rewriting the `package.json` test script gave `VERIFICATION_EDIT_BLOCKED`. With no operator verifier, nothing ran (BLOCKED / `VERIFIER_NOT_APPROVED`; covered by the PR test and mutation M2, not re-driven by hand).
- **Worker path, property 7.** By reading, it comes from `import.meta.url` and `import.meta.resolve("tsx")`, not from cwd, env or PATH. The verifier's stdio has no IPC fd.
