## Changes
1. **Signals** — `cli/src/mcp.ts:996-1011`: SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers registered in `startStdio` only. They await `VerifyService.stop()`, shut the bridge down, then `process.exit(130|143)`. The hard-exit timer is kill grace + 5 s and is not unref'd.
   - `cli/src/core/verify_service.ts:383`: new `stop()` (abort and wait until settled), backed by `settled` at `:229`.
   - `verify_service.ts:185`: after the grace, the verifier is SIGKILLed first and the worker spared so it still removes its scratch trees; the worker is killed after a second grace.
2. **Catch** — `cli/src/mcp.ts:969`: the un-awaited verify call now writes a `-32603` error with the request id.
3. **Read-only** — `cli/src/index.ts:1213-1222` (inside `serve-mcp`): `readOnly` now comes from `buildConfig(options)`. An empty HOME with no model still starts (every test server runs that way).
4. **Docs** — `docs/MCP_GUIDE.md:136-144` rewritten with the four additions; line 128 now names `REDACTED`. `docs/USER_MANUAL.md` is unchanged (still true).

## Tests added (`cli/test/mcp_verify.test.ts`)
- `:293` "SIGTERM to the server stops a verifier that ignores SIGTERM and removes the scratch trees before the server exits". With the handlers removed it fails (runs to its 180 s limit).
- `:336` a `REDACTED` case in the refusals test.

## Results
- `mcp_verify`: 7 pass, 0 fail, three runs in a row.
- `verify_service`: 10/0. `mcp.test.ts`: 4/0.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean; `check_invariants.mjs` exits 0.

## Caveats
- If `buildConfig` throws, the server still starts and `nexus_verify` is refused as read-only, with a stderr notice; exiting 2 there would have broken startup for existing users with an unrelated config error.
- The SIGKILL stage is not exercised by any test: under bubblewrap the sandbox dies on SIGTERM even when the verifier traps it, so the test exits in about 7 s on the graceful path.
- The catch at `mcp.ts:969` has no test.

<summary>Agent "Review VR-2 ACP half and follow-ups" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS at `f25030a3c0c0cc64e5abbefadad06c648a9b59c4`.** The head has not moved (confirmed with `gh pr view`). I found no blocker. Findings are by reading unless marked "confirmed by running". The worktree is removed and nothing was committed or posted.

**Findings, ranked**

1. **Part A, non-blocking, confirmed by running:** id-less `_nexus/receipt/get` can return a verification receipt, which breaks the stated property that it returns turn receipts only. `latestReceipts` (`cli/src/acp_stdio.ts`, receipt handler near line 353) mixes both kinds, and the test at `cli/test/acp_stdio.test.ts:~118` asserts this behaviour. A client that expects the turn-receipt shape gets a different document. Fix: keep the id-less path on turn receipts only, with a separate map for verification receipts, and update the test.

2. **Part A, documented limit not yet documented (cwd/workspace choice):** a verification covers whatever directory the client passes as `session/new` or `session/load` `cwd`.
   - `session.cwd` is used as the worktree, and the base is pinned from `HEAD` of that directory.
   - The operator's verifier command and deliverable list are fixed at startup, but deliverables are normalised against the launch workspace (`config.ts:529`) and then resolved by the worker against the session cwd.
   - So a client-chosen repo containing, for example, a `package.json` whose test is `exit 0` can get a verdict.
   - The receipt does record `workspace` and `base_pinned_at`. The `_nexus/verify` summary omits `workspace`, so a client reading only the result cannot see which tree was covered.
   - `docs/evidence/v1.3.0/VR2.md` does not mention any of this.
   - Fix: document it, add `workspace` to the summary, and either refuse a `cwd` outside the operator's `--workspace` or state that the client chooses the tree.

3. **Part A, documented limit not yet documented (base re-pinning):** the base is pinned in `startSession` for both new and load (`acp_stdio.ts:~229`). A client can commit weakened tests, open a new session, and get a base that already contains the weakening. The only protection is that the client cannot move the base of an existing session.
   - The client also approves tool calls itself in review mode, so it can make those commits.
   - The evidence doc says "before an ACP session starts is the baseline". That is true but does not say each new session re-pins.
   - For ACP, the pin only protects a single session. Say so, or have the operator pin once at `nexus acp` start.

4. **Part A, test gap, confirmed by running (mutation 2 below):** nothing tests refusal of verify during a prompt or a stopping turn, or of a prompt during a verify. There is also no read-only refusal test for ACP. The cross-session receipt test at `acp_stdio.test.ts:~125` uses a session id that is not active, so it proves little. Fix: add tests for verify-during-prompt, prompt-during-verify and read-only, and use a second real session for the cross-session receipt test.

5. **Part A, non-blocking:** shutdown timing. The ACP signal deadline is 10 s (`ACP_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS`), the same as `VERIFY_KILL_GRACE_MS`. A verifier that ignores SIGTERM is killed at 10 s, but `process.exit` can fire first, and the exit hook then SIGKILLs the worker, so scratch trees may be left behind. MCP avoids this with a 15 s hard exit. Fix: make the ACP deadline at least `VERIFY_KILL_GRACE_MS + 5s` when a verification is running. Not run; by reading only.

6. **Part A, minor:** `_meta` is accepted on `_nexus/verify` while the property says nothing else is accepted. It is never read, so it reaches no execution path. Say so in the docs, or reject it.

**What held up**
- Extra, forged or claimed members (`verifier`, `base`, `cwd`, verdict) are refused before anything runs; this is tested.
- Verify and `startingSessions`/overlap checks are synchronous, so two verifies cannot race.
- `$/cancel_request` aborts the worker and replies once; this is tested.
- `close()` aborts and awaits the verification, and SIGINT/SIGTERM and stdin end both go through `close()`.
- Read-only comes from the same resolved config, and the auto-approval startup refusal is unchanged.
- `acp-adapter-v1.schema.json` is untouched. The v2 ledger entry exists. `gen_acp_schema.mjs --check` reports v2 current.
- `initialize` advertises the extension truthfully.
- Receipt lookup is bound to the session in `VerifyService`.

**Part B**
- The MCP signal handlers are sound. They are registered with `process.on` and do not keep the loop alive. The hard-exit timer is only armed after a signal, and the `stopping` guard makes repeat signals harmless. stdin-EOF shutdown is unchanged. Exit codes 143/130 are correct.
- The 15 s hard exit gives the worker 5 s after the verifier is killed at 10 s, so scratch removal is plausible.
- The SIGKILL-without-worker step uses a fresh `descendants()` listing and the timers are cleared on settle. A signalled non-descendant would need a pid-reuse race inside that window. Negligible.
- `buildConfig` fails closed: `readOnly` defaults to `true`, and the catch path logs a diagnostic.
- The new SIGTERM test is sound: it checks exit 143, worker and verifier gone, and no verdict. I did not run it against the pre-fix code.

**Mutations (each run, then `git checkout`)**
- Accept an extra param: `acp_stdio` 8/9 pass and `acp_validate` 14/15 pass, so the tests fail as they should.
- Allow verify during a prompt: `acp_stdio` 9/9 and `acp_review_fixes` 8/8 still pass, so this mutation is not caught (finding 4).
- Drop the receipt session binding: `acp_stdio` 8/9 and `verify_service` 9/10 pass, so the tests fail as they should.

**Test counts at head** (throwaway HOME, one file at a time, 0 failures in every file): `acp_stdio` 9, `acp_validate` 15, `acp_schema` 7, `acp_mapping` 22, `acp_review_fixes` 8, `acp_client_conformance` 2, `verify_service` 10, `mcp_verify` 7, `mcp` 4.

**Evidence doc:** the claims match the tests except for the omissions in findings 2 and 3. It claims "69 passed" from a run at a different base, which I did not reproduce. The doc says the run was on `4196a306` plus a patch, and "No hosted CI has run".

**Not checked:** a real-adapter stdio run with a real worker (I used mutation runs and reading), SIGTERM of `nexus acp` during a live verify, two verifies on two sessions, receipt eviction under ACP, Node 22.</result>

<result>**The head moved while I reviewed.** I reviewed `8d5b546500eb782ed593f8ea2c2fbad03da859fa`. The PR head is now `7320b78a1e99729285998f87c1d2b815cd70d7f6`, which I did not review, so this verdict does not carry over to it.

**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES at `8d5b546500eb782ed593f8ea2c2fbad03da859fa`.** The core PA-3 requirement is not met, and the new test does not test it.

**Blocking**
1. **Process restarts are not detected (confirmed by running).** The "boot id" is `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id` (`operation_runner.ts:719`). It changes only on a host reboot, so a crash mid-turn or a clean exit followed by a restart on the same boot keeps the same id.
   - A probe of `goalStatus` showed `{status:"RUNNING", activeBootId: &lt;current boot&gt;}` stays `RUNNING` with nothing driving it.
   - A goal with no `activeBootId` (state written before this PR) also stays `RUNNING`.
   - Only a different boot id pauses a goal (`goal.ts:50-56`). Where there is no `/proc` (macOS), nothing pauses at all.
   - The plan wants a goal "active from an earlier process" to resume paused. A per-process nonce plus the existing lock-liveness check would fit, but `activeBootId` is only cleared for COMPLETE/CANCELLED/PAUSED (`operation_runner.ts:~762`). A clean exit in WAITING or BLOCKED keeps it.
2. **The new test does not cover pause-on-boot (confirmed by mutation).** `goal_lifecycle.test.ts:~183` seeds the state as `PAUSED` with a pre-written stale reason, so `goalStatus` returns PAUSED without any new logic.
   - I removed both stale-boot checks (`operation_runner.ts` and `goal.ts:50`). All 6 tests still passed.
   - The test also returns early when `currentBootId()` is undefined, so it passes vacuously on hosts without `/proc`.
   - No test seeds a `RUNNING`, `WAITING` or `BLOCKED` state with a foreign boot id, which is the case the PR exists for.
   - There is no test for a corrupt or truncated state file, a legacy state, or two processes booting at once.

**Non-blocking**
- **Model output and tool calls cannot change the lifecycle (by reading).** `goalStatus` still requires a closed ledger, a passing verifier receipt and an independent ACCEPT. I deleted the reviewer and verifier checks and the existing completion test failed (5 pass, 1 fail). That test, not the new one, guards a model "done". The new test adds nothing for it. The new transitions are called only from `driveOperation` and `GoalOrchestrator.resume`/`pause`, so I found no path from model text or a tool result.
- **Explicit resume and budgets (by reading, partly unchecked).** Resume is explicit: `prepare` clears the stale pause in `goal.ts:~271`. The diff touches no approval, budget or `policy_runtime.ts` code, so nothing is loosened. The P10 budgets are untouched. I did not confirm that resume re-checks them, and I did not trace the policy path.
- **Pause reason is overwritten (by reading).** `goalStatus` shows `pauseReason ?? stale`, so a stale-boot reason can be replaced by an older operator reason.
- **Schema (by reading).** `activeBootId` and `pauseReason` are optional, validated in `operation_schema.ts:229-234`, and need no migration. `CURRENT_OPERATION_SCHEMA_VERSION` is not bumped. An older build ignores the fields, so this is acceptable.
- **State-file handling is unchanged.** The existing store quarantines invalid operations on load (`operation_runner_persistence.ts:~112`) and has a lock and atomic save. I did not test two processes booting at once or a truncated file.
- **Style.** The diff adds `delete (state as any).activeBootId` casts (`operation_runner.ts:~762, ~768`). Several lines are dense.

**Doc (`PA3_GOAL_LIFECYCLE.md`)**
It claims a goal left active "from an earlier host boot" shows as `PAUSED`. That matches the code, but no test shows it. The claim is also narrower than the plan's "earlier process", and the doc does not say that crashes without a reboot are not covered.

**Test counts at `8d5b546500eb`** (each file run alone with a throwaway HOME): every `goal*.test.ts` and `operation*.test.ts` file passed with 0 failures. `goal_lifecycle` had 6 pass, `goal_ledger_propagation` 2, and `operation_view` 34. `operation_view` timed out in my first run but passed on the base and on a rerun at head (35s), so I put it down to a transient slowdown.

**Not checked**
- The new head `7320b78a`.
- Non-Linux behaviour.
- A real two-process race.
- P10 budget and policy re-checks on resume.

The review worktree is removed. No edits, commits or comments were made.</result>

   - Only the primary turn loop is counted, at `run_turn.ts:~692`, via `beforeSubmit` and `reserveInferenceSubmission` (`run_turn.ts:263`).
   - The docs (`COMPACTION_AND_CONTINUITY.md`) and the plan progress note state that subagents and durable operation units are not counted.
   - They do not state that three side calls are also uncounted: findings extraction (`agent.ts:3307`), the compaction probe (`agent.ts:3758`) and the compaction summary (`agent.ts:3797`). Each sends a model request. "Covers the primary objective turn" is ambiguous about them.
   - Fix: list them as uncounted, or count them.

3. **Non-blocking, by reading.** The project `.env` can raise the budget. `NEXUS_MAX_INFERENCE_SUBMISSIONS` is in `PROJECT_ENV_KEYS` (`config.ts:188`). A repository can therefore move a new objective's ceiling from 1,000 to 10,000. This matches the existing `NEXUS_MAX_TOOL_INVOCATIONS` precedent, but the docs never say a project can set it. A raise on resume is blocked, because `Math.min(saved, configured)` is used at `run_turn.ts:~193`.

4. **Non-blocking, by reading.** The non-durable path (`!this.durable`) uses `ExecutionTracker.fresh()` (`run_turn.ts:160`). The counter is per-turn there, with no persistence, and the docs do not say so.

**Answers to the questions**

- **Limit behaviour:** the turn ends with `execution=LIMIT_REACHED`, reason `INFERENCE_SUBMISSION_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`, outcome `NOT_ESTABLISHED`. This is never COMPLETED or VERIFIED.
- **Retry at the limit:** a denied retry moves the recovery record to `EXHAUSTED` and rethrows. Nothing retries afterwards.
- **Boundary:** `min(1, max - count)` allows exactly N submissions. The test confirms 1/1 sends exactly one request.
- **Persistence:** the reservation is saved before dispatch, and a save failure sends no request. A malformed counter or ceiling throws before any inference. Legacy records report their earlier history as unknown.
- **Config:** invalid values throw (fail-closed), with a minimum of 1, a default of 1,000 and a maximum of 10,000. I found no tool call or model path that sets the value.
- **Policy:** `policy_runtime.ts` has no tool-invocation clamp to mirror, and the PR does not touch policy or approval code. Nothing is loosened by reading. I did not exercise this.
- **Protocol:** the change is additive. Only new entries were appended to `RUN_RESULT_VERSIONS.json` and `VERSIONS.json`, and `schema_version` stays 1. The tests `run_json_contract` and `protocol_schema` pass. The doctor config key is consistent.

**Blocker 1 (goal staleness): mostly fixed.**
- "This process" is now `activePid` plus `activeHost` (`cli/src/goal.ts:65-79`, set at `goal.ts:280`). Liveness of the previous owner is `process.kill(pid, 0)`. There is no lock or nonce.
- I called the real `goalStatus` on seeded operations with a dead pid, a foreign `activeBootId`, and no identity at all. RUNNING, WAITING and BLOCKED all came back PAUSED with a reason.
- PAUSED, CANCELLED and COMPLETE were left alone.
- A pid that is still alive (a live child process, or this process) stayed RUNNING, so a second concurrent `nexus` is neither paused nor stolen.
- Limits:
  - A reused pid reads as alive, so the goal is never paused.
  - A state on a different host, or with a pid but no host, stays RUNNING.
  - A state with only the current `activeBootId` and no pid stays RUNNING.
  - The reason text reads badly: "left active by earlier process (pid N) is no longer running" and "left active by an earlier process left it active…".

**Blocker 2 (the test must exercise it): fixed for goals, with one gap.**
- With `staleDriverReason` forced to return undefined, `goal_lifecycle.test.ts` went from all passing to 5 pass and 2 fail. I restored the file with `git checkout`.
- The new same-boot test seeds RUNNING with a dead pid and does not need `/proc`.
- The host-boot test still has `if (!boot) return;` at `goal_lifecycle.test.ts:187`, so it passes vacuously without `/proc`.
- No test references `pauseForStaleDriver`, `stale_driver` or "Paused on resume" on the `driveOperation` path.

**New finding, blocking: a regression in `driveOperation`.**
- `cli/test/operation_entry_integrity.test.ts` ("a stale snapshot … not written back over COMPLETE") fails at head (2 pass, 1 fail; PAUSED instead of COMPLETE). On `origin/release/v1.3.0` the same file passes 3 of 3.
- Cause: `operation.ts:570` plans operations as RUNNING with no identity. `driveOperation` now treats any "existing" RUNNING operation without identity as stale (`operation_runner.ts:~735-745`, rule "no identity means stale").
- A freshly planned operation resumed through `operation_cli.ts:391`, `loop.ts:471` or `batch.ts:223` can therefore be paused without ever running.
- Fix: apply the no-identity rule only in goal code. Alternatively, have `plan` stamp identity, or make `driveOperation` skip the rule when the operation has never run. Then add a `driveOperation` test for the stale path.

**Non-blocking items:**
- `pauseReason ?? stale` still hides the stale reason behind an older operator reason. Probed: RUNNING with a dead pid and an old `pauseReason` reports only the operator text. Not fixed.
- The doc is more accurate now, but it does not list the limits above (pid reuse, other host).
- The `(state as any)` casts remain and have multiplied (`operation_runner.ts:784-792`, plus the new `catch (err: any)`). Use a typed optional delete instead.

**Item 4 (lifecycle and policy):**
- The delta only reads the identity fields and the pid. No model text, tool call or workspace content can set them.
- `policy_runtime.ts` is untouched.
- `goal.ts:352` pauses on the stale reason, and resume goes through the same `driveOperation` path as start. I did not run an approval or budget check against it.

**Item 5 (token usage, `7320b78a`):**
- It only reads `tokenStats` totals and counts, and exposes no content.
- Reported and estimated tokens are split, so nothing is double counted across the two buckets.
- It reloads one task file per unit on every `result()` call, which is acceptable.
- `agentRuns` sums `unit.runs`, which may include retries. That is cosmetic.

<task-id>a7ff865090af4d524</task-id>
<tool-use-id>toolu_0167xrxstiZDgH1fnM5RMbHV</tool-use-id>
<output-file>/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/d25d0d24-8aed-4b03-a951-966dc54f0666/tasks/a7ff865090af4d524.output</output-file>
<status>completed</status>
<summary>Agent "Review PR #761 flight log integrity" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS** at `d24997ea926da335ff0e91ba8e88aca810831651`. The head did not move during the review. The worktree is removed. I made no commits, pushes or comments, and I ran no processes in the background.

**Findings, ranked**

1. **Non-blocking, confirmed by running: the gap is hidden if the recorder restarts before another write.**
   - Sequence: `session_start`, `c1`, then `c2` is skipped (cyclic argument), the process stops, and a new `FlightRecorder` on the same id appends `c3` and `session_end`.
   - `resumeChain` (`flight.ts:~88-92`) finds the file intact and resumes from the last good hash.
   - `loadFlight` then reports `chain intact (4 records), closed by its session end`, with `c2` absent.
   - The `chained=false` flag lives only in memory, and the skip leaves nothing on disk.
   - Restart on a skipped log does stay broken if a later record was already written (probe D).
   - Fix: on a skip, write a durable chained marker record if the disk allows. If it does not, accept that a trailing skip is undetectable and say so in the plan doc.

2. **Non-blocking, confirmed by running, then by reading: the skip is invisible to the operator.**
   - Every caller discards the return value (`dispatch_tool_call.ts:235` and the `recordToolResult` calls).
   - `recordingSkipped` appears only in `flight.ts:136` and `161`. There is no stderr or TUI warning, no turn-result or receipt field, and no counter.
   - The tool action continues, and the only trace is an unchained log read later.
   - Fix: add a recorder `degraded` getter and a one-time stderr warning. Consider a strict managed-policy option to stop. The plan doc defers this, and it is not implemented.

3. **Non-blocking, confirmed by running: attacker-controlled input can disable chaining for the rest of the session.**
   - A cyclic argument gave `sanitization_failed` (probe A) and a BigInt gave `sanitization_failed` (probe B).
   - Most of the cyclic and BigInt effect comes from the redactor throwing, not from the append.
   - Recording continues, but unchained. Detection afterwards is real: the log reads `CHAIN BROKEN at position 2`.
   - "An attacker-controlled input can make recording degrade while actions continue" holds. The skipped record is never seen, and the later ones are unprotected.
   - Whether a model can supply a cyclic or BigInt value depends on how tool arguments are parsed. JSON-parsed model arguments cannot be cyclic. I did not trace that.

4. **Non-blocking, by reading: a broken flight log does not fail audit verify.**
   - A log that was already broken at export time produces a note, not a finding (`audit_bundle.ts:1014`). `audit_export.ts:676` warns on export.
   - That is existing GV-2 behaviour, but the PR's "verifier reports broken" claim relies on it.

5. **Nit, confirmed by running: a skip on the first record (probe F) reads as `not chained (... written before hash chaining)`.** That wording is misleading. Consider a distinct message.

**Your numbered questions**

- **Q1:** a healthy session verifies as `chain intact (3 records), closed`. A mid-session skip followed by a write reads `CHAIN BROKEN` (probes A, B, D). A trailing skip with no later write reads as intact and not closed (probe E). Restart after a trailing skip reads as intact and closed (finding 1).

- **Q6:** the plan doc matches the code and tests. The line "reads as CHAIN BROKEN" is true only when a later record is written, which is finding 1. The 4-line PR #760 progress text is unrelated churn.

<summary>Agent "Review PR #767 orphan fix" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS at 8ab2e902b320ca462a58b721364ed7b87abfd607.** The PR head did not move, but #767 is already merged (merge commit 2dea2179). I tested "base" as 061843fc, the first parent of that merge.

**1. Diagnosis: partly holds.**
- Each verification runs several verifiers back to back. `execute` in `verify.ts:1110-1130` and `executorIn` in `verify.ts:1280` both call `checkpoint()` before running.
- `checkpoint()` (`verify.ts:738-741`) awaits `setImmediate`, which turns the event loop and lets the SIGTERM handler (`verify_worker.ts:14`) run. So a worker that was sent SIGTERM aborts at the next gap between verifiers.
- That makes the race narrower than the PR text says. The worker has to get past `checkpoint()` and into `spawnSync` after its verifier was killed but before its own SIGKILL. That is plausible only under load.
- The race is not reproduced, and the PR says so itself. The freeze is harmless, and it shrinks the window.
- Uncovered window, reproduced: `signalTree` (`verify_service.ts:172-185`) takes one `ps` snapshot and then kills. A descendant that forks after the snapshot, such as `timeout` starting `bwrap`, survives. The stage-3 kill hits `timeout` but misses the new `bwrap`, which is then orphaned.
- Stages 1 and 2 are covered by the next stage, as long as the worker is still alive to trigger it.
- If the worker exits on its own after stage 2, `finish` clears the timer. Stage 3 never runs, and descendants of a dead worker can no longer be found through `ps`.
- `onProcessExit` (line 195) runs `signalTree("SIGKILL")` synchronously, which is fine. The `ps` call inside it takes up to 5 s.

**2. Correctness (by reading).**
- `child.pid` undefined returns early. A dead pid makes `kill` throw, and that is caught.
- Pid reuse between the SIGSTOP and the SIGKILL is microseconds wide and no worse than before.
- A stopped worker is always followed by a SIGKILL in the same call. The group kill at line 183 also covers it. The only way it stays stopped is if SIGKILL throws. That is unlikely, and `kill` is already being called on a process the server owns.
- The freeze only applies when `worker &amp;&amp; name === "SIGKILL"`. Stage 2 passes `worker=false`, so it is unchanged.
- The `exit` and `disconnect` events fire normally after a SIGKILL of a stopped process.
- Scratch trees: a SIGKILLed worker could not clean up before this change either. The service and tests do not remove them at that stage. The watchdog test at `verify_service.test.ts:288` only covers the spared path.
- Windows: not checked. SIGSTOP does nothing there, and `try/catch` hides the failure.

**3. Loop results.** My script is `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/43bcbb67-2cff-4fa8-a05e-f8aa598df73b/scratchpad/loop.mts`. It aborts the verifier at a random 0-400 ms with `killGraceMs` 150, then looks for a marker process 300 ms later.

| | Plain `sleep` | `trap '' TERM; sleep` |
|---|---|---|
| Head | 30 runs, 0 orphans | about 140 runs, 2 orphans |
| Base (061843fc) | 30 runs, 0 orphans | 110 runs, 0 orphans |

- Both head orphans were `bwrap` with ppid 1, with cancellation roughly 200 ms after start. That is the post-snapshot fork window above.
- Two of 140 against zero of 110 is not a significant difference. I do not read it as a regression from the freeze.
- Part of the head TRAP count came from two copies of that run that I started by mistake and that ran at the same time.
- I did not reproduce the original worker race at either commit.

**4. Tests at head.**
- `verify_service`: 10 of 10 pass.
- `mcp_verify`: 7 of 7 pass.
- `leak_guard`: 6 of 6 pass.

1. **Non-blocking, by reading. `signalTree` signals `child.pid` after the worker has been reaped** (`verify_service.ts:191, 205, 222`).
   - The `finish` sweep (:240) always runs after the worker has exited and been reaped.
   - The kill timers can also fire after `exit` but before `disconnect`. Both cases send SIGSTOP and SIGCONT to a pid that may have been reused. `list()` then uses that pid as a root, so the reused process's children can be stopped and killed.
   - Linux reuses pids only after a wrap, so this is rare. In a container with a small `pid_max` and heavy churn it is not impossible.
   - Fix: guard with `child.exitCode === null &amp;&amp; child.signalCode === null`. In the sweep, use only `survivors()` as roots and skip the worker.
2. **Non-blocking, by reading. Pids found by the last listing are neither stopped nor signalled** (:212, :214).
   - When the pass limit is hit, the final `targets` are never stopped or signalled. For a SIGKILL stage, those late forks escape.
   - They are not left stopped, so this is not a stuck-T problem.
   - Fix: `for (const pid of new Set([...stopped, ...targets])) send(pid, name)`.
3. **Non-blocking, by reading. Two smaller points on `seen`.**
   - `seen` is never pruned. It is bounded by one run, so this is minor.
   - `startedAt` compares clock ticks of 10 ms, so a reused pid would need to start within the same tick. That is not realistic.

**Q1 pid safety**
- `startedAt` indexing is correct. I ran it on a process named `a) b 1 2 3` and it returned the right field.
- A pid with undefined `startedAt` is never stored (`started !== undefined`, :197), so undefined is never compared.
- Roots are the worker plus remembered survivors, and `descendants` follows children only. Pid 1 has ppid 0 and is filtered out, so init's other children are not pulled in. I see no path to the server or the client.
- The only reuse hole is finding 1.

**Q2 stuck-T**
- The SIGTERM stage sends CONT to all stopped processes and the worker.
- SIGKILL with `worker=false` kills the descendants and sends CONT to the worker.
- SIGKILL with the worker kills everything.
- If `ps` fails or times out, it returns an empty listing. Only the worker is stopped, and it is then continued or killed. `send` swallows errors.
- If `child.pid` is undefined, the function returns before stopping anything.
- Everything is synchronous, so nothing interleaves.
- The only stuck case is the server dying mid-function.

**Q3 graceful path:** the worker is stopped, then SIGTERM is queued, then CONT is sent, so the handler still runs. Both test files passed, and the scratch-removal assertions are:
- `verify_service.test.ts:252`, after cancellation.
- `verify_service.test.ts:288`, after the watchdog timeout, in the "watchdog stops the worker" test.
- `mcp_verify.test.ts:112`, after cancellation, and :311 on SIGTERM to the server.

**Q4 sweep**
- It runs one `ps` plus a `/proc` read per remembered pid, roughly tens of milliseconds, on the event loop. The worst case is 5 s if `ps` hangs.
- It also covers the watchdog, because the watchdog aborts the same controller (:313–315).
- It only touches seen or descendant processes, so no legitimate leftovers are hit.
- It is acceptable. It's the same code path as finding 1.

**Q5 exit-time worst case**
- One call makes up to 1 + 6 = 7 `ps` runs. At 5 s each, that is 35 s if `ps` hangs, and about 0.1 s normally.
- Each concurrent run registers its own exit listener, so the bound is multiplied by the number of live runs.
- Consider a total deadline on `onProcessExit`, or one pass only.

**Q6 loops**

| Run | Runs | Orphans | Stopped-forever |
|---|---|---|---|
| TRAP=1 | 120 | 0 | not measured |
| TRAP=1 with my stopped check (copy at `…/scratchpad/loop_stop.mts`) | 60 | 0 | 0 |

The stopped check ran after settle and again 300 ms later.

**Tests:** `verify_service.test.ts` 10/10, `mcp_verify.test.ts` 7/7.

**Q7 other**
- The comments explain why, which is good.
- `loop.mts` and `loop_stop.mts` ran against a stale worktree path form, which I corrected, so there was no effect on results.
- Candidate unit tests:
  - `startedAt` parsing with a comm containing `)` and spaces.
  - `descendants` with several roots, and with a root that has been reparented.
  - A pid-reuse guard, once finding 1 is fixed.

**Not checked:** Node 22, the Docker sandbox engine, a hanging `ps`, and pid wrap in practice. Findings 1 and 2 are by reading only.

I left no sleep-marker processes and nothing in state T. The `rev770` worktree is removed.</result>

So the included file is labelled with the includer's scope (`local`), as the check assumes. The parser steps through the fields two at a time and reads `fields[index]` as the scope. ## Security probe: `workspace_configures_git_programs` guard in `audit_export.ts`

**Tip SHA tested:** `4ae25a10d5e1f33726a717b0fe1970fbd5f06517` (`origin/release/v1.3.0`). git 2.43.0, Node ran via tsx. All 17+submodule cases ran once with the check, once with it disabled (`false &amp;&amp;`).

### Results

| Case | With check (exec? / binding / reason) | Check disabled (exec? / binding) |
|---|---|---|
| 01 clean filter (.git/config) | no / unavailable / workspace_configures_git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 02 filter.process | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / unavailable(candidate_identity_unavailable) |
| 03 core.fsmonitor | no / unavailable / …git_programs | no / mismatch |
| 04 include.path | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 05 includeIf gitdir | no / **mismatch** / - | no / mismatch |
| 06 core.attributesFile | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 07 .git/info/attributes | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 08 .git-file + core.worktree | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 09 core.worktree | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 10 extensions.worktreeConfig | no / unavailable / …git_programs | **EXECUTED** / mismatch |
| 11 key-case fsMonitor | no / unavailable / …git_programs | no / mismatch |
| 12 parser-desync subsection | no / unavailable / …git_programs | no / mismatch |
| 13 hook post-index-change | no / **mismatch** / - | no / mismatch |
| 14 submodule filter | **EXECUTED** / unavailable / candidate_identity_unavailable | **EXECUTED** / unavailable(candidate_identity_unavailable) |
| 15 control A (none) | no / mismatch | no / mismatch |
| 16 control B (operator global) | **EXECUTED** / mismatch | EXECUTED / mismatch |
| 17 control C (lfs, no attrs) | no / unavailable / …git_programs | no / mismatch |

### (a) Bypasses — planted program EXECUTED with the check enabled
- **Case 14 (registered submodule).** A `filter.p.clean` planted in `.git/modules/sub/config` plus `sub/.gitattributes` **ran the marker command with the check enabled.** The guard only lists config of the top-level `workspacePath`; the submodule's own config is invisible to `git config --get-regexp` at the superproject root, so the guard returns "no hit", then `computeCandidateIdentitySync` hashes files inside `sub/` and fires the planted filter. The binding ends `unavailable / candidate_identity_unavailable` (the filter errored the scan) — so the refusal is incidental, not the guard catching it; the code already executed. This is a real bypass of the stated invariant ("nothing planted in an untrusted workspace executes with the exporter's rights").

### (b) Cases that prove nothing (did not execute even with the check disabled)
- 03 core.fsmonitor, 11 core.fsMonitor (case variant), 13 post-index-change hook, 05 includeIf gitdir, 12 parser-desync, 17 lfs-without-attrs. None run during candidate-identity hashing (fsmonitor/hooks aren't invoked by the hash path; the includeIf/desync/no-attrs variants never produced an active filter). They tell us only that the guard still *reports* unavailable for 03/11/12/17; they do not demonstrate an execution vector. 05 and 13 aren't even flagged (binding=mismatch), but also never execute, so no bypass.

### (c) Controls — all behaved as expected
- A (no config): not executed, mismatch. B (operator's `$HOME/.gitconfig` global filter): EXECUTED, mismatch — correctly *allowed*, scope `global` is trusted. C (lfs clean, no `.gitattributes`): unavailable/workspace_configures_git_programs — the documented over-refusal trade-off.

### Not run / caveats
Everything requested ran in one pass (no compile retries needed). The submodule bypass (14) is the actionable finding; I did not attempt to fix it or confirm whether a deeper `--get-regexp` scope walk or pre-scan refusal would close it.</result>

**1. Tests**
- `cli/test/audit_export.test.ts` at head passes: 21 of 21, including the new submodule test.
- I disabled both submodule conditions in a copy of the source (gitlink check and `modules/` check) and restored the original afterwards. The new test then fails with "the submodule's configured command ran".

**2. Probes** (real export, the new test's fixture style, filter planted last)

| Probe | Planted program ran? | Binding |
|---|---|---|
| (a) plain nested repo, not registered as a submodule | no | `partial`, no reason, so the export went ahead and hashed the workspace |
| (b) gitlink added with `update-index --cacheinfo 160000` | no | `unavailable`, `workspace_configures_git_programs` |
| (c) gitlink removed with `git rm --cached`, `.git/modules/sub` left in place | no | `unavailable`, `workspace_configures_git_programs` |

In (a), `status --untracked-files=all` did not descend into the inner repo. The inner filter did not run even though I re-stamped the inner file's mtime.

**3. Reading the function** (`cli/src/audit_export.ts:87-117`)
- `git ls-files --stage` and `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` run no planted program. Neither reads the working tree, so no filter applies. The `ls-files` call also sets `core.fsmonitor=false`. Both calls run before any program-naming config has been ruled out for the config the check reads. I read this off the code and did not probe it beyond the tests above.
- Error paths:
  - A failed `ls-files` or `rev-parse` returns true (refuse), and so does a `readdirSync` error other than ENOENT.
  - The only `return false` branch left is `:96`, for a directory that is not a Git work tree. That is correct, because no Git command runs there.
  - A `maxBuffer` overflow gives status null. `null !== 0` makes `:108` return true, so a huge index fails closed. I did not test it.
- Follow-up (nit, `:114`): an empty `modules/` directory is allowed. That is safe.
- Follow-up (nit, `:107`): `ls-files` can hit the 10 s timeout on a very large index. The result is refusal (`unavailable`), not a wrong answer, but the reason will read `workspace_configures_git_programs`. This is a misleading label rather than a security issue. A distinct reason would be clearer.

**4. False refusals**
- Plain repositories with no submodule still compare. The existing tests in `audit_export.test.ts` that use the fake receipt id show `mismatch`, and they pass at head.
- I did not run a dedicated test for this.

**Not checked**
- Node 24, because I ran only the default Node.
- Worktrees: a linked worktree whose `modules/` lives in the common git dir. `--absolute-git-dir` there points at the worktree's own gitdir, so a submodule stored in the common dir might be missed. Worth a probe.
- Sparse-checkout, `.gitmodules`-only entries, and `GIT_DIR` environment overrides.
- The Windows path, since the test skips on non-Linux.

## Decisions taken
- **D15:** `nexus runner serve` runs in the foreground under a documented `systemd --user` unit, with one worker process per job. Rejected alternatives: re-parenting the sidecar, double-fork, `systemd-run` per job.
- **Liveness:** each worker holds a kernel lock (flock). Signals go only to a pid whose kernel start time and boot id still match.
- **Attach:** the client talks to the runner through inbox files; you follow a job through its log and state only. Live attach is left to PF-3.
- **D19:** jobs are `read-only` by default; `--allow-worktree-write` is explicit. Unattended runs use a policy that allows only writes in the fresh worktree; exec and egress are denied. Never auto mode, `--yes` or full access. Jobs are always git-isolated at a base commit frozen when submitted.
- **Crash handling:** after a worker crash the job resumes automatically, at most 3 times, through the existing SI-7 recovery.
- **D21:** event triggers and SSH stay in v1.4. Also deferred: wake checks, time zones, schedule spec files. Missed runs are reported, not run, by default.
- **Hooks:** two events only, pre-tool and post-tool. Allow and `updatedInput` are ignored, `ask` counts as deny, and pre-tool fails closed. An invalid operator hooks file denies every call. Project hooks run only after their exact file hash is approved.
- **Plugins:** contribute hooks, trigger-loaded skills and MCP servers only — no in-process code, custom tools or verifiers. Git installs need a full commit id, and Nexus computes its own digest of the plugin's files.
- **D18:** in-process JS plugins stay, but workspace ones need per-file approval.

## Main risks
- `UnattendedApprovalPolicy` duplicates approval logic. It has an exhaustive table test, but must track any later change to `ApprovalPolicy`.
- Worktree-write jobs can only run commands you allowed in `command_rules.json`.
- Under systemd, a runner crash also ends its workers, so a crash mid-tool always needs a human decision.
- Hooks run after the interactive approval prompt.
- `agent.ts`, `index.ts`, `doctor.ts`, `plugins.ts` and `mcp.ts` are being edited by other agents.
- Edits by programs running as the same user are not detected.

## Could not determine
- Whether command `allow` rules match compound commands segment by segment, and how bwrap isolates pids. Both affect how the SI-7-against-runner test is written.
- Whether `nexus audit export` covers operations.
- Whether operation units can answer `NEEDS_INPUT` questions.
- GV-5's final plugin and MCP API. I read it from an uncommitted worktree.
- Whether the TUI can ask about project-hook approval without a protocol change.
- Whether git hosts allow fetching an unadvertised commit by id at depth 1. The plan has a fallback.

XERJ has only the opencode corpus loaded, so the goose, Gemini CLI and Codex parts rely on the plan's own peer notes.</result>

**Decisions taken**
- **PF-4:** no publish (D13). The package name stays `local-code-harness`, with `"private": true`. The lib only drives the sidecar as a separate process and does not export `ApplicationCore`, so it cannot skip the startup checks. The client API cannot send allow-always, and approvals it isn't told to grant are denied.
- **PF-3:** unix socket only, with no TCP or HTTP listener, so the origin check has nothing to apply to. The acceptance wording only names the socket, the token and mode 0600, so it is still met. Only the same user can connect. The token is 256 bits, kept in a 0600 file and compared in constant time.
- **PF-3 clients:** one controller and any number of read-only observers; only the controller answers approvals. On the socket, allow-always becomes allow-once, and `/mode auto`, `!`, `/cd` and `provider.*` are refused.
- **PF-3 approvals:** a pending approval is re-sent when a controller reattaches. It is denied after 600 seconds with no controller.
- **PF-3 startup:** `nexus serve` refuses auto approval and unconfined commands.
- **SB-1:** three profiles, selected through `--sandbox [engine]:profile`. There is no new flag and no new managed-policy key. The doctor schema stays at version 2.
- **MT-2:** `dispatchToolCallImpl` keeps its name and file. The complexity baseline moves forward only once, after the last slice, and only with your acknowledgement.
- **PA-2:**
  - After each merge, it re-runs every verifier already integrated plus the operator's verifier, capped at 16 distinct checks.
  - A failing check is re-run on the pre-merge tree to decide whether the new unit caused it.
  - A unit that edits another integrated unit's test files is refused.
  - The operation schema goes to v5.
  - "Neither is integrated silently" means the first unit lands with a verified record and the second is refused with a decision naming both units.

**Main risks**
- `dispatchToolCallImpl` has no complexity headroom: any added branch, including in SB-1 slice 3, fails CI.
- Whether TypeScript 7 can emit the lib's type declarations is untested; the plan includes a hand-written fallback. `"private": true` might change the license notices output and break the byte-for-byte build check.
- Contention with Codex claims on the hot files.
- Allowing a socket client to reattach and answer a pending approval is new behaviour.
- PA-2 adds verifier runs on every merge, and those checks run unsandboxed on the host.
- `agent.ts` is at 4,967 lines, 33 under MT-1's 5,000-line limit.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS** at `d4117b8668715da096b10e107ce6293d546f75dc`. At the end of the review the PR was still open and the head had not moved. I found no blockers.

**Findings**

1. Non-blocking, confirmed by reading and running: `docs/evidence/v1.3.0/VR2.md` and the PR body say `acp_client_conformance` passed 2/2. On this host it fails 0/2 at head. Both the Zed and CodeCompanion replays time out waiting for the turn output, and the log shows "Inference request aborted by operator". It also fails 0/2 on `origin/release/v1.3.0` (bd0cd1c2b), so it comes from the host (no inference or network) and not from this PR. The adapter's `initialize` output in the failure log is correct: the extensions are listed, and `session/new` succeeds. The recorded fixtures were therefore not shown to replay here. Fix: word the evidence as host-conditional, or note that the test needs a working turn.
2. Non-blocking, by reading: a client can permanently deny verification of any directory for the life of the process.
   - It can pin a directory before it has a commit.
   - It can open 16 throwaway directories, after which new workspaces are refused.
   - A failed `session/load` also uses a pin slot (`workspaceBase`, `acp_stdio.ts:248-257`).
   - The only person affected is the client's own, since stdio serves one client. This is a limit to document, not a fix. `docs/ACP.md` states most of it but not the denial angle.
3. Non-blocking, by reading: the base is pinned per canonical directory, not per repository.
   - A bind mount, a `mv`, or a new linked worktree gives a new key and a fresh pin.
   - The same-user limit already covers this, since the client can clone the repository anyway.
   - A subdirectory session is not a bypass, because `core/verify.ts:860-863` refuses a worktree that is not the top of the Git tree.
4. Non-blocking: `receipt.workspace` is a fresh `realpathSync` at issue time (`verify_service.ts:363`). The result's `workspace` therefore comes from that receipt, not from `session.cwd`. If the directory is renamed mid-run, the field could differ from what was passed. This is very unlikely in practice.

**Claim 1, the pin**
- Canonical is `fs.realpathSync` through `canonicalCwd`. It handles symlinks, `..` and trailing slashes. Linux is case-sensitive. The map key is the same string later passed as `worktree`.
- `session/new` and `session/load` both go through `startSession`, so they use the same pin.
- Two `session/new` calls on one directory cannot race. `pinBase` is a synchronous `spawnSync` and the map get/set has no `await` between them.
- A failed pin is stored and reused, so the refusal sticks until restart. Existing pins are kept at the cap; nothing is evicted or re-pinned.
- `pinBase` runs `rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options HEAD^{commit}` through the hardened `git()` wrapper. That wrapper has pinned flags, a checkout check, a scrubbed environment and a timeout. `rev-parse` runs no repository-configured program.

**Claim 2, result fields**
- `base_pinned_commit` and `base_pinned_at` come from the same `session.verifyBase` object that is passed to the service.
- Refusals and service errors return a JSON-RPC error with no result, so no field can describe something that did not run.
- `docs/protocol/acp-adapter-v1..v3.schema.json` are byte-identical to the base branch. `gen_acp_schema.mjs --check` reports v4 current, and the version constant is 4.

**Claim 3, receipt separation**
- Only an explicit id reaches `verifyService.receipt(id, sessionId)`, and it is bound to the session.
- Verify no longer sets `latestReceipts`. I covered the three orderings and the forged-id case by reading the code and the tests. I did not run my own probes.

**Claims 4 and 5, interlocks and shutdown**
- The interlock code is as described in the PR.
- `shutdownGraceMs()` is computed once, when the signal arrives. The longer deadline applies only while a verify is pending. With none running, the original bound is used.
- I did not probe cancel, close or stopping during a verify.

**Mutations** (source copied aside and restored; the worktree was clean afterwards)

| Mutation | Test file that failed |
|---|---|
| Remove the verify-during-prompt check (`if (false)`) | `acp_verify_followups` failed 1 |
| Remove the pin reuse | `acp_verify_followups` failed 1 |
| Restore `latestReceipts.set` in verify, with the id gate removed | `acp_verify_followups` failed 1 |

Dropping the id gate alone leaves everything passing. That is an equivalent mutation, because verify no longer records its receipt, so the gate is redundant. Only the `latestReceipts.set` mutation is a real one.

**Claim 6, test timing**
Besides the wait going from 10 s to 45 s, one assertion changed. The "latest receipt includes the verification receipt" check became "no turn receipt, so -32602". The new assertions add `workspace` and the pinned fields. None is weakened.

**Claim 7, docs**
The new `_nexus/verify` section in `docs/ACP.md`, `docs/evidence/v1.3.0/VR2.md` and `docs/MCP_GUIDE.md` match the code, except the conformance count in finding 1.

<result>## PR #774: REQUEST CHANGES at 9e9366d8686ea37437ff26f47e9b67583c462c9a
The head had not moved when I finished, and the PR is still open.

**Blocking**
1. **Stale sidecar schema.** `docs/protocol/sidecar-v1.schema.json` is out of date. The new `TurnResult` fields make `protocol_schema` fail 1 of 8: "checked-in protocol schema equals a fresh generation". Running `node cli/scripts/gen_protocol_schema.mjs` adds 12 lines, and `--check` fails. I reverted the regeneration in my worktree. The fix is to regenerate it and record the version decision in `docs/protocol/VERSIONS.json`. I did not check that failure on the base; #775, on a nearby base, passes this test. (confirmed by running)

**Non-blocking**
- The admission mutation survived. I made `initialTaskTimeStopReason` return `undefined`, and all 6 tests still passed. The loop-top `Date.now() &gt; turnDeadline` check is a backstop, since the timer is not armed at a zero limit. That makes the admission check redundant, and the test at line 78 does not pin it. With a zero limit the backstop uses `&gt;` on the same millisecond, so a step could dispatch. Add a test with a stubbed or frozen clock. (confirmed by running)
- The reset mutation was caught. Setting `taskElapsedMs = 0` on begin fails the "can only be lowered on resume" test (5 pass, 1 fail). (confirmed by running)
- A backwards clock jump within a process exhausts the whole budget. `finishTaskElapsedBudget` sets elapsed to the maximum whenever now &lt; `taskActiveSinceMs`. This fails closed, but a small NTP step permanently kills a long objective. Consider a monotonic `process.hrtime` delta within one process, and wall clock only across processes. (by reading)
- Legacy objectives with no counter are charged `now − startedAt`. Any objective older than 3 days (the 4320 min default) is exhausted on its first resume. This is deliberate and conservative, but it should be called out in the docs. (by reading)
- Idle time between clean turns is not counted. Only running intervals and crash or kill downtime are counted. Any open interval is charged through downtime, so a kill followed by a resume a week later exhausts the budget. The code behaves this way, but I found no operator-facing doc stating it. (by reading)
- `--no-continuation` leaves `budget` undefined, so there is no task limit. This matches the #762 turn deadline. (by reading)
- Persisted elapsed time is written once at begin, as `taskActiveSinceMs`, which survives a kill. The reservation is saved before any dispatch, and a failed save blocks inference. (by reading plus a test)
- The deadline timer is cleared on the catch path and on the normal path. The normal-path clear moved after the compaction drain. A timer firing during that drain is neutralised by `shouldOverrideStopAfterDeadline`. The unified `min(turn, task remaining)` timer avoids two timers. (by reading)
- The limit is configured by `--max-task-minutes` or `NEXUS_MAX_TASK_MINUTES`. It sits in `PROJECT_ENV_KEYS`, so a project `.env` can set it, as with the submission budget. It can only lower the ceiling, because a resume keeps `min(saved, new)`. It defaults to 4320 and is capped at `MAX_CONTINUATION_MINUTES`. Invalid values throw in `assertValidContinuationBudget`. The docs touch only the plan progress note, and I did not check whether it names the project-`.env` behaviour. (by reading)

## PR #775: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS, but the head moved
I reviewed af6c50e046ba157d716313ebbe4ed8b62442a22a. The head is now 7945d57b46f4e2e4a27d9d43bc250cef6c60c914, so this verdict does not cover it. I did not review the new commit.

**Non-blocking**
- **Doc over-claims resume.** The doc says "Resume continues after the operator raises the cap". I found no operator path that raises `policy.maxTotalTokens` on a saved goal. `resume` only clears the pause, and the policy is fixed at creation. A resume would hit the cap again immediately. Either add the path or reword the doc. (by reading)
- **Cap checks are before dispatch only.** One unit can overshoot by an unbounded amount, and the doc does not say so. (by reading)
- **Failure to measure is silent.** `rolledUpTokens` swallows child-state load errors, and no store means no enforcement. That fails open. It is the same posture as the existing usage code, and the doc does state it. (by reading)
- **Dead code.** `pauseForTokenBudget` has an empty `BLOCKED` branch. Remove it. (by reading)
- **Test gaps.** The test sets `operation.policy` directly. Nothing covers the env or config → `team.plan` path, the no-cap case, or the resume-after-raise claim. (by reading)

**Answers**
- The cap counts rolled-up `stats.total` per goal across its sub-operations. Reported and estimated tokens are both included. The goal ends `PAUSED` with a visible reason, never `COMPLETE`.
- Models, tools and workspace content cannot raise it. `NEXUS_OPERATION_MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS` and the config.json key are operator-controlled. The env key is not in `PROJECT_ENV_KEYS`, so a project `.env` cannot set it.
- The cap is optional and the schema change is optional. Goals without it behave as before.

## Acceptance: "a hook's allow has no effect"

`hooks_gate_bypass` runs each gate twice, without hooks and with an operator hook that answers approve in every dialect plus an `updatedInput` aimed at `Makefile`. The two receipts must be equal.

- **Dispatcher gates:** headless approval, operator refusal, `VERIFICATION_CONFIG_PROTECTED`, CS-9 forbidden, managed forbid, managed `require_human`, delegation. The hook is never started for any of them.
- **Read-only mode:** the hook runs, is recorded as `ignored_allow`, and the denial is word-for-word the same.
- **`updatedInput`:** the approved file is written and `Makefile` is untouched.
- **"A changed project hook asks again":** `hooks_project_trust`.
- **"Scripts of that shape transfer":** `hooks_examples` feeds a Claude-Code-shaped payload from `docs/HOOKS.md` to the example script.

## Security properties (test, then the mutation that fails it)

- **Gate order:** making the authority stage skip after a hook allow fails the read-only test.
- **Fail closed:** letting a hook error continue, or an invalid operator file pass, fails `hooks_deny`.
- **`ask` denies:** covered in `hook_output_reduce`.
- **Chain:** an allow that ends the chain fails `hooks_deny`.
- **Project trust:** treating unapproved as active, skipping re-validation, dropping the read-only rule, keeping the approval after removal, and dropping the change time from the file stamp each fail `hooks_project_trust`.
- **Secrets:** passing the full environment fails `hook_runner`; removing redaction fails `hooks_post_tool`.
- **Process control:** removing the kill-by-pid of remembered descendants, the stdout cap, or the left-open-output failure each fail `hook_runner`.
- **Visibility:** dropping `denialReason`, the receipt's `hooks`, or the flight record fails `hooks_deny`.
- **CLI:** approval without a hash, a wrong hash, or an invalid file being approvable fails `hooks_cli`.

## Plan premise that does not hold

The plan says the spine is reached only after every gate approved. Read-only mode, authority grants and the workspace boundary are enforced by the spine's authority stage, which runs after `contextAdmission`. A hook is therefore started for a read-only write. It still cannot allow it, but a hook that denies there replaces the gate's reason with `HOOK_DENIED`. I kept the plan's attachment, tested the real behaviour and documented it.

## Deviations and choices

- Project hooks are read without owner or mode checks (hash trust), because a default umask-002 clone would otherwise never run.
- An unknown decision value denies (`HOOK_OUTPUT_INVALID`).
- A hook that exits while a child holds its output open is `HOOK_FAILED`.
- An approved file observed missing has its approval revoked.
- The `post-tool` result is redacted; this is my addition.
- Hooks are serialised per agent, because read-only calls are dispatched in parallel.
- There is no session-start prompt for unapproved project hooks, only a notice on the first tool call.
- The plan's in-process "allow maps to admit" mutation guard was done as manual mutations instead.
- `tryEvent` (used by `nexus hooks test`) landed in the slice 2 commit; the change-time stamp fix landed in slice 3.
- Tests were run with `TMPDIR` on `/dev/shm`, because fsync on the shared disk took seconds; the wiring tests also passed once on disk.
- The slice 1 `context_convergence` run was cut by my own timeout. It passed 57/57 at the slice 2 and slice 3 states.

## Left

The other seven events, HK-2 and HK-3. `docs/releases/v1.3.0-READINESS.md` still lists HK-1 as open. The branch is 8 commits behind `origin/release/v1.3.0`.

## Release note

- Two events only: `pre-tool` and `post-tool`.
- Hooks are unsandboxed.
- An invalid `~/.config/nexus/hooks.json` denies every tool call.
- Non-zero exits other than 2 deny, unlike Claude Code.
- The approval prompt comes before hooks.
- `serve-mcp` runs no hooks.
- Project hooks are not a control over the agent, which can edit the file and so stop them.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review PR #778 policy enforcement" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UPS** at `afb4ed64f62d4b597eb75286e3fb92209ad367fb`. The head did not move. The PR has since merged. I found no loosening bypass of the stated invariant, but there is one real gap: a name-only `mcp.allow` entry is not a trust boundary against a workspace file.

**Bypasses, ranked**
1. **Non-blocking, but should be documented or tightened.** A bare-name `mcp.allow` entry admits any definition with that name (`permitsMcpServer`, `core/managed_policy.ts:820-839`; `McpConnection.policyDescriptor`, `mcp.ts:129-135`).
   - Confirmed by running: with `allow=["files"]` and a trusted workspace, a `.mcp.json` defining `files` with an attacker `command` started and ran that program.
   - The same holds for a WebSocket, HTTP or SSE URL under an allowed name. `docs/MANAGED_POLICY.md` says "only a name entry admits" a WebSocket server, but it never says a name entry pins nothing and that the workspace can redefine the name.
   - Fix: say so in the docs, and recommend `mcp.disable_workspace=true` or `command:`/`url:` entries. Optionally reject a name-only match when `fromWorkspace` is true.
2. **Non-blocking.** A hard link to a workspace file, placed in the user config directory, counts as non-workspace under `disable_workspace` (`isWorkspaceFile`, `workspace_trust.ts`).
   - Confirmed by running: the server started.
   - A repository cannot create that link, and `lstat` link-count detection is not feasible. It is a documentation-level limit.
3. **Non-blocking.** `nexus policy check &lt;file&gt;` echoes parts of an arbitrary readable file in the parse error (`policy_cli.ts:159-169`).
   - Confirmed by running: the first token of line 1 appeared as an "invalid key", and the value in `allow = sk-...` appeared as an "unsupported value".
   - Unreadable files and `/dev/zero` give clean errors, and `--json` stayed valid JSON with exit 2.
   - Fix: stop quoting the offending token, or truncate or redact it.
4. **Doc gap.** The `sha256:` pin covers only the entry file, not the modules it imports. The hash-then-import race is documented.

**Covered and held**
- MCP name tricks: case, trailing space, Cyrillic look-alike and zero-width space were all refused (confirmed by running).
- MCP config symlinked into the workspace and a symlinked parent directory were both treated as workspace under `disable_workspace`. A workspace symlink pointing out of the workspace was also treated as workspace.
- With the workspace set to `$HOME`, the user config is labelled workspace. `docs/MANAGED_POLICY.md` documents this.
- `addServer` and `addConnection` refuse an unlisted server; nothing was spawned.
- ACP: `mcpServers` is validated and ignored (`acp/validate.ts:152`), confirmed by reading.

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES** at `ad138d2fcafac87bf09c1b848f1e47945a8b2077`. The head had not moved and the PR was still OPEN when I finished. The `permitsMcpServer` half is sound. The `withoutFileText` half does not do what its title and doc say.

**Blocking**
1. `cli/src/policy_cli.ts` (`withoutFileText`) — confirmed by running. The regex pairs quotes left to right, so on `expected '=' after the key '&lt;key&gt;'` it pairs the 2nd and 3rd quote. That cuts the fixed text (`after t…`) and leaves the real token unpaired, so it is echoed in full.
   - A policy file whose line 2 is `dbpassword_hunter2_abcdefghij` produces `line 2: expected '=' after t…'dbpassword_hunter2_abcdefghij'`.
   - A 5000-character line produces a 5031-character error, and the `--json` output carries the same string.
   - Any key-like line with no `=` therefore reads out whole. This is the main leak the helper exists to stop.
   - Fix: truncate at the source, where `managed_policy_toml.ts:284` builds the message (cap `${key}`). Or use a pattern aligned to the message: `/'([^']*)'/` over each quote pair in order, and test it against this message. Add a test that uses this message.
2. Not blocking on its own: a secret of only lowercase letters, dots and underscores that is quoted and 9+ characters long passes the `^[a-z][a-z_.]{0,63}$` exemption in full. Documenting this is acceptable. The "unknown key" case echoes it as well, by design.

**Checked and fine**
- `fromWorkspace` is correct on every path that reaches `permitsMcpServer`. Only `loadAndConnect` computes it, with `isWorkspaceFile(file)`, which also catches symlinks into the workspace.
- Configs are not merged: each name's whole entry comes from the first file that defines it, and `!connections.has(name)` stops later files adding to it. So there is no mixed name/command entry.
- A workspace entry that is refused falls through to the user's same-named entry, which is fine.
- `addServer` and `addConnection` pass `false`. Their only callers are the operator's `/mcp add` and the REPL wizard, which persist to the user's own file. I read no route by which model text reaches them. I did not check whether the model can invoke slash commands.
- I found no plugin or include route for MCP servers.
- A bare command such as `npx` gives a workspace server no `command` in its descriptor. A name entry no longer matches it, so it is refused with the new message. I did not run this case. The docs say the same.
- A workspace set to `$HOME` marks user files as workspace files. That fails closed.
- The doc paragraph matches the code. The extra "What an entry does not pin" bullets are unrelated to this change.

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES** at `034c278cad62e7e849c4b68037d2ef32b2801af5`. The PR was still open and the head had not moved when I finished. I covered sections 1–4, 6, 7 and 8, and part of 5 and 9.

The hook output handling and gate ordering hold up. The blockers are all in trust and configuration: the model can get hooks approved, written or rewritten without the operator.

**Blocking**

1. **The model can approve its own project hooks.** (confirmed by running)
   - `hooks_cli.ts:~118` accepts `approve --sha256 &lt;hex&gt;` with no terminal. The store `extension_trust.json` and `hooks.json` are not in `DEFAULT_PROTECTED_PATHS` at `security.ts:163`. `policy_trust.json` and `trusted_workspaces.json` are, and GV-1 has a test for that.
   - My probe, in auto mode with and without `fullAccess`: `write_file .nexus/hooks.json`, then `run_command "nexus hooks approve --sha256 &lt;hash&gt; -w &lt;repo&gt;"`. The command exited 0, the store was written, and the hook ran on the next call.
   - The default `run_command` here ran unsandboxed: it could see `~/.config/nexus`. `--sandbox` is opt-in, and with it the home directory is masked. In review mode the operator would see the command.
   - Fix: require a TTY for approve and drop the headless `--sha256` path. Also add `extension_trust.json` and `hooks.json` to the protected paths, with a test like the GV-1 one. `isPathProtected` returns false for both today.

2. **With the workspace set to `$HOME`, the model can write an active operator hooks file.** (confirmed by running)
   - `write_file .config/nexus/hooks.json` completed, and with umask 022 the file is mode 644. `forbiddenMode: 0o022` passes it, so the "operator" hook ran with no approval.
   - With umask 002 (mode 664) the file is rejected, so every call is denied. That is a denial-of-service instead of code execution.
   - Fix: protect the path as in 1. Optionally require mode 600 for the operator file.

3. **Approval does not pin the script a hook names.** (confirmed by running)
   - I approved `command: ["./guard.sh"]`, then the model overwrote `guard.sh` with `write_file overwrite:true`. The next call ran the model's script with the operator's authority, outside the sandbox, with no re-approval.
   - `docs/HOOKS.md` never says this. It says the approval is for "the file's exact bytes".
   - Fix: state it plainly in the docs and in the `approve` output. Better, have `approve` refuse commands that point inside the workspace, or hash the resolved script.

**Non-blocking**

- `hook_runner.ts:~181`: a denial reason is passed to the model as `[hook X (project)] &lt;up to 1000 chars&gt;`. It is a single line and carries the hook name and source, but it is not marked as untrusted text. `IGNORE PREVIOUS INSTRUCTIONS…` passes through. Wrap it as quoted, hook-supplied text. Plain-text stdout and the `hooks[]` receipt summaries do not reach the model: `receipt.hooks` has no readers.
- A running hook is not stopped by cancel or the turn deadline (`hooks.ts` has no abort wiring). The docs say so. Hooks are serialised per agent, and queue wait is not counted in the 120 s budget.
- No test covers subagents or operation units, although `HOOKS.md` claims they are covered. By reading, subagents reuse the same `dispatchToolCall` and spine. Operation units get their own agent on a different workspace path, so project hooks silently do not run there. The docs mention worktrees but not units.
- The hooks stage also gates the operator's own `!cmd` shell command through the same spine. The docs do not mention it.
- Section 2 (by reading): the P10 budget is charged in `runSubagent` before dispatch, so a hook denial still counts and hooks cannot make calls free. For dispatcher-stage gates the hook is never started, so those receipts are identical. For spine-stage gates (read-only, workspace boundary, grants), a hook that denies replaces the gate's reason with `HOOK_DENIED`. The sandbox-denial events and anything else produced in the authority and execution stages are lost when a hook denies. I did not run each gate with an allow hook against the baseline, but `hooks_gate_bypass` covers it (11/11).

**Section 1, by running.** I fed 19 output shapes to `reduceHookOutput`, including duplicate keys, a BOM, `continue` as a string, null decisions, an array decision, a deny mixed with an allow, exit codes 1, 3 and 126, and stderr-only. None admitted anything. Every unreadable or unknown shape denied. Plain text and `[…]` are "no objection", as documented. A post-tool hook only reads the receipt. `verify_service` is a verbatim move into `process_identity.ts`.

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES** at `a299f8093a827450b3a4a4d642a557e94a2bcd5b`. At the end of the review the PR was still OPEN, with the same head and not merged. The library itself is sound. The one blocker is the verifier approval in the example and docs.

**Exports** (matching `docs/protocol/LIB_API_VERSIONS.json`):
- Runtime: `NEXUS_LIB_API_VERSION`, `spawnSidecar`, `SidecarClient`, `SidecarClientError`.
- Types: `ApprovalDecision`, `ApprovalHandler`, `ApprovalRequest`, `PromptOptions`, `SidecarChild`, `SidecarClientErrorCode`, `SidecarExit`, `SpawnSidecarOptions`, `ErrorCode`, `SidecarCommand`, `SidecarEvent`, `EngineeringOutcome`, `ExecutionState`, `TurnResult`.
- None of these runs a turn in-process, skips managed policy or the workspace-trust check, selects auto, or grants allow-always.

**Findings**

1. **Blocking, by running and reading.** The example approves any request whose tool is `"verifier"`.
   - Sites: `examples/embed/embed.mjs:34-38`, `docs/EMBEDDING.md` snippet, `examples/embed/README.md`. Commit a299f8 added it. The plan said the example approves only `run_command`, `write_file` and `edit_file`.
   - A verifier approval request is raised for the configured authoritative verifier. I observed `review:{command:"node --test",detail:"configured"}`.
   - The same tool name is used for model-declared requirement verifiers (`completion_oracle.ts:274-275`), discovered project verifiers and the type-check oracle (`completion_oracle.ts:126`). The example approves all of them without looking at the command or the origin.
   - A library default is not at fault, because the library denies by default. But a copied example lets a project-supplied or model-declared verifier run with no human grant. The CLI asks the operator for these, and auto-approves them only with `-y`.
   - Fix: approve only when `request.tool==="verifier" &amp;&amp; request.review?.command === "node --test" &amp;&amp; request.review?.detail === "configured"`, and deny the rest. Apply the same change to the docs snippet. Add a test where a model-declared verifier request is denied.

2. **Non-blocking, by running.** `extraArgs: ["-y"]` and `["--mode","auto"]` reach auto approval. Both runs ended VERIFIED with zero approval requests. The sidecar does not refuse them. This is documented in `docs/EMBEDDING.md` and was accepted in plan line 310. A test that pins it would help. Also consider rejecting `-y`, `--yes` and `--mode auto` in `extraArgs`, or refusing them in the sidecar as `nexus acp` does.

3. **Non-blocking, by reading.** There is no timeout on `onApproval` (`sidecar_client.ts:315`). A hung handler holds the turn until the abort signal fires. Add an optional `approvalTimeoutMs` that denies.

4. **Non-blocking, by reading.** There is a test gap. The no-handler default (`sidecar_client.ts:313`) was only caught once I mutated the real decision path (see mutation M1b below). The first mutation was an equivalent mutant.

5. **Non-blocking, by reading.** `tsconfig.lib.json` emits about 60 `.d.ts` files into `dist/types`, pulled in transitively. `package-linux.sh:64` copies the whole `dist` into the tarball, so the tarball grows. The `.d.ts` output is deterministic, so the two-build byte comparison should still hold. I did not run the package build.

**Question 1: import side effects.** `lib.ts` imports `sidecar_client.ts`, which imports only `acp/jsonrpc.ts` and `acp/types.ts` (type imports otherwise). Tracing the graph with a resolve hook shows nothing from `node_modules` and nothing from `index.ts` (`lib_entry.test.ts:98-121`). I imported `dist/lib.js` with a nonexistent HOME: exit 0, empty output, nothing created.

**Question 2: spawn.** The client runs `process.execPath` on `sidecar.js` (or `.ts` via tsx) with `-w` and the optional flags. The environment is the embedder's whole `process.env` by default, which is what the CLI does. The sidecar loads managed policy through `buildConfig` → `managedPolicy()`. It does not call `requireManagedPolicyAtStartup`. A bad policy therefore ends in a start-up failure with exit 1 rather than exit 2, which is non-blocking.

**Question 3: approvals.** I exercised the client with a fake child:
- With no handler, `{approve:true}`, `{always:true}`, `true`, `"Approve"` and `"approve-always"` all produce `deny`.
- Only `"approve"` produces `approve`, always with `always:false`. A promise resolving to `"approve"` also works.
- Concurrent requests are answered separately.
- A stale approval id gets `NO_PENDING_APPROVAL` from the sidecar, which the client ignores.
- `checkpoint.choice.request` cancels the turn.

**Question 4: robustness.** Lines are bounded at 16 MiB by `BoundedLineReader`. A wrong protocol version is dropped. There is a hello timeout with a SIGKILL to the child's own pid, and `shutdown` goes SIGTERM then SIGKILL on the pid only, with no process group. `leak_guard` passes.

**Question 5: packaging.** `package.json` adds `private`, `main`, `types`, `exports` and `prepublishOnly`, changes the `build` script, and keeps `bin` and the `test` scripts (`release_workflow_parity` passes). There is no lockfile change and no new dependency. `npm run build` succeeds. `dist/lib.js` and `dist/types/lib.d.ts` are produced. The smoke-package addition imports the packaged `lib.js` with no output and checks that the `.d.ts` and `sidecar.js` exist.

**Question 6: acceptance.** The example spawns the real `dist/sidecar.js` against the scripted gateway. The configured verifier `node --test` is approved through the `"verifier"` request, which is the finding 1 path. The result is VERIFIED. The tests assert exactly `outcome: VERIFIED\n`. A false completion claim is not VERIFIED, and a denied verifier gave UNVERIFIED in my run.

<result>All three blockers are fixed and committed locally as `3f769c90b` (not pushed); each has a test that fails without the fix. One limit remains: the terminal check does not stop an unsandboxed shell command.

## Blocker 1: self-approval
- **Protected paths** (`cli/src/security.ts:170,172`): `extension_trust.json` and `nexus/hooks.json`. I used `nexus/hooks.json`, not the bare name, so a project's `.nexus/hooks.json` and unrelated `hooks.json` files stay writable.
- **Terminal-only approval** (`cli/src/hooks_cli.ts:146`): nothing is approved unless stdin and stdout are both a TTY. `--sha256` only refuses a mismatch; the question is still asked. `revoke` stays non-interactive. The seam is the injected `interactive` predicate, with no environment variable.
- **Tests:** `hooks_self_approval` "protected paths…" and "…approves it by run_command does not end with an active hook" (real CLI through `run_command`); `hooks_cli` "approve is refused without a terminal…" and "on a terminal --sha256 says which content is meant…"; two assertions added to `managed_policy_enforcement`.
- **Mutations:** removing either protected name, or the `interactive()` check, fails these tests.

**What the TTY guard cannot stop:** `nexus trust` has no TTY guard and there is no agent marker on child processes, so nothing existed to reuse. An unsandboxed command in auto mode can fake a terminal with `script` or write the store directly. This is stated in `docs/HOOKS.md`.

## Blocker 2: workspace = HOME
- **Fix** (`cli/src/core/hooks.ts:403`, applied at `:548`): an operator hooks file inside the workspace is invalid, so every call is denied with "Start Nexus in a project directory". The owner and mode check was already there.
- **Test:** "with the home directory as the workspace…". The file tools are refused, a planted file is not run, and the same file is accepted from a project directory.
- **Mutation:** accepting the inside-workspace file fails it.

## Blocker 3: script pinning
- **Fix** (`cli/src/core/hooks.ts:311`, `:382`): the approval records the SHA-256 of the program and of every argument that is a workspace file, re-checked before each run. A change makes the hooks inactive and the notice names the file.
- **Refusals:** a relative path leaving the workspace and a symlink out of it are refused.
- **My choice:** project hooks must use array commands; a shell string cannot be pinned, so it is refused.
- **Trusted as installed:** programs outside the workspace (documented).
- **Shown in:** `nexus hooks list` and `approve`.
- **Tests:** "an approval covers the script the hooks run…" (overwritten with the agent's `write_file`); `hooks_cli` "a script the approved hooks run is covered…" and the unpinnable cases.
- **Mutations:** skipping the file comparison, allowing shell strings, or allowing a link out each fail them.

## Schema
`ExtensionTrustEntry` gains optional `files`. The version stays 1, the schema is regenerated, a second ledger entry is added and `--check` passes. Older approvals of hooks that run workspace files ask again.

## Smaller items (all done)
- **Denial framing** (`hooks.ts:640`): a fixed sentence plus the reason as one JSON string, capped; the "IGNORE PREVIOUS INSTRUCTIONS" test passes.
- **Docs:** the four corrections are in `docs/HOOKS.md`.
- **Subagent test:** added and passing.

- **TOCTOU (read).** `resolve()` hashes the files once per call, and the spawn comes later. The window includes queue time behind another hook (`this.last`, up to its timeout). The script is re-read by the interpreter, not pinned by file descriptor. Reaching it needs a concurrent process, so only an unsandboxed shell can use it. Non-blocking.
