<result>The four reviewer attacks are closed: none of them runs replaced content any more, and none can be approved. Committed locally as `a69316624` (not pushed).

## The rule (`cli/src/core/hooks.ts:333`, `coveredProjectFiles`)

A project hook names a program and files; inline code is not accepted. Every argument, and the value of `--opt=value` and `-ovalue` (`:426`), goes through `reference()` (`:359`):

- **Regular file in the workspace:** pinned (hashed, shown in `list` and `approve`).
- **Nothing by that name, or outside the workspace:** accepted.
- **Directory, dangling link, anything else in the workspace:** refused; the file is invalid and not run.

For known shells and interpreters (`:305`, version suffixes included):

- **Inline code** (`:421`): any single-dash option containing `c e i m p r`, plus `--eval`, `--print`, `--command`, and `eval` for deno and bun, is refused.
- **Loaders** (`:414`): `-r`, `--require`, `--import`, `--loader` need a pinned file as their value, so `--require=./pre.js` is accepted and pinned.
- **Wrappers** (`:407`): `env` and `busybox` as the program are refused.

The single-dash rule is deliberately broad, so it also refuses harmless forms such as `bash -euo pipefail ./guard.sh`. Operator hooks are unaffected. The check re-runs before every call.

## Tests (`hooks_cli` 13, `hooks_self_approval` 10, each 3 runs in a row)

- **Refused forms:** 24 commands cannot be approved and list as `invalid`.
- **Attacks end to end:** the four reviewer forms, with an old-style approval on record and the file overwritten by the agent's `write_file`: no hook runs and the notice says why.
- **`--require=./pre.js`:** pinned, overwritten, hooks stop and name `pre.js`.
- **Ordinary forms still work:** `["./guard.sh"]`, `["bash","./guard.sh"]`, `["node","guard.js"]`, `["node","scripts/guard.js","--flag"]`, `["/usr/bin/true"]`, and `hooks_examples`.
- **Terminal check:** now `isInteractiveTerminal` (`hooks_cli.ts:99`), tested with a terminal on stdin only and on stdout only.
- **"Approved record names a file no longer reached":** kept and tested. It protects the case where a removed workspace program lets `PATH` fall through to a different one.
- **Hash cache:** added, keyed by device, inode, size, mtime and ctime.

## Mutations

Each of these fails a test: accepting directories, inline options, unpinned loader values, or `env`; dropping the `=value` or `-ovalue` check; dropping either TTY side; dropping the removed-file comparison; a cache that ignores content identity.

## Gates

All hook test files, `leak_guard`, `security`, `managed_policy_enforcement`, `tsc`, the complexity gate, `check_invariants`, `check_doc_versions` and the hooks schema `--check` pass. No schema change.

## Not covered (stated in `docs/HOOKS.md`)

- The interpreter list is a refusal list; other programs that take code or module names (`awk`, `make`, `sqlite3`) are not recognised.
- Files a pinned script itself loads, and anything a program finds by its own rules (`node_modules`, config in the working directory), are not pinned.
- The window between the hash check and the program reading the file is not closed.
- The unsandboxed fake-terminal and direct-store-write limit from the previous round still stands.</result>

**Design decisions beyond the plan**
- The flag is `--listen` on the existing sidecar, not a new `nexus serve` command.
- Stdio remains the owner of the process. Socket clients are additional, and EOF on stdin still shuts the sidecar down.
- `attach`, `attached` and `listening` are not in the `SidecarCommand`/`SidecarEvent` unions. The Rust parity test requires a hand-written Rust variant for each union member, so putting them there would force a Rust change. They are documented as transport lines instead, and only the error codes are in the schema.
- `approve` with `always` is refused with `NOT_PERMITTED` rather than downgraded to once, and the approval stays open.
- Every `slash` command is refused, as are `shutdown`, `model.select`, `models.list` and `provider.*`.
- Per-connection `initialize` is answered by the hub, so `sidecar.ts` has no `negotiate()` change.
- The 600-second approval timeout is dropped, because the stdio owner always exists.
- A stale-socket sweep runs at start and removes only dead `s-*.sock` sockets owned by this uid.

**What the socket cannot protect against**
- A same-uid process, or unconfined model-driven code, that can read the 0600 descriptor can attach.
- Root can always attach.
- Node cannot read `SO_PEERCRED`, so the 0700 directory is the boundary.
- libuv unlinks the bound path when the server closes, even if someone replaced the file there.

**Open**
- The bwrap mask of the socket directory is not done, and the claim in `PROTOCOL.md` that `/run/user/&lt;uid&gt;` is hidden from sandboxed commands is not yet verified against `sandbox.ts`.
- Slow-observer drop has no test.
- The other-uid directory check can only be tested with a stand-in uid, since I can't chown without root.</result>

<result>**Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES at 512d0f9d20774067d540a49e56679e5fc60ac40c.** One blocker (sandbox containment). The PR head has since moved to aa365cf3e, a one-line test change in `cli/test/protocol_doc.test.ts`. The PR is still OPEN. I did not review or run aa365cf3e.

**Blocker (run)**
- `cli/src/sandbox.ts:805` and docs/PROTOCOL.md:72. A model command can reach the socket and read the token in these cases:
  - **No sandbox, which is the default.** `config.sandbox` is undefined unless `--sandbox` is passed. A command run this way read the descriptor and attached as controller. A controller can answer approvals and prompt, so an approved command could then approve the later approvals for its own turn.
  - **`--sandbox` (bwrap) with the default directory.** The general branch masks `/run/user/&lt;uid&gt;` (a tmpfs, only if that path exists) and `/tmp` (a fresh tmpfs). With `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/&lt;uid&gt;` or unset (the `/tmp/nexus-sockets-&lt;uid&gt;` fallback), the sandboxed command saw no descriptor. The read-only host branch uses `--tmpfs /`, so it hides the directory too (read only, not run).
  - **`--sandbox` with `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` anywhere else.** A path under `/var/tmp` or inside the workspace let the sandboxed command read the token and attach. The doc's claim is true only for the two standard locations.
  - **Fix:** pass the real socket directory into the sandbox args as a `--tmpfs` mask, whatever it is. State the unsandboxed limit plainly in the doc.
- In review mode the model cannot reach the descriptor on its own. `read_file` is jailed to the workspace, and `cat &lt;descriptor&gt;` through `run_command` needed approval and showed the path.

**Suggestions (non-blocking)**
- `session_socket.ts:64`: the controller may send `checkpoint.choice` with `allow_sensitive` and `answer` with `approve`. These are one-shot, like approve-once, but the PR text doesn't list them. Document them or drop them from the socket.
- `sidecar.ts:1663` (read): `--listen` refuses auto only at startup. If the stdio parent later approves with `always` or runs `/mode auto`, a socket controller's prompts run ungated. Refuse socket prompts while the mode is auto.
- `session_socket.ts:159`: eight idle unauthenticated connections block a legitimate attach for up to 5 s. Only same-uid processes can connect, so this is low.
- A stale-sweep orphan `.json` stays when libuv has already unlinked the socket. It is harmless.

**Verified clean (run unless marked read)**
- **Token:** wrong, empty, short, long, prefix, uppercase, trailing newline, number, array, null and object tokens all got the identical `UNAUTHORIZED` refusal. A command first, a command in the same packet as the attach, and a wrong token followed by a prompt started no turns. A right token after failures worked. Timing was flat (about 0.5 ms for every case). `timingSafeEqual` is used (read). The token never appeared in stdout, stderr, stdio events, observer output, or files under HOME and the workspace.

<result>BG-2 is done in `/home/mike/nexus-wt/bg2` on `v1/bg2-schedules`, as three local commits (not pushed, no PR). Every new test file passed three runs in a row, and the BG-1 files, `fault_kill_mid_tool_call`, `examples`, `protocol_schema`, `leak_guard`, `run_json_contract` and `security` passed once. `tsc`, the complexity gate (the five baseline functions did not grow), `check_invariants` and `check_doc_versions` are clean. No runner or worker processes are left behind.

**Commits**
- `dc6792984`: schedule store, cron/every/once parser and due-time computation, plus the protected path.
- `54253348e`: runner integration, `nexus schedule add|list|status|remove|pause|resume|run-now` (`-j/--json`), and the shared `core/job_submission.ts`. `index.ts` gets one import and one register line.
- `9f4cf34ea`: acceptance, hostile and G-4 tests, schedule schema and ledger, `docs/SCHEDULES.md`, `examples/schedules`, manual, CHANGELOG and STATE_COMPATIBILITY.

**Policies**
- **Time:** UTC only, so no daylight-saving shift; a leap day is real; no leap seconds. `every` keeps its phase from creation.
- **Missed runs:** all are counted and reported as one gap, never replayed. The default is one catch-up job (`run-once`); `--on-missed report` runs none. The newest slot counts as on time if it is within 30 seconds.
- **Overlap:** a slot due while the schedule's previous job runs starts nothing and is recorded as an `overlap` gap.
- **Clock stepped back:** if the clock is more than 2 minutes behind where the runner last saw it, the step is counted and the next slot recomputed. A slot that already has a job is never started again.
- **Caps:** 64 schedules, the last 50 gaps and 20 missed receipts kept, 200,000 slots per scan, and the runner's existing queue of 32.
- **Trust:** `add` and every fire need a workspace in the operator's trust list.

**Tests (pass counts)**
| File | Tests |
|---|---|
| `schedule` | 14 |
| `schedule_store` | 10 |
| `schedule_schema` | 2 |
| `schedule_engine` | 13 |
| `schedule_hostile` | 7 |
| `schedule_cli` | 6 |
| `schedule_failing_verifier`, `schedule_approval_denied`, `schedule_missed`, `schedule_examples` | 1 each |
| `schedule_unattended` | 2 |

**Mutations.** Each of 18 mutations made its tests fail: protected path removed, unknown keys allowed, policy enum unchecked, file mode, symlink and owner checks, directory check, schedule cap, trust list, overlap check, burst advance, clock-step handling, slot dedupe, cron range and field count, workspace link check, missed info dropped from the receipt, and `UnattendedApprovalPolicy` letting exec and egress through. The last one is the allow-always guard. The mutation runner is `/dev/shm/bg2mut/run.py`; sources were restored from copies.

**What a schedule cannot protect against.** Another program running as your user, including an agent's `run_command` with full access, can still write a record into the schedule directory. The file tools refuse it, but shell commands are only advisory-checked. A planted record is still bounded: it can only name one of the two job levels, the workspace must be in your trust list at each run, and the unattended rules still apply. The host clock is trusted; a wrong clock is reported, not fixed.

**Schema and ledger**
- The receipt's `missed` (reserved as always null) is now null or `{count, first, last, truncated?}`, and there is a new optional `schedule_occurrence`.
- I appended entries to the job-receipt and job-status ledgers and kept both at version 1, because nothing shipped carried the old shape.
- The new `schedule-v1.schema.json` and `SCHEDULE_VERSIONS.json` cover the schedule record, its `latest` pointer and the missed-run receipt.

**BG-1 observations (not fixed here)**
1. An unattended job's `--verifier` goes through the approval policy for exec, and command rules do not apply to it. A verifier like `node --test` is never run, so `task_completed` always fails. The job ends BLOCKED through stagnation with reason `EXECUTION_FAILED`, and the receipt never names the verifier, so the "seeded failing verifier" case is BLOCKED but not for the verifier's own failure. `git status --short` does run.
2. As far as I could see, the file-tool protected paths do not cover `runner/inbox` or `runner/jobs`; I only protected the schedule files.

**Non-blocking suggestions**
- **`schedule.ts`:** cron `* * * * *` is accepted, so it fires every minute. `every` has a 5-minute floor. Reject crons whose next two slots are under 5 minutes apart. Overlap, the 32-job queue and budgets bound the flood.
- **`schedule.ts` `scanSlots`:** a 200k-slot cron scan blocks the runner tick for about 2.6 s, and `fireDue` scans two or three times. Use a closed form or a lower cap.
- **`schedule_cli.ts` `run-now`:** it checks for a running job without the schedule lock. A race with the runner could give two concurrent jobs of one schedule. Not reproduced.
- **`security.ts`:** `inbox` and `jobs` are not protected paths (`isPathProtected` returned open). BG-1 relies on workspace confinement and denial of unapproved commands, which I did not exercise. Add an absolute-path pattern for the runner root.
- **Schedule tampering:** after B1, a tampered `verifier` field becomes an unapproved sandboxed command. It is the same trust boundary the docs state; add a sentence.

1. **Blocking until the moved head is green.** `cli/test/job_receipt_schema.test.ts` failed 2 of 6 at `c5182bbc7`: the old receipt shape and summary no longer match (read and ran). The new commit `1c840a53e` addresses it.

3. **Non-blocking, ran it.** `cli/src/core/schedule.ts:113-119`: the cron floor skips the midnight wrap when day-of-month or day-of-week is restricted. `59,0 23,0 1,2 * *` is accepted and fires 23:59 on day 1 and 00:00 on day 2, one minute apart. Fix: compute the wrap per allowed day pair.
4. **Non-blocking, read.** `submissionProblem` in `job_runner.ts:99` does not call `verifierProblem`, so a hand-written inbox request skips the checks. This stays within the own-account boundary the docs state. Fix: call it at runner accept.
5. **Non-blocking, ran it.** `0 0 31 2 *` is accepted and never fires (`nextAfter` returns undefined). Fix: reject it at `add`.

**Other results**
- The cron floor rejects `* * * * *`, `*/2`, and `0,3`. It accepts `*/5` and hourly.
- `MAX_SLOT_SCAN` only caps counting of missed slots. A leap-day schedule computes its next slot (2028-02-29), a yearly one computes, and a once-job in 2031 is fine. A year of `*/5` is truncated at 20,000 and takes about 289 ms.
- `runnerRoot()` protection held for these paths: an absolute inbox or jobs path, a symlink, a relative path from a workspace that is HOME, `..`, uppercase, and a double slash. A sibling directory sharing the prefix is not protected.
